| 3 | *Alladhīna yattabiʿūna'l-shahawāt* | *An tamīlū maylan ʿaẓīmā* — **that you swerve** |
| 4 | God | *An yukhaffifa ʿankum* — **to lighten** |

**And the structure itself carries information, which I record as my own reading:** after the rulings, what the will that lays them down wants is stated. **The verse, that is, explains the intention behind a list of prohibitions running to twenty-three items.**

### سُنَنَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِكُمْ — the word

**سُنَّة — root س-ن-ن: to do something evenly and smoothly; to open a way.** *Sanna'l-māʾa* — he made the water flow; *masnūn* — evenly shaped.

**The word is plural here: *sunan* — ways.** And the expression "the ways of those before you" states that the rulings have a continuity within history.

**The concept of *sunnat Allāh* was treated in `048-fetih.md`, and a record was entered there:** *"the concept is a measure for understanding the past, not a warrant for judging the present."* **I keep that record here too.**

### مَيْلًا عَظِيمًا — the absolute object

**The sentence reinforces the verb *an tamīlū* (that you swerve) with *maylan ʿaẓīmā* (a great swerving).**

**And the word's second occurrence in the sūra must be recorded:** the same root will return in the hundred and twenty-ninth verse — *fa-lā tamīlū kulla'l-mayl* ("do not incline wholly to one side").

| Verse | The inclining | Who does it |
|---|---|---|
| **27** | *maylan ʿaẓīmā* | Man's swerving from religion |
| **129** | *kulla'l-mayl* | Turning towards one side between spouses |

**The same root, at two different scales.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### وَخُلِقَ ٱلْإِنسَٰنُ ضَعِيفًا — the weight of the sentence

**"Man was created weak."**

**And where this sentence is spoken must be recorded: as the ground of the will to lighten.** Weakness, that is, is not named as a defect; it is named as **the reason for the lightening of the ruling**.

**The root ض-ع-ف had occurred in this sūra at the ninth verse (*dhurriyyatan ḍiʿāfan*) and at the seventy-fifth (*al-mustaḍʿafīn*).**

**Now the root returns in a third place and in its most general form: *khuliqa'l-insānu ḍaʿīfā*.**

| Verse | Who is weak | In what respect |
|---|---|---|
| **9** | The children left behind | **A situation** |
| **28** | **Man — in general** | **Creation** |
| **75, 98, 127** | Those made weak | **Something that is done** |

**Read together, the three rows show the three layers the sūra builds around weakness: a state, a nature, and a wrong done.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the places of the words can be counted in the text.

**And this is an important place for this sūra:** the sūra is built on the right of the one who has no protection, and in the twenty-eighth verse it is said that **being without protection belongs to everyone**. **Those addressed by the rulings, that is, are weak too.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The verses between the twenty-sixth and the twenty-eighth come immediately after a long list of prohibitions and set a frame around the list: **the aim is not to restrict but to make clear and to lighten.** That the intention is stated after a body of rules also determines how the rules are to be read.

**Two.** The sentence *khuliqa'l-insānu ḍaʿīfā* makes, in the middle of a text of obligations, one of the plainest admissions about human beings. **This sentence produces neither an excuse nor a humiliation.** It says that the measure of the burden is set according to the strength of the one who carries it.

**And a limit:** no conclusion such as "man is weak, and therefore not responsible" follows from here. The verse names weakness not so that responsibility should be lifted but so that it should be **lightened** — the verb itself is *yukhaffifa*, not *yarfaʿa*.

---


## 4/29-30 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا لا تأكلوا أموالكم بينكم بالباطل إلا أن تكون تجارة عن تراض منكم ولا تقتلوا أنفسكم إن الله كان بكم رحيما · ومن يفعل ذلك عدوانا وظلما فسوف نصليه نارا وكان ذلك على الله يسيرا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū lā taʾkulū amwālakum baynakum bi'l-bāṭili illā an takūna tijāratan ʿan tarāḍin minkum, wa lā taqtulū anfusakum, inna'llāha kāna bikum Raḥīmā · Wa man yafʿal dhālika ʿudwānan wa ẓulman fa-sawfa nuṣlīhi nārā, wa kāna dhālika ʿala'llāhi yasīrā*

"O you who believe! Do not consume one another's property among you by false means — unless it be a trade by mutual consent among you. And do not kill yourselves. God is merciful towards you. Whoever does this in enmity and wrongdoing, We shall cast him into a fire; and that is easy for God."

### The opening of the block and its place in the sūra

**The twenty-ninth verse opens a new block, and the opening address changes:** in the first verse it was *yā ayyuha'l-nās* (O mankind); here comes *yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū* (O you who believe).

**But the subject does not change.** For twenty-eight verses the sūra has been occupied with **property in someone else's hands**: the orphan's property (2-10), the shares of inheritance (11-12), the bridal gift (4, 20-21, 24-25). **The twenty-ninth verse builds the same subject again in its most general form:** *amwālakum baynakum* — "the property among you".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the repetition of the verb:** the verb *lā taʾkulū* (do not consume) occurs in the sūra at the second verse (*wa lā taʾkulū amwālahum*), at the sixth (*wa lā taʾkulūhā isrāfan*), at the tenth (*yaʾkulūna amwāla'l-yatāmā*), and here. **All four are about property, and all four use the same verb.**

### بِٱلْبَٰطِلِ — the root ب-ط-ل

**The root's original sense: to come to nothing, to be void, to be without effect.** *Baṭala* — it came to nothing; *bāṭil* — what has no ground, what has no counter-value.

**And the word's function here must be recorded: the verse does not say "by forbidden means", it says *bi'l-bāṭili*.** The measure, that is, is property changing hands **without a counter-value**: if what is given has nothing given back for it, that transaction is *bāṭil*.

**The same pattern occurs at `002-bakara.md` 2/188** (*wa lā taʾkulū amwālakum baynakum bi'l-bāṭil*), and there the giving of bribes to judges is named. **The two verses build the same sentence with two different examples.** This is a repetition that can be verified.

### إِلَّآ أَن تَكُونَ تِجَٰرَةً عَن تَرَاضٍ مِّنكُمْ — the form of the exception

**The verse lays down a prohibition and gives its exception with two qualifications.**

| Qualification | Word | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **The first** | *Tijāratan* | The change of hands must have a **counter-value** |
| **The second** | *ʿAn tarāḍin minkum* | The change of hands must rest on **mutual consent** |

***Tarāḍin* — root ر-ض-و, the *tafāʿul* form.** And this form in Arabic states **reciprocity**: *taqātala* (they fought one another), *taʿāwana* (they helped one another). **The word is therefore not "to consent" but "to consent to one another" — consent cannot be one-sided.**

**And the root comes for the third time in this sūra.** In the fourth verse there was *fa-in ṭibna lakum ʿan shayʾin minhu nafsan* (if of their own accord they leave you some part of the bridal gift); in the twenty-fourth *fīmā tarāḍaytum bihī min baʿdi'l-farīḍa* occurred and was treated there under the heading "the field of consent".

| Verse | The subject of the consent | Word |
|---|---|---|
| **4** | The return of part of the bridal gift | *Ṭibna … nafsan* |
| **24** | An arrangement above the appointed bridal gift | *Tarāḍaytum* |
| **29** | **Every kind of transfer of property** | *Tarāḍin* |

**The same measure is present in all three, and the third is the widest.** I record this as an observation about word order.

### وَلَا تَقْتُلُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَكُمْ — a disagreement

**The clause is short and its word carries several senses: *anfusakum* — "your souls".**

**There is disagreement in the classical commentaries over this expression, and it must not be concealed:**

| Reading | What *anfusakum* means | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Do not kill one another** | The Qurʾān's naming of a community as a single body; in the same sūra the records *baʿḍukum min baʿḍ* (25) and *min nafsin wāḥida* (1); the verse's coming immediately after the discussion of property — the subject is relations within the community |
| **B** | **Do not kill yourselves** | The word's first and direct sense; the pronoun referring to the second person plural |
| **C** | **Do not cast yourselves into ruin** | The verb's figurative extension; the parallel with *wa lā tulqū bi-aydīkum ila'l-tahluka* at `002-bakara.md` 2/195 |

**All three readings are transmitted; I impose no preference and draw no legal ruling from them.**

**But an indication that can be verified from the text may be recorded:** immediately before the clause stands the expression *amwālakum **baynakum*** ("the property among you"), and *baynakum* plainly states reciprocity. **This is an indication supporting reading A. I record it as an indication, not as a preference.**

**And this too must be recorded:** two clauses stand side by side in the same verse — **the taking of property unjustly** and **the taking of life**. The sūra forbids both in one breath.

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ بِكُمْ رَحِيمًا — the choice of verse-ending

**The verse lays down two prohibitions and closes with mercy.**

**The sūra's order of verse-endings was set out in a table in the introductory section:** the rulings mostly closed with an attribute of **watching** (*raqīb*, *ḥasīb*, *shahīd*, *samīʿ*, *baṣīr*, *khabīr*). **Here there is not watching but mercy.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is verses twenty-six to twenty-eight:** the block immediately before ended with *yurīdu'llāhu an yukhaffifa ʿankum* (He wishes to lighten your burden). **The verse-ending continues that line.**

### عُدْوَٰنًا وَظُلْمًا — the qualification in the thirtieth verse

**The thirtieth verse qualifies the breach of the prohibition in the twenty-ninth with two words: *ʿudwānan wa ẓulman*.**

**The root ع-د-و: to exceed the bound, to cross the limit. The root ظ-ل-م: to put a thing somewhere other than its place.** The two roots were compared in the discussion of `005-maide.md` 5/2 and 5/8: the one states **doing too much**, the other **not giving what is due**. **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the coming of the two words together produces a qualification:** the verse qualifies not every loss of property or every death but what is done **deliberately and wrongfully**. **This foreshadows the gradation that will be established in the ninety-second and ninety-third verses.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The qualification *ʿan tarāḍin minkum* ties the validity of a transaction to **the will of both parties**. That consent be mutual is not the same thing as one party's having signed; the pattern of the word says this.

**Two.** The measure *bi'l-bāṭil* asks a different question from "is it forbidden or not": **is there a counter-value?** This question can be asked even of transactions that look formally flawless.

---

## 4/31 — إن تجتنبوا كبائر ما تنهون عنه نكفر عنكم سيئاتكم وندخلكم مدخلا كريما

*In tajtanibū kabāʾira mā tunhawna ʿanhu nukaffir ʿankum sayyiʾātikum wa nudkhilkum mudkhalan karīmā*

"If you avoid the great things you are forbidden, We shall cover over your lesser failings and admit you to an honoured place."

### ٱجْتِنَاب — the root ج-ن-ب

**The concrete sense of the root: the side, the flank.** *Janb* — the side; *jānib* — the side or aspect of a thing. **And *ijtanaba* (Form VIII): to leave a thing to *one side* and walk on, to keep away from it.**

**The picture in the word is worth recording: to avoid a prohibition is to leave it aside and keep walking.** The verb describes not a struggle but **a distance**.

**The same root will return in this sūra at the thirty-sixth verse: *wa'l-ṣāḥibi bi'l-janb* — "the companion at your side".** **That one root should be used in two opposite directions, one of keeping away and one of nearness, is a fact that can be verified within the sūra.**

### كَبَآئِر — the great things

**The verse divides the prohibitions in two: *kabāʾir* (the great ones) and *sayyiʾāt* (the lesser failings).**

**And how the division is to be made is not stated in the verse.** In classical exegesis there is a wide debate over the list and the definition of the *kabāʾir*; **that list is not given in this commentary and no definition is preferred.** The reason is the method's rule against issuing legal rulings.

**What can be said from the text is this: the verse establishes a *proportion*.** Avoiding the great ones brings the covering over of the lesser ones. **The verse, that is, asks not for faultlessness but for a priority.**

**نُكَفِّرْ** — root ك-ف-ر: **to cover.** The root was treated in detail in `002-bakara.md` (the farmer covering the seed with earth). **I rely on that treatment.** And its use here in the direction of good is worth recording: **the act of covering can, from the same root, name both denial and forgiveness.**

**مُّدْخَلًا كَرِيمًا** — *mudkhal*: a place one enters. *Karīm* — root ك-ر-م: **precious, generous, noble.** **The verse does not use an attribute belonging to the place; it uses an attribute belonging to a relationship.**

---

## 4/32 — ولا تتمنوا ما فضل الله به بعضكم على بعض للرجال نصيب مما اكتسبوا وللنساء نصيب مما اكتسبن واسألوا الله من فضله إن الله كان بكل شيء عليما

*Wa lā tatamannaw mā faḍḍala'llāhu bihī baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍ, li'l-rijāli naṣībun mimma'ktasabū wa li'l-nisāʾi naṣībun mimma'ktasabna, wa'sʾalu'llāha min faḍlih, inna'llāha kāna bi-kulli shayʾin ʿAlīmā*

"Do not covet that by which God has favoured some of you over others. To the men belongs a share of what they have earned, and to the women belongs a share of what they have earned. Ask God of His bounty. God knows all things."

### This verse will be treated in detail — and the reason is in the text

**This verse uses the same words as the thirty-fourth, which will come two verses later.** And this is a datum of the text that is most often skipped in exegesis. **It must be established first, because the thirty-fourth verse comes on top of this one.**

### One: the same sentence returns two verses later

| Verse | Sentence | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **32** | *Mā **faḍḍala'llāhu bihī baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍ*** | Those addressed — so that they **not covet** |
| **34** | *Bimā **faḍḍala'llāhu baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ*** | As the **ground** of a ruling |

**In both sentences there is the same verb (*faḍḍala*), the same structure (*baʿḍ … ʿalā baʿḍ*) and the same indefiniteness.**

**And the indefiniteness itself is a datum of the text, which I record separately:** both verses say *baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍ* / *baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ* — **"some of you over others" / "some of them over others".** **In neither of the two verses does the expression "men over women" occur.** This is a countable fact: both the subject and the object of the sentence are indefinite pronouns.

**I record this as an observation about word order, not as an exegetical preference.** How this indefiniteness is read in the thirty-fourth verse will be given there, in a table of the disagreement.

### Two: نَصِيب — given to both sides

**The two clauses in the middle of the verse are exact copies of each other:**

| | **For the men** | **For the women** |
|---|---|---|
| **Preposition + noun** | *Li'l-rijāli* | *Li'l-nisāʾi* |
| **What is given** | *Naṣībun* | *Naṣībun* |
| **Its source** | *Mimma'ktasabū* | *Mimma'ktasabna* |

**In all three rows the word is the same; the only thing that changes is the person-ending of the verb.** *Iktasabū* — third person plural masculine; *iktasabna* — third person plural feminine. **The sentence, that is, builds the same word twice, changing only the inflection.**

**And this happens for the second time in the sūra.** The seventh verse did the same thing:

> *Li'l-rijāli **naṣībun** mimmā taraka'l-wālidāni wa'l-aqrabūn wa li'l-nisāʾi **naṣībun** mimmā taraka'l-wālidāni wa'l-aqrabūn* (4/7)

| Verse | Subject | The source of the share |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | **Inheritance** | *Mimmā taraka'l-wālidāni wa'l-aqrabūn* — from what is left |
| **32** | **Earnings** | *Mimma'ktasabū / mimma'ktasabna* — from **what is earned** |

**In both verses there is the same word (*naṣīb*), the same paired construction and the same symmetry.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### Three: the verb ٱكْتِسَاب used for both

**And now comes the most striking side of this verse.**

**The root كسب: to obtain something, to earn it; and the concrete association the lexicographers record is going after a task and getting it.**

**The classical lexicographers have a record about Form VIII (*iktasaba*):** it is said that between *kasaba* and *iktasaba* the second states **greater effort and intent**. **I relate this record as the lexicographers give it; there is no complete agreement on the distinction.**

**What belongs here is this, and it is a datum of the text: the verse uses the verb *iktasaba* for both parties.**

**The sentence could have been built like this, but it is not:**

| It could have been | It is |
|---|---|
| To the men a share of what they earned, to the women a share of what is **given** them | To the men a share of **what they earned**, to the women a share of **what they earned** |
| A single *naṣīb*, divided between two parties | **Two separate *naṣīb*s, each tied to a separate earning** |

**The verse, that is, establishes by repeating the word that both parties are in the position of *the one who earns*.** The verb is not passive but active; and active twice over.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the inflection of the verb:** *iktasabna* is the feminine form and it stands once in the sentence, exactly in the place of its masculine counterpart. **The text writes the woman's earning not as an extension of the man's earning but as an item in its own right.**

**And a limiting record:** no legal arrangement and no theory of property is drawn from here. **What is said is the sentence's own structure: the same verb and the same noun are given to both parties.**

### Four: the forbidding of coveting and what is given in its place

**The verse forbids one thing and puts something else in its place. The two must be set side by side:**

| | What is forbidden | What is put in its place |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Lā tatamannaw* — do not covet | *Wa'sʾalu'llāha* — ask of God |
| **Direction** | Towards what another has | **Towards the source** |
| **Subject** | *Mā faḍḍala'llāh* — what has been given | *Min faḍlih* — what is to be given |

**تَمَنِّي — root م-ن-ي: to let a thing pass through the heart, to picture it.** From the same root comes *amāniyy* (vain fancies), and this word will return in the hundred and nineteenth verse of the sūra.

**And the distinction must be recorded: what is forbidden is not asking but wanting *what another has*.** The second half of the sentence commands asking outright.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the presence of the two verbs in the same verse:** the verse closes off comparison and leaves asking open.

### What this says to the present

**One.** The verse describes comparison itself as a burden: a desire built by looking at what another has is named in the verse *tamannī* and is forbidden. What is put in its place is not the asking itself but **the direction of the asking**.

**Two.** That the word *naṣīb* is given twice, to both parties, continues the line established in the sūra's seventh verse. **The sūra counts the party who has a share twice — and each time the second counting is identical to the first.**

---

## 4/33 — ولكل جعلنا موالي مما ترك الوالدان والأقربون والذين عقدت أيمانكم فآتوهم نصيبهم إن الله كان على كل شيء شهيدا

*Wa li-kullin jaʿalnā mawāliya mimmā taraka'l-wālidāni wa'l-aqrabūn, wa'lladhīna ʿaqadat aymānukum fa-ātūhum naṣībahum, inna'llāha kāna ʿalā kulli shayʾin Shahīdā*

"For all of what parents and near kin leave We have appointed heirs. And to those whom your oaths have bound, give their share. God is witness over all things."

### مَوَٰلِي — the root و-ل-ي

**The concrete sense of the root: to be near, to be adjoining.** *Walī* — the one who is near, who takes charge of another's affair; *mawlā* — both master and freedman, both helper and the one bound. **Because of the *nearness* in its root, the word is used in both directions at once.**

**The root was treated at `008-enfal.md` 8/72-75 under the heading "the bond of *walāya*". I rely on that treatment.**

### نَصِيب — a third time

**The word occurs in this sūra twice in the seventh verse, twice in the thirty-second, and once here.**

| Verse | To whom | From what |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | To the men and to the women | From what is left |
| **32** | To the men and to the women | **From what is earned** |
| **33** | *Alladhīna ʿaqadat aymānukum* | From what is left |

**The same word five times in three verses.** And the command is the same every time: **give the share.**

### وَٱلَّذِينَ عَقَدَتْ أَيْمَٰنُكُمْ — a disagreement

**The expression means literally "those whom your oaths have bound", and there is disagreement in the classical commentaries over whom it means.**

| Reading | Who | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Those with whom a pact has been made** — those given a promise of mutual aid and a share in the estate | The sense of binding, of contract, in the word *ʿaqd*; the use of the plural *aymān* (oaths) |
| **B** | **Those adopted or attached** | The word's sense of a bond resting on an agreement |
| **C** | That the ruling was **later abrogated**, the verse regulating a transitional period | That the verses of inheritance (11-12) have determined the shares |

**All three readings are transmitted; I impose no preference and draw no legal ruling from this.** **The legal force of this verse is disputed in classical jurisprudence, and that debate is not the subject of this text.**

**What can be said from the text is this:** the verse builds a sentence that counts even a bond not made by blood as **holding a share**. **And this will widen further in the thirty-sixth verse — there the neighbour and the travelling companion will enter the list.**

---

## 4/34-35 — الرجال قوامون على النساء بما فضل الله بعضهم على بعض وبما أنفقوا من أموالهم … واللاتي تخافون نشوزهن فعظوهن واهجروهن في المضاجع واضربوهن فإن أطعنكم فلا تبغوا عليهن سبيلا · وإن خفتم شقاق بينهما فابعثوا حكما من أهله وحكما من أهلها إن يريدا إصلاحا يوفق الله بينهما

*Al-rijālu qawwāmūna ʿala'l-nisāʾi bimā faḍḍala'llāhu baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ wa bimā anfaqū min amwālihim, fa'l-ṣāliḥātu qānitātun ḥāfiẓātun li'l-ghaybi bimā ḥafiẓa'llāh, wa'llātī takhāfūna nushūzahunna fa-ʿiẓūhunna wa'hjurūhunna fi'l-maḍājiʿi wa'ḍribūhunna, fa-in aṭaʿnakum fa-lā tabghū ʿalayhinna sabīlā, inna'llāha kāna ʿAliyyan Kabīrā · Wa in khiftum shiqāqa baynihimā fa'bʿathū ḥakaman min ahlihī wa ḥakaman min ahlihā, in yurīdā iṣlāḥan yuwaffiqi'llāhu baynahumā, inna'llāha kāna ʿAlīman Khabīrā*

"Men are *qawwām* over women, because God has favoured some over others and because they spend of their property. Good women are *qānit*; they guard, where no one sees, what God has guarded. As for those women whose *nushūz* you fear, admonish them, leave them alone in their beds, and *ḍarb* them. If they obey you, then seek no way against them. God is exalted, great. — And if you fear a rift between the two of them, send an arbitrator from his family and an arbitrator from hers. If the two of them wish to set things right, God will bring them together. God is Knowing, Aware."

### First, a note on method — and this note holds for the whole block

**This is the most debated place in the sūra. So I set down at the outset what is done in this section and what is not.**

**One. The two verses are treated together.** The thirty-fourth verse is not a unit separable from the thirty-fifth: the first verse takes as its subject the settling of a dispute from within, the second the settling of the same dispute from outside by arbitration, and **the block ends with the word *iṣlāḥ*.** To cut the block in the middle is to damage the text.

**Two. No legal ruling is issued.** The readings of the classical commentaries and of the schools of law are related **as a table of the disagreement, with their grounds.** **None of them is preferred and none of them is binding.**

**Three. Current debates are not entered into.** How this verse is understood today, how it is answered in this or that legal order, and the social debates around it are not the subject of this text.

**Four. No demeaning language is used and the reader is not scolded.** No general judgement of worth about either party is drawn from the verse.

**Five. What is not in the wording of the verse is not added to it.** Below, the words and grounds **the verse itself gives** are established first; the readings come only after that.

### One: قَوَّٰم — the root ق-و-م

**The root ق-و-م: to rise to one's feet, to stand up, to keep a matter standing.**

**The root has occurred twice already in this sūra:** at the fifth verse, *allatī jaʿala'llāhu lakum **qiyāmā*** ("the property God has made a means of standing upright for you"), and it was treated there under the heading "the weave of roots in the sūra". **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the pattern must be recorded: *qawwām* is on the فَعَّال pattern.** In Arabic this pattern states two things:

| Function | Example | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **Frequency / continuity** | *Ghaffār* — the one who forgives much | One who does a thing continually |
| **Trade / occupation** | *Khayyāṭ* (tailor), *najjār* (carpenter), *khabbāz* (baker) | One who has taken that work upon himself |

**The same word was treated at `005-maide.md` 5/8, and this was recorded there:**

> *"That is, a *qawwām* is not 'one who acts justly on one occasion' but one who makes it his business to keep justice standing. The command asks not for a behaviour but for **a character**."*

**I rely on that treatment.** And setting the word's three occurrences in the Qurʾān side by side must be recorded as a datum of the text:

| Place | Expression | Over what / with what |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/34** | *Al-rijālu **qawwāmūna** ʿala'l-nisāʾ* | With the preposition *ʿalā*, the subject of an obligation |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/135** | *Kūnū **qawwāmīna** bi'l-qisṭ* | With justice |
| **al-Māʾida 5/8** | *Kūnū **qawwāmīna** li'llāh* | For God |

**The same pattern is present in all three. In two of them the word is a *command*; in the first it is a *statement of a situation*.** This is a countable fact.

**And this must be recorded: the verse does not explain the word *qawwām*.** How much authority and how much obligation the word states is not written in the verse; **what is written in the verse is two grounds.**

### Two: the two grounds the verse itself gives

**The particle *bimā* occurs twice in the sentence, and two grounds are set out:**

| Order | Ground | Its kind |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Bimā **faḍḍala'llāhu baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ*** | Something given |
| **2** | *Wa bimā **anfaqū min amwālihim*** | **Something done** — an act |

**The second ground must be treated in detail, because its wording is plain and not in dispute.**

***Anfaqū*** — a perfect verb, third person plural: **they spent.** ***Min amwālihim*** — **of their property.**

**The verse, that is, ties the second ground to an *act*.** And the act is in the same field as the verb *iktasabū / iktasabna* two verses earlier: property, earning, spending.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the tense of the verb:** *anfaqū* is past and states **an action**. **A ground that states an action is bound to that action.** The verse has thus tied the ground not to an attribute but to something done.

**That this explanation is found in the classical commentaries too must be recorded:** the connecting of *qawwām*-hood with the obligation of maintenance is widely transmitted. **But there is disagreement over the relative weight of the two grounds and over the scope of the first, and it is set out in the table below.**

### Three: بِمَا فَضَّلَ ٱللَّهُ بَعْضَهُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ — a table of the disagreement

**First the datum of the text: the sentence does not say "men over women". It says *baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ* — "some of them over others".**

**And two verses earlier, in the thirty-second, the same structure occurred** (*mā faḍḍala'llāhu bihī baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍ*) — **and there the sentence was the subject of a prohibition: do not covet this.** This is a datum of the text that can be verified, and it was set out in a table above.

**There is disagreement in the classical commentaries over how this expression is to be read. I put it in a table; I impose no preference.**

| Reading | What *baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ* states | Its ground | Its qualification |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | A particular advantage given to men | The context of the sentence; that the second ground (spending) is ascribed to the man | It has difficulty accounting for the indefiniteness of the expression *baʿḍ … ʿalā baʿḍ* |
| **B** | **Mutual advantages** — different capacities given to both parties | The indefiniteness of the pronoun; that the word *baʿḍ* is open to both parties; the general use of the same expression at 4/32 | It loosens the sentence's link with the ruling |
| **C** | Since the subject of the advantage is not specified, it cannot be confined to **any particular field**; the verse gives the ground for a division of duties | That the verse continues with maintenance; that the sentence is left unrestricted | It leaves the scope indefinite |

**All three readings have been defended in the classical and later literature. I prefer none of them and none of them is binding.**

**And a limiting record is written plainly, per the method (`USLUP.md`):** no conclusion that one sex is superior to the other **in respect of being human** can be drawn from this sentence. The sūra's first verse (*min nafsin wāḥida*) and its twenty-fifth (*baʿḍukum min baʿḍ*) were treated in this commentary, and the records made there hold here too.

### Four: قَٰنِتَٰت and حَٰفِظَٰت لِّلْغَيْبِ

**قَٰنِتَٰت — root ق-ن-ت: to keep at a task with constancy and reverence; to submit.**

**And the root's distribution in the Qurʾān must be recorded, because it shows the direction of the word:**

| Place | Who | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| `002-bakara.md` 2/238 | Those who pray | *Qūmū li'llāhi **qānitīn*** — to God |
| `033-ahzab.md` 33/35 | *Al-qānitīna wa'l-qānitāt* | Both parties are counted together |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/34** | *Al-qānitāt* | Not specified |

**Two tendencies are transmitted in the classical commentaries over towards whom the word is directed here:** some explain it as obedience to God, some as constancy to the order within the family. **I relate both tendencies; I impose no preference.**

**What can be said from the text is this, and it is an observation about word order:** in the Qurʾān the dominant use of the root is directed towards God; and at `033-ahzab.md` 33/35 the word is counted **for both parties**. **This establishes no ruling beyond the wording of the verse, but it is a datum that ought to be put in the table.**

**حَٰفِظَٰتٌ لِّلْغَيْبِ بِمَا حَفِظَ ٱللَّهُ** — "those who guard, where it is not seen, what God has guarded."

**The root ح-ف-ظ occurs twice in the clause: *ḥāfiẓāt* and *ḥafiẓa'llāh*.** And the word order is worth recording: **the ground of the guarding is tied again to a guarding** — *bimā ḥafiẓa'llāh*.

**غَيْب — root غ-ي-ب: to be hidden, to be out of sight.** Here the word states **the place where no one sees**.

### Five: نُشُوز — the analysis of the word and the symmetry within the sūra

**The root ن-ش-ز. And its concrete sense must be recorded: *nashz* — high ground, a hillock, a rise.**

**The family of senses that follows from this:**

| Word | Sense | The link |
|---|---|---|
| **نَشْز / نَشَز** | High ground, a hillock | The concrete sense |
| **نَشَزَ** | To rise; to get up from where one is | From the rise |
| **نُشُوز** | **Getting up from where one is; going out from one's place in a relationship** | — |
| **أَنْشَزَ** | To raise, to lift up | The raising of the bones at `002-bakara.md` 2/259 is from this root |

**The picture in the word is a *rising and a leaving of one's place*.** One party's going out from the position it holds within a relationship.

**And now comes the most important datum of the text in this discussion.**

**The word occurs twice in this sūra, and in its second occurrence it is used of the MAN.**

| Verse | Clause | Whose *nushūz* | What is commanded |
|---|---|---|---|
| **4/34** | *Wa'llātī takhāfūna **nushūzahunna*** | **The woman's** | *Fa-ʿiẓūhunna … wa'hjurūhunna … wa'ḍribūhunna*; and then **arbitration in 4/35** |
| **4/128** | *Wa ini'mraʾatun khāfat min baʿlihā **nushūzan** aw iʿrāḍā* | **The man's** | *Fa-lā junāḥa ʿalayhimā an yuṣliḥā baynahumā ṣulḥā* — **and *wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr*** |

**This is a symmetry that can be verified from the text and a countable fact:** the same root, in the same sūra, is applied to both parties.

**And the detail of the symmetry must be recorded too:**

| | **4/34** | **4/128** |
|---|---|---|
| **The verb of fearing** | *Takhāfūna* — second person plural | *Khāfat* — third person feminine singular |
| **Who fears** | The husband's side | **The woman herself** |
| **The subject** | *Nushūz* | *Nushūz* **and** *iʿrāḍ* (turning away) |
| **The path** | Three steps, then arbitration | **Settlement** |
| **The block's close** | *Iṣlāḥ* (35) | *Wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr* (128) |

**It can be seen that both blocks open with the same word and close in the same field: *iṣlāḥ* and *ṣulḥ*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the two tables above:** the sūra recognises the same phenomenon for both parties, and **ends both blocks in the direction of agreement.**

### Six: three steps — a table of the disagreement

**The verse sets out three verbs: *fa-ʿiẓūhunna*, *wa'hjurūhunna fi'l-maḍājiʿi*, *wa'ḍribūhunna*.**

**The table below gives, with their grounds, the readings transmitted in the classical exegetical and juristic literature about these three steps. None of them is this text's preference, none of them is binding, and no practice can be derived from here.**

| Step | Word | The readings transmitted | Their grounds |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **وَعْظ** (*waʿẓ*) — root و-ع-ظ: to remind in a way that softens the heart | Reminding with words; the opening of the matter by talking | The sense of the root; the verb's standing first |
| **2** | **هَجْر فِي ٱلْمَضَاجِع** (*hajr*) — root ه-ج-ر: to separate, to leave | (a) Leaving alone in bed; (b) separating the beds; (c) restricting only the bed and not speech | That the qualification *fi'l-maḍājiʿ* **restricts** the verb: the leaving is not absolute but tied to a place |
| **3** | **ضَرْب** (*ḍarb*) — root ض-ر-ب | (a) A touch that is **not heavy, that leaves no mark, that does not injure**; this qualification is widely transmitted in the classical literature. (b) That the step is a **deterrent**, and that its actual carrying out is not intended. (c) Reading it, on the strength of the root's wide semantic field, in the direction of **parting / withdrawing**. (d) That the step is tied to particular conditions and is a last resort | For (a): the restricting qualifications transmitted in the classical commentaries. For (b): the ordered and progressively narrowing structure of the steps. For (c): the many-sided use of the root ض-ر-ب in the Qurʾān, as in *ḍaraba fi'l-arḍ* (to set out on a journey), *ḍaraba mathalan* (to give a parable). For (d): the qualification *fa-in aṭaʿnakum fa-lā tabghū* in the continuation of the verse |

**This table is not a fatwā and cannot be read as a guide to practice.** The reason it is placed here is the rule on disagreement in the method (`USLUP.md`): the disagreement is not concealed, no preference is imposed.

**And there are three qualifications that follow directly from the wording of the verse; these are not disagreement but data of the text:**

| Qualification | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **One** | *Wa'llātī **takhāfūna** nushūzahunna* | The subject is not an act that has occurred but a situation that is **feared** |
| **Two** | *Wa'hjurūhunna **fi'l-maḍājiʿi*** | The second step is restricted by being tied to a **place** |
| **Three** | *Fa-in **aṭaʿnakum** fa-lā tabghū ʿalayhinna **sabīlā*** | The **stopping point** of the steps is written out plainly |

**The third qualification must be stressed separately:** having counted the steps, the verse **sets an end** to them, and states that end as a prohibition — *fa-lā tabghū ʿalayhinna sabīlā*: "seek no way against them."

**The root ب-غ-ي: to want, to seek; and to exceed the bound.** These two sides of the word are worth recording: what is forbidden is **the search for a pretext**.

### Seven: إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ عَلِيًّا كَبِيرًا — the choice of verse-ending

**The verse closes with two attributes: *ʿAliyy* (exalted) and *Kabīr* (great).**

**The sūra's order of verse-endings was set out in a table in the introductory section, and most of the endings were attributes of watching. Here there are two attributes of *greatness*.**

**About the function of this verse-ending the following is widely transmitted in the classical commentaries:** because the ruling addresses the party into whose hand authority is given, the closing reminds that party **that there is one greater than himself**.

**I relate this explanation as it is transmitted.** And I add the following **as my own reading**: the verse-ending is directed at the party the verse addresses — because it is he who is addressed by the commands in the verse.

### Eight: 4/35 — the arbitrator, and the block's ending in *iṣlāḥ*

**The thirty-fifth verse is the continuation of the thirty-fourth, and the block is completed only here.**

**And the first thing to be recorded is the change in the word order:**

| | **4/34** | **4/35** |
|---|---|---|
| **The verb of fearing** | *Takhāfūna* | *Khiftum* — the same root |
| **The object of the fear** | *Nushūzahunna* — one party's | *Shiqāqa **baynihimā*** — **what is between the two** |
| **Who is addressed** | One of the spouses | **The community outside** |
| **The remedy** | Inside, three steps | **Two arbitrators from outside** |
| **The aim** | — | *Iṣlāḥ* |

**In the second verse, that is, the matter ceases to be one party's conduct and becomes *what is between the two*.**

***Shiqāq* — root ش-ق-ق: to split, to cleave in two.** *Shaqq* — a fissure. **The word draws the picture of a whole being split in two.** And the qualification *baynihimā* (between the two of them) does not tie the responsibility to one side.

**فَٱبْعَثُوا۟ حَكَمًا مِّنْ أَهْلِهِۦ وَحَكَمًا مِّنْ أَهْلِهَآ** — and the symmetry here is a countable fact:

| | The husband's side | The wife's side |
|---|---|---|
| **Number** | *Ḥakaman* — one | *Ḥakaman* — one |
| **Source** | *Min ahlihī* | *Min ahlihā* |

**The clause is built twice and only the pronoun changes.** This is the same structure as the *naṣīb* symmetry in the thirty-second verse — the sūra repeats this construction.

***Ḥakam* — root ح-ك-م: to make a thing firm, to hold it in check.** From the same root come *ḥikma* and *ḥukm*; and *ḥakama* — the bit of a horse, the thing that holds it. **The picture in the root is holding in check.**

**إِن يُرِيدَآ إِصْلَٰحًا يُوَفِّقِ ٱللَّهُ بَيْنَهُمَآ** — and here there is a disagreement of word order:

| Reading | Who *yurīdā* are | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **The two arbitrators** | The nearest preceding dual noun is the arbitrators |
| **B** | **The two spouses** | The real parties to the setting right are the spouses; the pronoun *baynahumā* refers to the spouses |

**Both readings are transmitted; I impose no preference.** **What can be verified from the text is this: there are two dual pronouns in the sentence (*yurīdā* and *baynahumā*), and the verse does not specify either of them plainly.**

**The root ص-ل-ح was treated in this sūra at the fifteenth and sixteenth verses** (*fa-in tābā wa aṣlaḥā fa-aʿriḍū ʿanhumā*), and it was recorded there under the heading "the block's real qualification": **a door of return is left open after the ruling.** **I rely on that treatment.**

### Nine: where the block ends — and its being read together with 4/128

**Now the block must be looked at as a whole.**

| Step | Verse | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 34 | Three steps from within |
| 2 | End of 34 | *Fa-in aṭaʿnakum fa-lā tabghū* — **the stopping point** |
| 3 | 35 | Two arbitrators from outside |
| 4 | End of 35 | ***Iṣlāḥ* and *tawfīq*** |

**The block ends not with a sanction but with the bringing together of the two.**

**And the sūra does the same thing once more in the hundred and twenty-eighth verse:**

> **وَإِنِ ٱمْرَأَةٌ خَافَتْ مِنۢ بَعْلِهَا نُشُوزًا أَوْ إِعْرَاضًا فَلَا جُنَاحَ عَلَيْهِمَآ أَن يُصْلِحَا بَيْنَهُمَا صُلْحًا وَٱلصُّلْحُ خَيْرٌ**
> *"If a woman fears *nushūz* or turning away from her husband, there is no fault upon the two of them in setting things right between themselves by a settlement. **And settlement is better.**"* (4/128)

**I set the two blocks side by side. The table compares the wording of the two verses; it establishes no ruling.**

| | **4/34-35** | **4/128** |
|---|---|---|
| **Root** | *Nushūz* | *Nushūz* |
| **Whose** | The woman's | **The man's** |
| **Who fears** | Those addressed | **The woman herself** |
| **The path** | Three steps → arbitration | Settlement directly |
| **The close** | *Iṣlāḥan … yuwaffiqi'llāhu baynahumā* | ***Wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr*** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the sūra describes two separate paths in two separate places for the same phenomenon, and **closes both paths with the same family of words — *ṣulḥ* and *iṣlāḥ*.**

**And this is the sūra's general line:** in the twenty-fifth verse *wa an taṣbirū khayrun lakum*, in the hundred and twenty-eighth *wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr*, in the hundred and forty-ninth *in tubdū khayran aw tukhfūhu aw taʿfū ʿan sūʾin*. **These three were set out in a table in the discussion of the twenty-fifth verse; I rely on that.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The structure of the block itself provides for a dispute to be handled **by stages**: first talking, then distance, then an arbitrator from outside. And at every stage there is a qualification of **stopping**: *fa-lā tabghū ʿalayhinna sabīlā*.

**Two.** The arbitration arrangement of the thirty-fifth verse asks that the dispute be handled by **an equal number of people from the families of both parties**. This is a construction that rules out the circle of one party settling the matter on its own.

**Three.** That the word *nushūz* is applied to both parties in two separate verses shows that the sūra does not build this matter in one direction only. **This is not an interpretation but the distribution of the word within the sūra.**

**And a limit:** this section makes no decision about how the verse is to be applied. None of the tables of disagreement above is binding; **to derive a ruling is the business of the literature of jurisprudence, which has the competence for it.**

---

## 4/36 — واعبدوا الله ولا تشركوا به شيئا وبالوالدين إحسانا وبذي القربى واليتامى والمساكين والجار ذي القربى والجار الجنب والصاحب بالجنب وابن السبيل وما ملكت أيمانكم إن الله لا يحب من كان مختالا فخورا

*Wa'ʿbudu'llāha wa lā tushrikū bihī shayʾā, wa bi'l-wālidayni iḥsānan wa bi-dhi'l-qurbā wa'l-yatāmā wa'l-masākīni wa'l-jāri dhi'l-qurbā wa'l-jāri'l-junubi wa'l-ṣāḥibi bi'l-janbi wa'bni'l-sabīli wa mā malakat aymānukum, inna'llāha lā yuḥibbu man kāna mukhtālan fakhūrā*

"Serve God and associate nothing with Him. And to parents show kindness, and to near kin, to orphans, to the poor, to the neighbour who is kin, to the neighbour who is a stranger, to the companion at your side, to the traveller, and to those your right hands possess. God does not love the one who is self-admiring and boastful."

### The construction of the verse — one command and nine addresses

**The verse opens with a command and then counts nine addresses. With the command that makes ten items, and the table shows this.**

| Order | Wording | Who | The kind of tie |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Wa'ʿbudu'llāha wa lā tushrikū bihī shayʾā* | **God** | Service — the frame of the list |
| **2** | *Bi'l-wālidayni iḥsānā* | Parents | Given at birth |
| **3** | *Bi-dhi'l-qurbā* | Near kin | The tie of blood |
| **4** | *Wa'l-yatāmā* | Orphans | Being without protection |
| **5** | *Wa'l-masākīn* | The poor | Need |
| **6** | *Wa'l-jāri dhi'l-qurbā* | **The neighbour who is kin** | Place + kinship |
| **7** | *Wa'l-jāri'l-junub* | **The distant / stranger neighbour** | Place only |
| **8** | *Wa'l-ṣāḥibi bi'l-janb* | **The companion at one's side** | Being together, and nothing more |
| **9** | *Wa'bni'l-sabīl* | The stranded traveller | Transience |
| **10** | *Wa mā malakat aymānukum* | **Those under one's hand** | Dependence |

**And the direction of the ordering is a countable fact: the list moves from the nearest to the furthest.** It begins with the parents, continues with the tie of blood, and then the kind of tie changes — place, then merely companionship on the road, then the traveller.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the list:** the verse does not define the person who has a right **by belonging**. The two neighbours in the sixth and seventh places show this plainly: one is the neighbour who is a relative, the other the neighbour who is not — **and both are in the same list, under the same command.**

### إِحْسَٰن — the root ح-س-ن and the kind of command

**A single word in the accusative governs the whole list: *iḥsānan*.**

**The root ح-س-ن: to be beautiful, to be good.** *Iḥsān* (Form IV) — to do well; and the second sense the lexicographers record: **to do a thing completely, giving it its due.**

**And the word order here must be recorded:** the verse does not say *wa ātū* (give) or *anfiqū* (spend). **The command is not a command about property but a command about *conduct*.** The nine addresses in the list are counted not as people to whom money is to be given but as **people towards whom one is to behave well**.

**This distinction makes the following comparison necessary.**

### A comparison with `002-bakara.md` 2/177 and 2/215

**The Qurʾān sets out the same circle in two other places as well, and when the three lists are set side by side the differences become visible. The table compares the wording of the three verses.**

| Who | **al-Baqara 2/177** | **al-Baqara 2/215** | **al-Nisāʾ 4/36** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Parents** | — | *Al-wālidayn* | *Al-wālidayn* |
| **Near kin** | *Dhawi'l-qurbā* | *Al-aqrabīn* | *Dhi'l-qurbā* |
| **Orphans** | *Al-yatāmā* | *Al-yatāmā* | *Al-yatāmā* |
| **The poor** | *Al-masākīn* | *Al-masākīn* | *Al-masākīn* |
| **The stranded traveller** | *Ibna'l-sabīl* | *Ibni'l-sabīl* | *Ibni'l-sabīl* |
| **The one who asks** | *Al-sāʾilīn* | — | — |
| **The freeing of slaves** | *Fi'l-riqāb* | — | — |
| **The neighbour who is kin** | — | — | ***Al-jār dhi'l-qurbā*** |
| **The stranger neighbour** | — | — | ***Al-jār al-junub*** |
| **The companion on the road** | — | — | ***Al-ṣāḥib bi'l-janb*** |
| **Those under one's hand** | — | — | ***Mā malakat aymānukum*** |
| **The object of the command** | **Property** — *wa āta'l-māla ʿalā ḥubbih* | **Property** — *mā anfaqtum min khayr* | ***Iḥsān*** — conduct |

**Two things follow from the table, and both are data of the text:**

**One. The common core is five items:** near kin, orphans, the poor, the traveller, and (in two places) parents. **All three verses are built around the same core.**

**Two. The four items al-Nisāʾ adds are of a different kind from the common core:** two neighbours, the companion on the road, and those under one's hand. **None of these is defined by blood or by need; all of them are defined by *nearness*** — a nearness of place, of a passing moment, or of structure.

**And this coincides with the line recorded in the discussion of `002-bakara.md` 2/177.** What was written there was this:

> *"That is, the list widens from the near to the far and spills outside belonging… the measure does not change according to who the person before you is."*

**Al-Nisāʾ's list goes four steps further in the same direction.** I record this as my own reading; its ground is the table above.

### ٱلْجَار and ٱلْجَنْب — the analysis of two words

**The root ج-و-ر: to be side by side, to be a neighbour; and from there *jiwār* — protection, taking under one's shelter.** The second sense the word carries is worth recording: in Arabic to be someone's *jār* also means to **give him security**.

**ٱلْجَنْب — root ج-ن-ب: the side, the flank.** And this root occurred in the thirty-first verse as *ijtanibū* (avoid); **here the same root returns in the direction of nearness.**

| Verse | Word | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *Tajtanibū* | **Moving away** — leaving the prohibition aside and passing on |
| **36** | *Al-junub*, *bi'l-janb* | **Nearness** — the one at your side |

**The same root, five verses apart, in two opposite directions.** This is a fact that can be verified within the sūra.

**وَٱلصَّاحِبِ بِٱلْجَنۢبِ — there is disagreement over whom it means:**

| Reading | Who | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **The companion on the road** — one walked beside for a while | That the qualification *bi'l-janb* states transience; its coming immediately before the traveller in the list |
| **B** | **Any companion** who sits beside you, who is beside you at work | The generality of the word |
| **C** | **The wife** | That the word *ṣāḥib* is used in the Qurʾān for a spouse as well |

**All three readings are transmitted; I impose no preference.**

**What can be said from the text is this:** the word ties the bond neither to duration nor to descent but to **being side by side**. **This is the weakest tie in the list, and it has been taken into the list.**

### وَمَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَٰنُكُمْ — the end of the list

**The list ends with the section of society seen as lowest.**

**And this happens for the second time in the sūra.** In the twenty-fifth verse, while a ruling was being given about the same group, *baʿḍukum min baʿḍ* ("you are, one of you, from another") was set into the middle of the sentence, and it was treated there. **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the shared subject of the two verses:** the sūra names this group twice, and on both occasions names it **by taking it into a list**.

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُحِبُّ مَن كَانَ مُخْتَالًا فَخُورًا — the verse-ending

**Having counted nine addresses, the verse closes with two attributes, and the choice of the verse-ending must be recorded.**

***Mukhtāl* — root خ-ي-ل: to suppose a thing, to imagine it; and from there, to suppose oneself something.** From the same root comes *khayl* (horse), and the lexicographers make the link through **the swagger in a horse's gait**.

***Fakhūr* — root ف-خ-ر: to boast.** *Fakhr* — boasting.

**And the distinction between the two attributes is worth recording:**

| Attribute | Where it takes place |
|---|---|
| ***Mukhtāl*** | **Inside** — a person's opinion of himself |
| ***Fakhūr*** | **Outside** — that opinion coming into speech |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the place of the verse-ending:** a list of nine items closes with the rejection of **seeing oneself as above others**. **What is needed for the list to work is stated in the verse-ending.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The two items about the neighbour say that a right arises from **unchosen** ties as well. A neighbour is not chosen; and the verse nonetheless sets him immediately after near kin.

**Two.** That the command is *iḥsān* shows that the list is not a list for charity. What is to be given to most of the nine addresses counted is not money.

---

## 4/37-39 — الذين يبخلون ويأمرون الناس بالبخل ويكتمون ما آتاهم الله من فضله وأعتدنا للكافرين عذابا مهينا · والذين ينفقون أموالهم رئاء الناس ولا يؤمنون بالله ولا باليوم الآخر · وماذا عليهم لو آمنوا بالله واليوم الآخر وأنفقوا مما رزقهم الله

*Alladhīna yabkhalūna wa yaʾmurūna'l-nāsa bi'l-bukhli wa yaktumūna mā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlih … Wa'lladhīna yunfiqūna amwālahum riʾāʾa'l-nāsi wa lā yuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa lā bi'l-yawmi'l-ākhir … Wa mādhā ʿalayhim law āmanū bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhiri wa anfaqū mimmā razaqahumu'llāh*

"They are miserly and they enjoin miserliness upon others, and they conceal what God has given them of His bounty… And those who spend their wealth to be seen by others and believe neither in God nor in the Last Day… What harm would it have done them, had they believed in God and the Last Day and spent of what God has provided them?"

### Two contrary states following the thirty-sixth verse

**The thirty-sixth verse gave a list. These three verses describe two states in which that list does not work.**

| State | Verse | Verb | The problem |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The first** | 37 | *Yabkhalūn* — **they do not give** | The holding back of property |
| **The second** | 38 | *Yunfiqūn riʾāʾa'l-nās* — **they do give** | The direction of the giving |

**And setting the two states side by side produces a record, which I set down as my own reading:** the verses are not concerned only with not giving; **they say that giving too can be corrupted.** In the thirty-eighth verse property is actually spent, and it is rejected all the same.

### بُخْل and the enjoining of it

**The root ب-خ-ل: to hold in hand, not to give.**

**And in the thirty-seventh verse three verbs are set out, and the three form a chain:**

| Order | Verb | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Yabkhalūn* | He himself does not give |
| 2 | *Yaʾmurūna'l-nāsa bi'l-bukhl* | **He urges others not to give either** |
| 3 | *Yaktumūna mā ātāhumu'llāh* | He conceals what has been given him |

**The second step is worth recording:** the verse says that the attitude has a **spreading** side. This pattern is present at `107-maun.md` 107/3 as well (*wa lā yaḥuḍḍu ʿalā ṭaʿāmi'l-miskīn* — he does not urge the feeding of the poor): **there too the matter passes from not giving to not urging.**

**The third step also produces an observation:** *yaktumūna mā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlih* — to conceal what one has. **To hide what is in one's hand, that is, ensures that none of the addresses in the list ever comes to the door.**

### رِئَآءَ ٱلنَّاسِ — the root ر-أ-ي

**The word *riʾāʾ* is the verbal noun of Form III (*mufāʿala*) from the root ر-أ-ي: it states reciprocity.** **Ostentation, that is, is not a one-sided business: someone who sees is required.**

**The word was treated at `107-maun.md` 107/6 (*alladhīna hum yurāʾūn*) and at `002-bakara.md` 2/264 (*ka'lladhī yunfiqu mālahū riʾāʾa'l-nās*).** **The verse in al-Baqara is almost identical with this one:**

| Place | Expression |
|---|---|
| al-Baqara 2/264 | *Ka'lladhī yunfiqu mālahū **riʾāʾa'l-nās** wa lā yuʾminu bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhir* |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/38** | *Alladhīna yunfiqūna amwālahum **riʾāʾa'l-nās** wa lā yuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa lā bi'l-yawmi'l-ākhir* |

**The two verses set the same two things side by side: ostentation and not believing in the hereafter.** This is a repetition between two sūras that can be verified.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the shared word order of the two verses:** what is set over against ostentation is not a rule of morals but the question of **where the return is expected**.

### وَمَاذَا عَلَيْهِمْ لَوْ ءَامَنُوا۟ — the form of the question

**The thirty-ninth verse is a question, and the form of the sentence must be recorded: *wa mādhā ʿalayhim* — "what would it have cost them?"**

**The question is not a threat but a question of *cost*.** And this fits the general language of the sūra: in verses twenty-six to twenty-eight the intention behind the rulings was stated after them; here what is lost by refusing is asked.

---

## 4/40-42 — إن الله لا يظلم مثقال ذرة وإن تك حسنة يضاعفها ويؤت من لدنه أجرا عظيما · فكيف إذا جئنا من كل أمة بشهيد وجئنا بك على هؤلاء شهيدا · يومئذ يود الذين كفروا وعصوا الرسول لو تسوى بهم الأرض ولا يكتمون الله حديثا

*Inna'llāha lā yaẓlimu mithqāla dharra, wa in taku ḥasanatan yuḍāʿifhā wa yuʾti min ladunhu ajran ʿaẓīmā · Fa-kayfa idhā jiʾnā min kulli ummatin bi-shahīdin wa jiʾnā bika ʿalā hāʾulāʾi shahīdā · Yawmaʾidhin yawaddu'lladhīna kafarū wa ʿaṣawu'l-rasūla law tusawwā bihimu'l-arḍ, wa lā yaktumūna'llāha ḥadīthā*

"God does not wrong anyone by so much as an atom's weight. If there is a good deed, He multiplies it and gives from Himself a great reward. How will it be when We bring a witness from every community and bring you as a witness against these? On that day those who denied and disobeyed the Messenger will wish that the earth were levelled over them. And they will not be able to conceal a single word from God."

### مِثْقَالَ ذَرَّةٍ — the smallness of the measure

**م-ث-ق-ل: weight. ذ-ر-ر: the smallest particle.** The lexicographers explain *dharra* as **the smallest of ants** or as **the dust seen in a shaft of sunlight**.

**The word was treated in detail at `099-zilzal.md` 99/7-8 (*fa-man yaʿmal mithqāla dharratin khayran yarah*). I rely on that treatment.**

**And the word order here must be recorded: on the side of *wrongdoing* the verse brings the measure down to the smallest, while on the side of *recompense* it multiplies it** — *yuḍāʿifhā* (He multiplies it).

| Direction | Measure |
|---|---|
| **Wrongdoing** | Not even so much as *mithqāla dharra* |
| **A good deed** | *Yuḍāʿifhā* — **many times over** |

**This is not a symmetry; it is a deliberate imbalance.** I record it as an observation about word order.

### شَهِيد — one of the sūra's key concepts

**In the forty-first verse the word *shahīd* occurs twice: a witness from every community, and one witness more.**

**The root ش-ه-د was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/282 and in `024-nur.md`: to be present, to see; and to report what one has seen. I rely on those treatments.**

**And the density of the root in this sūra must be recorded.** In the sūra's verse-endings there occur *shahīd* (33), *ḥasīb* (6, 86), *raqīb* (1), *samīʿ baṣīr* (58), *khabīr* (35, 135); and in the forty-first verse witnessing becomes a **scene**.

**This is the continuation of the line established in the sūra's introductory section:** *"the sūra sets a witness beside the person whose right is unprotected."* **I rely on that.**

### لَوْ تُسَوَّىٰ بِهِمُ ٱلْأَرْضُ — a clause of wishing

**The root س-و-ي: to be even, to level.** *Tusawwā bihimu'l-arḍ* — "that the earth be levelled with them."

**It is transmitted that in the classical commentaries the expression is explained in two ways:** to wish to be mixed into the soil and vanish, or to wish that the earth would flatten out and cover them. **I relate both explanations; I impose no preference.**

**وَلَا يَكْتُمُونَ ٱللَّهَ حَدِيثًا** — "they cannot conceal a single word from God."

**And the choice of verb must be recorded: *yaktumūn* had occurred in the thirty-seventh verse too** (*yaktumūna mā ātāhumu'llāh*). **The same root twice, five verses apart: one a concealing in this world, the other an inability to conceal.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 4/43 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا لا تقربوا الصلاة وأنتم سكارى حتى تعلموا ما تقولون ولا جنبا إلا عابري سبيل حتى تغتسلوا … فتيمموا صعيدا طيبا فامسحوا بوجوهكم وأيديكم إن الله كان عفوا غفورا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū lā taqrabu'ṣ-ṣalāta wa antum sukārā ḥattā taʿlamū mā taqūlūn … fa-tayammamū ṣaʿīdan ṭayyiban fa'msaḥū bi-wujūhikum wa aydīkum, inna'llāha kāna ʿAfuwwan Ghafūrā*

"O you who believe! Do not draw near to prayer while you are intoxicated, until you know what you are saying… If you find no water, turn to clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands with it. God is ever pardoning, forgiving."

### First, a note on method

**A wide discussion in classical jurisprudence arose from this verse: ablution, the full washing, dry ablution and their conditions. That discussion is not entered into in this section.**

**The reason is the method's rule against issuing legal rulings.** There is also a well-known classical disagreement over the scope of the expression *aw lāmastumu'l-nisāʾ* in the verse; **the details and consequences of that disagreement are not the subject of this text.** What is treated here is the **linguistic side** of the verse.

### حَتَّىٰ تَعْلَمُوا۟ مَا تَقُولُونَ — the measure the verse itself gives

**The most striking side of the verse is that it ties the prohibition not to a *condition* but to a *state of knowing*.**

**The sentence could have been built like this, but it is not:**

| It could have been | It is |
|---|---|
| Do not draw near to prayer while intoxicated | Do not draw near to prayer while intoxicated, **until you know what you are saying** |

**The verse, that is, gives its own ground within itself: that what is said in prayer be said knowingly.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the particle *ḥattā*:** the particle states a **limit**, and the limit is the understanding of the words.

### The root ص-ع-د and طَيِّبًا

***Ṣaʿīd* — root ص-ع-د: to rise, to go up.** From the same root come *ṣuʿūd* (ascent) and *ṣaʿūd* (a hard climb). **The lexicographers explain *ṣaʿīd* as "the top of the earth, the soil at the surface"** — that is, the **rising**, outermost face of the ground.

***Ṭayyib* — root ط-ي-ب: pleasant, clean, good.** The word occurred in the second verse of this sūra as well: *lā tatabaddalu'l-khabītha bi'l-ṭayyib* ("do not exchange the good for the foul"). **The same root, once for the quality of property and once for the quality of earth.**

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ عَفُوًّا غَفُورًا — the verse-ending

**The root ع-ف-و: to wipe away, to erase the trace of; and to pardon.** **The root غ-ف-ر: to cover** (*mighfar* — the helmet that covers the head).

**And the difference between the two roots is worth recording:**

| Root | Picture |
|---|---|
| **ع-ف-و** | The **erasing** of the trace |
| **غ-ف-ر** | The **covering over** of what is there |

**That this verse-ending stands in this verse must be recorded:** the verse gives a ruling of relief and closes with two attributes of pardon. **This continues the line of *yurīdu'llāhu an yukhaffifa ʿankum* in the twenty-eighth verse.**

---

## 4/44-46 — ألم تر إلى الذين أوتوا نصيبا من الكتاب يشترون الضلالة ويريدون أن تضلوا السبيل · والله أعلم بأعدائكم وكفى بالله وليا وكفى بالله نصيرا · من الذين هادوا يحرفون الكلم عن مواضعه ويقولون سمعنا وعصينا … ولو أنهم قالوا سمعنا وأطعنا واسمع وانظرنا لكان خيرا لهم وأقوم

*A-lam tara ila'lladhīna ūtū naṣīban mina'l-kitābi yashtarūna'l-ḍalālata wa yurīdūna an taḍillu'l-sabīl · Wa'llāhu aʿlamu bi-aʿdāʾikum wa kafā bi'llāhi waliyyan wa kafā bi'llāhi naṣīrā · Mina'lladhīna hādū yuḥarrifūna'l-kalima ʿan mawāḍiʿihī …*

"Have you not seen those who were given a share of the Book? They buy error and want you to lose the way… Some of them shift words from their places and say 'we hear and we disobey'… Had they said 'we hear and we obey, listen, and look upon us', it would have been better for them and more upright."

### First, a note on method

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) a record is entered, and this record holds for all the discussions running from the forty-fourth verse to the hundred and sixty-second.**

**The verses do not lay down a ruling about a whole community.** The wording shows this plainly:

| Wording | What it says |
|---|---|
| *Alladhīna ūtū **naṣīban** mina'l-kitāb* | "Those given **a share** of the Book" — an indefinite section |
| ***Mina**'lladhīna hādū* | *Min* — **"some of them"**; a particle stating a part |
| End of 4/46: *fa-lā yuʾminūna **illā qalīlā*** | A qualification of exception |
| 4/162: *lākini'l-rāsikhūna fi'l-ʿilmi **minhum*** | A section is set apart **from within the same community** |

**The verses themselves, that is, make a division.** And what is at issue in this commentary are **attributes**; whoever bears the attribute is included in it. **No general ruling is made about any religious, ethnic or national group.**

### يُحَرِّفُونَ ٱلْكَلِمَ عَن مَّوَاضِعِهِۦ — *taḥrīf*

**The root ح-ر-ف was treated in detail at `002-bakara.md` 2/75, and what was recorded there was this:**

> *"The root **حرف**: an edge, a tip, a border. Not the middle of a thing but its rim… On this reckoning **taḥrīf**, in the word's own logic, is: **to bend a thing at its edge.** Not to tear the text down the middle, but to pull it slightly at the rim, to shift its direction. A slight crookedness produces a great deviation at a distance."*

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is this: in the expression in al-Nisāʾ there is an addition — *ʿan mawāḍiʿihī* ("from their places").**

**م-و-ض-ع: the place in which a thing has been set.** **The verse, that is, describes distortion as a *displacement*:** the word is not destroyed, it is **moved from its place**.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the use of the two words together:** when *ḥarf* (edge) and *mawḍiʿ* (the place in which a thing is set) come side by side, the picture that emerges is this — **the word stands; the place where it stands changes.**

### سَمِعْنَا وَعَصَيْنَا — and the sentence set over against it

**The verse relates one utterance, then gives the utterance that could have taken its place. Setting the two side by side is the verse's own doing.**

| What is said | What could have been said |
|---|---|
| *Samiʿnā wa **ʿaṣaynā*** — we hear and we disobey | *Samiʿnā wa **aṭaʿnā*** — we hear and we obey |
| *Wa'smaʿ **ghayra musmaʿin*** | *Wa'smaʿ* |
| *Rāʿinā* | ***Unẓurnā*** |

**In all three items what changes is small; and the verse qualifies this with the word *aqwam* (more upright, more erect).**

***Aqwam* — the elative from the root ق-و-م.** **The root occurs in this sūra at the fifth verse (*qiyāmā*), at the thirty-fourth (*qawwāmūn*) and here; it will return once more at the hundred and thirty-fifth (*qawwāmīna bi'l-qisṭ*).** **The root's four occurrences in the sūra will be set out in a table in the concluding section.**

**وَلَىًّۢا بِأَلْسِنَتِهِمْ** — *layy*: root ل-و-ي, **to twist, to bend.** **And the word carries the picture of distortion over into the tongue: as the words are shifted from their places, so the tongue too is twisted.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse uses two verbs in the same place — *yuḥarrifūn* (to shift) and *layyan bi-alsinatihim* (twisting with their tongues). **Both are verbs that state crookedness.**

---

## 4/47-50 — يا أيها الذين أوتوا الكتاب آمنوا بما نزلنا مصدقا لما معكم … إن الله لا يغفر أن يشرك به ويغفر ما دون ذلك لمن يشاء · ألم تر إلى الذين يزكون أنفسهم بل الله يزكي من يشاء

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba āminū bimā nazzalnā muṣaddiqan limā maʿakum … Inna'llāha lā yaghfiru an yushraka bihī wa yaghfiru mā dūna dhālika li-man yashāʾ · A-lam tara ila'lladhīna yuzakkūna anfusahum, bali'llāhu yuzakkī man yashāʾ*

"O you who were given the Book! Believe in what We have sent down, confirming what is with you… God does not forgive that partners be associated with Him; what is other than that He forgives for whom He wills. Have you not seen those who declare themselves pure? No — it is God who purifies whom He wills."

### مُصَدِّقًا لِّمَا مَعَكُمْ — the function of the word

**The root ص-د-ق was treated at the fourth verse of this sūra (*ṣaduqātihinna*). I rely on that treatment.**

***Muṣaddiq* — active participle: one who confirms.** And the form of the call must be recorded: the verse does not ask those addressed to abandon what they hold but **to believe in something that confirms what they hold**.

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يَغْفِرُ أَن يُشْرَكَ بِهِۦ — and twice in the sūra

**This sentence occurs twice in the sūra: at the forty-eighth verse and at the hundred and sixteenth. The two occurrences are almost identical, and the only difference is in one word.**

| Verse | Sentence | Difference |
|---|---|---|
| **48** | *Wa yaghfiru mā dūna dhālika **li-man yashāʾ*** | — |
| **116** | *Wa yaghfiru mā dūna dhālika **li-man yashāʾ*** | The verse continues on with *wa man yushrik bi'llāhi fa-qad ḍalla ḍalālan baʿīdā* |

**That two verses repeat the same sentence is a countable fact.** And the places of the repetition must be recorded: one inside an address to the People of the Book, the other inside the discussion of Satan's promise. **The same sentence, for two different parties addressed.**

**And the structure of the sentence is worth recording:** one exception is laid down (*lā yaghfiru an yushraka bihī*) and **everything else** falls within the scope of forgiveness (*mā dūna dhālika*). **The narrowing half of the sentence is a single item; the widening half is without limit.**

### يُزَكُّونَ أَنفُسَهُمْ — the root ز-ك-و

**The root's original sense: to increase, to grow; and from there, to be purified, to be clean.** **The lexicographers record these two sides of the root — *increase* and *purification* — together:** a thing grows when it is cleared of foreign elements.

**And what the verse does is a *change of subject*:**

| Clause | Subject |
|---|---|
| *Yuzakkūna **anfusahum*** | They themselves |
| *Bali'**llāhu** yuzakkī man yashāʾ* | **God** |

**The same verb, with two different subjects, in a single verse.** This is a fact of word order that can be verified.

**I record this as my own reading:** the verse does not forbid the act — **it changes its subject.** Purification, that is, is not rejected; what is rejected is the judgement a person passes about himself.

**And the same field was treated at `053-necm.md` 53/32:** *fa-lā tuzakkū anfusakum huwa aʿlamu bi-mani'ttaqā*. **The two verses use the same verb and the same direction.**

---

## 4/51-57 — ألم تر إلى الذين أوتوا نصيبا من الكتاب يؤمنون بالجبت والطاغوت … أم يحسدون الناس على ما آتاهم الله من فضله · إن الذين كفروا بآياتنا سوف نصليهم نارا · والذين آمنوا وعملوا الصالحات سندخلهم جنات تجري من تحتها الأنهار

*A-lam tara ila'lladhīna ūtū naṣīban mina'l-kitābi yuʾminūna bi'l-jibti wa'l-ṭāghūt … Am yaḥsudūna'l-nāsa ʿalā mā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlih …*

"Have you not seen those who were given a share of the Book? They believe in *jibt* and in *ṭāghūt*… Or do they envy people for what God has given them of His bounty?"

### ٱلْجِبْت and ٱلطَّٰغُوت

**The word *jibt* occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** The lexicographers **do not agree on whether the word is of Arabic origin**; for its sense such renderings as "a thing with no good in it", "an idol", "soothsaying" are transmitted. **Nothing definite is said in this commentary about the origin of the word.**

***Ṭāghūt* — root ط-غ-ي: to exceed the bound, to overflow.** **The root was treated at `016-nahl.md` 16/36 (*ani'ʿbudu'llāha wa'jtanibu'l-ṭāghūt*), and it was recorded there that in respect of its pattern the word states intensification and is used for both singular and plural. I rely on that treatment.**

**The word occurs twice in this sūra: at the fifty-first verse and at the sixtieth.** In the second it will be named as the authority resorted to for judgement.

### أَم يَحْسُدُونَ ٱلنَّاسَ — envy made a subject

**The root ح-س-د: to wish that a favour someone has should leave him.**

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded:** the sentence says *mā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlih* — "what God has given them of His bounty". **The same expression occurred at the thirty-second verse** (*mā faḍḍala'llāhu bihī baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍ*), and there **coveting** was forbidden.

| Verse | Verb | Those addressed |
|---|---|---|
| **32** | *Lā tatamannaw* — do not covet | Those who believe |
| **54** | *Am yaḥsudūn* — do they envy | The group described |

**The same field, in two separate places: one a command, the other a diagnosis.** I record this as an observation about word order.

### كُلَّمَا نَضِجَتْ جُلُودُهُم بَدَّلْنَٰهُمْ جُلُودًا غَيْرَهَا — the fifty-sixth verse

***Naḍija* — root ن-ض-ج: to be cooked, to ripen.** **The use of the word in this context is striking: the root is used for the ripening of fruit as well.**

**The qualification entered in this commentary about descriptions of the hereafter holds here too:** nothing definite is said about the nature and manner of the description.

**وَنُدْخِلُهُمْ ظِلًّا ظَلِيلًا** (57) — *ẓillan ẓalīlā*: **a shade and the attribute of shade, from the same root.** This is one of the patterns of emphasis in Arabic. **And the block closes with a shade.**

---

## 4/58 — إن الله يأمركم أن تؤدوا الأمانات إلى أهلها وإذا حكمتم بين الناس أن تحكموا بالعدل إن الله نعما يعظكم به إن الله كان سميعا بصيرا

*Inna'llāha yaʾmurukum an tuʾaddu'l-amānāti ilā ahlihā wa idhā ḥakamtum bayna'l-nāsi an taḥkumū bi'l-ʿadl, inna'llāha niʿimmā yaʿiẓukum bih, inna'llāha kāna Samīʿan Baṣīrā*

"God commands you to deliver trusts to those entitled to them, and when you judge between people to judge with justice. Excellent is what God admonishes you with. God is Hearing, Seeing."

### The verse's place in the sūra

**This verse opens the sūra's ninth block and joins two commands in one sentence.**

**And the coming of the two commands together builds a structure:**

| Command | Field | Who to whom |
|---|---|---|
| *Tuʾaddu'l-amānāti ilā ahlihā* | **What is held** | The holder of the trust → its owner |
| *Idhā ḥakamtum … an taḥkumū bi'l-ʿadl* | **What is decided** | The one who judges → the parties |

**What the two commands have in common must be recorded: in both, something belonging to another is in one party's hands.** In the one it is property or an office, in the other a decision.

**And this is the very centre of the line established in the sūra's introductory section:** *"the person whose right is in someone else's hands."* **I rely on that.**

### أَمَانَة — the root أ-م-ن and `033-ahzab.md` 33/72

**The root's original sense: to be secure, to be without fear.** *Amn* — security; *īmān* — trusting and giving trust; *amīn* — trustworthy; *amāna* — **what is left with someone in trust.**

**The widest use of the word is at `033-ahzab.md` 33/72, and I rely on that treatment. A summary of what was recorded there:**

> *"**Al-amāna** comes with the definite article — that is, it points to something known — but its content is not opened up… That the verse leaves the word indefinite is a datum of the text."*

**And in the table of disagreement given there over what the trust is, this verse was named as the ground of one view:** *"E. Trusts between people — what individuals leave with one another: property, a secret, an office. Its ground: the dictionary sense of the word; al-Nisāʾ 4/58."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**Now the two verses must be set side by side, because the same word is used at two different scales:**

| | **al-Aḥzāb 33/72** | **al-Nisāʾ 4/58** |
|---|---|---|
| **Number** | *Al-amāna* — **singular**, definite | *Al-amānāt* — **plural** |
| **Who carries it** | *Al-insān* — the human being as a kind | *Yaʾmurukum* — the community addressed |
| **What is asked** | It is stated, not commanded | ***Tuʾaddū*** — **let them be delivered** |
| **Direction** | Given to the human being | *Ilā **ahlihā*** — **back to its owner** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the number and the verbs of the two verses:** the verse of al-Aḥzāb builds the trust as a **burden**; the verse of al-Nisāʾ makes it plural, as things **to be returned**. **The one gives the concept, the other its application.**

***Tuʾaddū* — root أ-د-ي: to convey a thing fully, without deficiency.** *Adāʾ* — the complete discharge of a debt or an obligation. **The verb states not "to give" but "to convey as is due".**

***Ilā ahlihā* — "to those entitled to them".** **The root أ-ه-ل: to belong to a place, to settle in it.** *Ahl* — the true owner of a thing, the one worthy of it. **The verse does not say "to its owner", it says "to those entitled to it"** — and this word is wide enough to take in both ownership and desert.

### وَإِذَا حَكَمْتُم بَيْنَ ٱلنَّاسِ — the breadth of the address

**The conditional pattern of the clause must be recorded: *idhā ḥakamtum* — "when you judge".**

**The verse names no office.** The condition is framed so as to take in every case in which the act of judging occurs.

**And the qualification *bayna'l-nās* must be recorded separately: "between people".** The verse describes the parties to be judged by no belonging.

**This will return once more, and more sharply, at the hundred and thirty-fifth verse:** *wa law ʿalā anfusikum awi'l-wālidayni wa'l-aqrabīn*. **The two verses work the same field, and will be tabled together in the concluding section.**

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ سَمِيعًۢا بَصِيرًا — the verse-ending

**The sūra's order of verse-endings was set out in a table in the introductory section. This verse's ending is two attributes of *perception*: hearing and seeing.**

**And that the ending is chosen to suit the subject must be recorded:** a trust is most often something **unseen**; a judgement is something **spoken**. **The two attributes answer to the two commands separately.**

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the same method established in the discussion of `005-maide.md` 5/8:** there too it was shown that the verse-ending is chosen to suit the subject (*khabīr*, because He knows the inward side of testimony). **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 4/59 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا أطيعوا الله وأطيعوا الرسول وأولي الأمر منكم فإن تنازعتم في شيء فردوه إلى الله والرسول إن كنتم تؤمنون بالله واليوم الآخر ذلك خير وأحسن تأويلا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū aṭīʿu'llāha wa aṭīʿu'l-rasūla wa uli'l-amri minkum, fa-in tanāzaʿtum fī shayʾin fa-ruddūhu ila'llāhi wa'l-rasūli in kuntum tuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhir, dhālika khayrun wa aḥsanu taʾwīlā*

"O you who believe! Obey God, and obey the Messenger, and those among you who hold command (*ulu'l-amr*). If you differ over anything, refer it to God and the Messenger — if you believe in God and the Last Day. That is better and finer in outcome."

### First, a note on method

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) two things are not done in this discussion:**

**One. No preference is imposed in the disagreement over who the *ulu'l-amr* are.** The disagreement is given as a table, with its grounds, and none of it is binding.

**Two. Current politics is absolutely not entered into.** The argument over which office, which order or which authority this verse fits today **is not the subject of this text.** This record was entered in the sūra's introductory section as well; I rely on that.

**What is treated is the wording and the word order of the verse.**

### One: the repetition of the verb أَطِيعُوا۟ — and where it is not repeated

**This is the plainest datum of the text in the verse, and a countable fact.**

| Order | To whom | Is the verb repeated for it |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Allāha* | ***Aṭīʿū*** — yes, with its own verb |
| **2** | *Al-rasūla* | ***Aṭīʿū*** — **yes, the verb is repeated** |
| **3** | *Uli'l-amri minkum* | **No — the verb is not repeated**; the word is coordinated with the second verb |

**The wording of the sentence is this: *aṭīʿu'llāha wa aṭīʿu'l-rasūla wa uli'l-amri minkum*.**

**For the first and the second, that is, the verb is brought separately; for the third it is not.** This is a fact that can be seen and counted in the structure of the sentence.
