**It is widely recorded in the classical commentaries that this difference carries a meaning, and it is explained thus:** the verb's not being repeated shows that the obedience in the third place is to be understood **not on its own account but as bound to the second**.

**I relate this explanation as it is transmitted, and I add the following as my own reading:** this is, before it is exegesis, a **grammatical observation** — the difference between repeating the verb and coordinating carries meaning in Arabic, and here the two stand side by side in the same sentence. **The sentence itself has established a gradation.**

### Two: a second datum of the text — to what a dispute is referred

**And in the second half of the verse the same sequence is not built again.**

| Clause | Those counted |
|---|---|
| **The first half** | God, the Messenger, ***ulu'l-amr*** |
| **The second half** | *Fa-ruddūhu **ila'llāhi wa'l-rasūl*** — God and the Messenger |

**When the place to which a disagreement is to be taken is named, the third item is not named.** This too is a countable fact.

**I record this as an observation about word order, not as a ruling:** the verse counts three parties to be obeyed and two authorities for a dispute.

### Three: أُو۟لِى ٱلْأَمْرِ — a table of the disagreement

**Whom the word means is not explained in the verse, and there is disagreement in the classical commentaries. I put it in a table; I impose no preference and none of it is binding.**

| Reading | Who the *ulu'l-amr* are | Its ground | Its qualification |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **The scholars / the jurists** | That the continuation of the verse takes disputes to the Book and the Sunna, and that this is the business of the people of knowledge; the expression *alladhīna yastanbiṭūnahū minhum* in the eighty-third verse | It has difficulty answering to the administrative association of the word *amr* |
| **B** | **Those who govern / those in charge of affairs** | The sense of the word *amr* as "affair, command, governance"; the presence of the community's order in the verse's context | Its limit is indefinite; under what conditions it holds is not written in the verse |
| **C** | **Both together** — in every field, those competent in that field | The generality of the word; that *amr* is not confined to a particular field | It is the widest reading in scope |
| **D** | **Those put in charge** of particular assignments | That the qualification *minkum* makes being from within the community a condition | It narrows the general address of the verse |

**All four readings have been defended in the classical literature. I prefer none of them.**

**But there are three qualifications that follow directly from the wording of the verse, and these are not disagreement but data of the text:**

| Qualification | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **One** | *Uli'l-amri **minkum*** | The condition of being **from within** the community addressed |
| **Two** | That the verb *aṭīʿū* is not repeated for the third | It **binds** that obedience to the second |
| **Three** | *Fa-ruddūhu **ila'llāhi wa'l-rasūl*** | The third item is **absent** from the authority for a dispute |

**These three qualifications are read from the verse's own sentence. I draw no political conclusion from them and do not count the drawing of one as this text's business.**

### Four: تَنَازُع — and `008-enfal.md` 8/46

***Tanāzaʿtum* — root ن-ز-ع, Form VI: to contend with one another.**

**The concrete sense of the root ن-ز-ع: to pull a thing out of its place.** *Nazaʿa* — he pulled out, he tugged. **So *tanāzuʿ* is two parties pulling something each towards himself** — the picture of a rope pulled from both ends.

**The word was treated at `008-enfal.md` 8/46, and a chain was recorded there. I carry it over here:**

> *"And the chain must be recorded: *tanāzuʿ* (quarrelling) → *fashal* (losing heart) → *dhahābu'r-rīḥ* (the going of the strength). **Three steps, in a single sentence.**"*

**And it was shown in al-Anfāl that this chain coincides with the sūra's first verse:** *wa aṣliḥū dhāta baynikum* — set right what is between you. **I rely on that treatment.**

**Setting the two verses side by side gives a link between two sūras that can be verified:**

| | **al-Anfāl 8/46** | **al-Nisāʾ 4/59** |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Wa lā tanāzaʿū* — **a prohibition** | *Fa-in tanāzaʿtum* — **a condition** |
| **Context** | In the ranks of battle | Judgement within the community |
| **Outcome** | *Fa-tafshalū wa tadhhaba rīḥukum* | *Fa-ruddūhu ila'llāhi wa'l-rasūl* |
| **What it gives** | The **cost** of quarrelling | The **way out** of quarrelling |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two verses use the same word:** one forbids contention and states its outcome; the other **assumes contention will occur** and points to an authority. **The two do not stand in for one another; they complete one another.**

### Five: أَحْسَنُ تَأْوِيلًا — the closing

***Taʾwīl* — root أ-و-ل: to return, for a thing to return to its origin.** *Awwal* — first; *maʾāl* — the place a thing comes to. **So *taʾwīl* is the bringing of a thing to the place it will come to.**

**And the choice of the word here must be recorded: the verse says *aḥsanu taʾwīlā* — "finer in outcome".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the sentence gives two grounds — *khayr* (better) and *aḥsanu taʾwīlā* (finer in outcome). **The first looks to worth, the second to outcome.**

---

## 4/60-63 — ألم تر إلى الذين يزعمون أنهم آمنوا بما أنزل إليك وما أنزل من قبلك يريدون أن يتحاكموا إلى الطاغوت وقد أمروا أن يكفروا به … فأعرض عنهم وعظهم وقل لهم في أنفسهم قولا بليغا

*A-lam tara ila'lladhīna yazʿumūna annahum āmanū bimā unzila ilayka wa mā unzila min qablika yurīdūna an yataḥākamū ila'l-ṭāghūti wa qad umirū an yakfurū bih … Fa-aʿriḍ ʿanhum wa ʿiẓhum wa qul lahum fī anfusihim qawlan balīghā*

"Have you not seen those who claim they believe in what was sent down to you and in what was sent down before you? They want to take their disputes to *ṭāghūt*, though they were commanded to reject it… Turn away from them, admonish them, and speak to them, about themselves, a word that reaches home."

### Immediately after the fifty-ninth verse

**The position of the block must be recorded: the fifty-ninth verse said where a dispute is to be taken; the sixtieth takes as its subject its being taken somewhere else.**

***Yataḥākamū* — the root ح-ك-م, Form VI: for two parties to resort to someone's judgement.** **The word is from the same root as *ḥakamtum* in the fifty-ninth verse and *ḥakam* in the thirty-fifth.**

| Verse | Word | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *Ḥakaman … wa ḥakaman* | Two arbitrators from within the family |
| **58** | *Ḥakamtum … taḥkumū* | Those who judge |
| **59** | *Fa-ruddūhu* | The authority for a dispute |
| **60** | *Yataḥākamū ila'l-ṭāghūt* | **Another authority** |

**The same root in four verses, in four different positions.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

### يَزْعُمُونَ — the choice of verb

**The root ز-ع-م: to assert something without certain knowledge.** **The lexicographers record that the word is mostly used for "a claim without foundation".**

**And the choice of the verb produces a record: the verse does not say "they did not believe", it says "they *claim* that they believe".** What is at issue, that is, is the distance between **the claim** and **the conduct**.

**This is the same field treated at `063-munafikun.md` 63/1, and I rely on that treatment:** there too the matter was framed not as the truth of the statement but as **the speaker's relation to what he says**.

### قَوْلًا بَلِيغًا — the analysis of the word

**The root ب-ل-غ: to reach a place, to arrive.** *Bulūgh* — arriving; *balāgh* — conveying, delivery.

***Balīgh* — "a word that arrives".** **The verse, that is, qualifies the word not by its beauty but by where it arrives.**

**And the place of the qualification must be recorded: *wa qul lahum fī anfusihim* — "about themselves".** The word is spoken not in public but to the person himself.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the two qualifications:** the verse asks both that the word **reach the one addressed** and that it be said **to him himself**.

---

## 4/64-70 — وما أرسلنا من رسول إلا ليطاع بإذن الله … فلا وربك لا يؤمنون حتى يحكموك فيما شجر بينهم ثم لا يجدوا في أنفسهم حرجا مما قضيت ويسلموا تسليما … ومن يطع الله والرسول فأولئك مع الذين أنعم الله عليهم من النبيين والصديقين والشهداء والصالحين وحسن أولئك رفيقا

*Wa mā arsalnā min rasūlin illā li-yuṭāʿa bi-idhni'llāh … Fa-lā wa rabbika lā yuʾminūna ḥattā yuḥakkimūka fīmā shajara baynahum thumma lā yajidū fī anfusihim ḥarajan mimmā qaḍayta wa yusallimū taslīmā … Wa man yuṭiʿi'llāha wa'l-rasūla fa-ulāʾika maʿa'lladhīna anʿama'llāhu ʿalayhim mina'l-nabiyyīna wa'l-ṣiddīqīna wa'l-shuhadāʾi wa'l-ṣāliḥīn, wa ḥasuna ulāʾika rafīqā*

"We sent no messenger except that he should be obeyed, by God's leave… No, by your Lord, they do not believe until they make you the judge in what has branched out between them, and then find in themselves no constraint over what you have decided, and submit in full submission… Those who obey God and the Messenger are with those whom God has favoured: the prophets, the *ṣiddīq*s, the witnesses and the righteous. How good a company they are!"

### شَجَرَ — the picture in the word

**The root ش-ج-ر: a tree.** And in its verbal form *shajara* — **to branch out, to become intertwined.**

**So the expression *fīmā shajara baynahum* means literally "in what has branched out between them".** **The word draws the picture of a dispute branching and tangling like a tree.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** the verse describes a disagreement not as a simple opposition but as a situation whose **branches have grown into one another** — and for exactly that reason an arbitrator from outside is needed.

**The same logic was present in the thirty-fifth verse: there too *shiqāq* (a splitting) required an arrangement of arbitration.** **The two verses propose the same remedy at two different scales.**

### حَرَجًا — the root ح-ر-ج

**The concrete sense of the root: dense woodland, a place hard to pass through.** From there comes the sense of **narrowness, constraint**.

**And the coming together of the two words is worth recording: *shajara* (to branch out) and *ḥaraj* (dense woodland).** **Both words come from vegetation, and they are in the same verse.** This is a countable fact of language.

### The three steps the verse lays down

**The sixty-fifth verse describes submission in three stages, and the sequence must be recorded:**

| Step | Wording | Where it takes place |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yuḥakkimūka* | **Outside** — accepting an arbitrator |
| **2** | *Thumma lā yajidū fī **anfusihim** ḥarajā* | **Inside** — feeling no constraint |
| **3** | *Wa yusallimū taslīmā* | Reinforced by the absolute object |

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse does not count outward acceptance as sufficient, and ties the second step plainly to **the inside** (*fī anfusihim*).

### مِنَ ٱلنَّبِيِّۦنَ وَٱلصِّدِّيقِينَ وَٱلشُّهَدَآءِ وَٱلصَّٰلِحِينَ — four classes

**The sixty-ninth verse counts four classes, and the sequence must be recorded.**

| Class | Root | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| *Al-nabiyyūn* | ن-ب-أ | Those who bring news |
| *Al-ṣiddīqūn* | ص-د-ق | **Intensity in truthfulness** — the *fiʿʿīl* pattern |
| *Al-shuhadāʾ* | ش-ه-د | Those who bear witness |
| *Al-ṣāliḥūn* | ص-ل-ح | Those who set right / those who are sound |

**And what the verse gives is not a *rank* but a *companionship*: *maʿa'lladhīna anʿama'llāhu ʿalayhim*.** The sentence does not say "they become of them", it says "they are with them".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the preposition *maʿa*.**

**وَحَسُنَ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ رَفِيقًا** — *rafīq*: root ر-ف-ق, **gentleness, ease; and companionship on the road.** From the same root comes *rifq* (to act gently).

**And the choice of the word coincides with the thirty-sixth verse:** there *al-ṣāḥib bi'l-janb* (the companion at one's side) had been taken into the list. **The sūra uses the word for companionship at two different scales.**

---

## 4/71-74 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا خذوا حذركم فانفروا ثبات أو انفروا جميعا · وإن منكم لمن ليبطئن … فليقاتل في سبيل الله الذين يشرون الحياة الدنيا بالآخرة

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū khudhū ḥidhrakum fa'nfirū thubātin awi'nfirū jamīʿā · Wa inna minkum la-man la-yubaṭṭiʾann … Fa'l-yuqātil fī sabīli'llāhi'lladhīna yashrūna'l-ḥayāta'l-dunyā bi'l-ākhira*

"O you who believe! Take your precautions; go out in companies or go out all together. Among you there is one who holds back… Let those who sell the life of this world for the hereafter fight in the way of God."

### حِذْر — the analysis of the word

**The root ح-ذ-ر: to guard against a harm, to take precaution.**

**And the form of the command must be recorded: *khudhū ḥidhrakum* — "*take* your precautions".** The verb gives precaution as **something to be taken up**.

**The word will occur once more, in this block and in the hundred and second verse (the prayer of fear).** Both occurrences concern a state of war.

### لَيُبَطِّئَنَّ — the one who holds back

**The root ب-ط-أ: to be slow, to lag.** **Form II (*baṭṭaʾa*) states transitivity and intensity.**

**And how the verse describes this group must be recorded: no name is given, a verb is given.** *La-man la-yubaṭṭiʾann* — "one who holds back". **The description is tied not to a person but to a behaviour.**

**This accords with the method's rule about attributes and is the line maintained throughout this commentary.**

### يَشْرُونَ ٱلْحَيَوٰةَ ٱلدُّنْيَا بِٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ — the word's two directions

**In Arabic the root ش-ر-ي is one of the *aḍdād*: it states both selling and buying.**

**And which direction it is used in here is known from the sentence's particle *bi-*:** in Arabic the price comes with *bi-*; here the price is *al-ākhira*. **What is given, that is, is this world, and what is taken is the hereafter.**

**The word occurred at the forty-fourth verse as well: *yashtarūna'l-ḍalālata* — "they buy error".** **The same root, in two opposite directions.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 4/75-76 — وما لكم لا تقاتلون في سبيل الله والمستضعفين من الرجال والنساء والولدان الذين يقولون ربنا أخرجنا من هذه القرية الظالم أهلها واجعل لنا من لدنك وليا واجعل لنا من لدنك نصيرا

*Wa mā lakum lā tuqātilūna fī sabīli'llāhi wa'l-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildāni'lladhīna yaqūlūna rabbanā akhrijnā min hādhihi'l-qaryati'l-ẓālimi ahluhā wa'jʿal lanā min ladunka waliyyan wa'jʿal lanā min ladunka naṣīrā*

"What is the matter with you that you do not fight in the way of God, and for the men, women and children who have been made weak, who say: 'Our Lord! Bring us out of this town whose people are oppressors; give us from Yourself one to take charge of us, and give us from Yourself a helper.'"

### مُسْتَضْعَفِين — the analysis of the word and two grounds

**The root ض-ع-ف, Form X (*istifʿāl*): to reckon someone weak and to make him weak.**

**What the pattern carries must be recorded, and this was treated in detail at `028-kasas.md` 28/4-5. The record made there was this:**

> *"**يَسْتَضْعِفُ** — root **ض-ع-ف**, Form X: to reckon someone weak and to make him weak. **The pattern matters: weakness is not something that happens of itself but something that is *done*.**"*

**And one further observation was recorded there:**

> *"The verse describes what Pharaoh did with three verbs, and the first of them is dividing… **That is, oppression does not begin with direct force; it begins by breaking the community into pieces** — and only in a divided community can one section be set apart and made *mustaḍʿaf*."*

**I rely on both records and do not repeat them.**

**The second ground is `008-enfal.md` 8/26. What was recorded there:**

> *"**مُّسْتَضْعَفُون** — Form X: those who are seen as weak and made weak… **And in al-Anfāl the same fear is named in the *past tense*: as a state now over.**"*

**Setting the three verses side by side gives the word's three positions in the Qurʾān:**

| Place | Who | Tense | What is said |
|---|---|---|---|
| `028-kasas.md` 28/4-5 | A community | **Ongoing** — *yastaḍʿifu* | The reversal of the position is promised |
| `008-enfal.md` 8/26 | Those addressed | **Past** — *kuntum … mustaḍʿafūn* | A reminder |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/75** | *Mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildān* | **Present** | **Named as the ground for war** |

**The three verses use the same pattern, and the three stand in three different tenses.** This is a weave across three sūras that can be verified.

### The threefold enumeration itself

**Having said *al-mustaḍʿafīn*, the verse counts three items: *mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildān*.**

**And the enumeration itself produces a record: the word is not left standing alone, it is opened up.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the line established in the sūra's introductory section:** the sūra names the one without protection not as **an abstract category** but as **enumerated items** — like the nine addresses in the thirty-sixth verse.

**And the word *al-wildān* must be recorded: from the root و-ل-د, children.** **The sūra will use this word once more at the hundred and twenty-seventh verse: *wa'l-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-wildān*.**

### ٱلْقَرْيَةِ ٱلظَّالِمِ أَهْلُهَا — a detail of word order

**The construction means literally "the town whose people are oppressors".**

**And the word order must be recorded: the description is attached not to the town but to *its people*.** The sentence does not say *al-qaryati'l-ẓālima* (the oppressive town); it says *al-ẓālimi **ahluhā***.

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the wrong is ascribed not to a place but **to the acts of the people in that place**.

**And this accords with the method's rule about attributes: the ruling is tied not to a place or to a whole community but to an act.**

### وَلِيًّا … نَصِيرًا — the two words of the prayer

**The prayer asks for two things, and both had occurred earlier in the sūra:** in the forty-fifth verse, *wa kafā bi'llāhi waliyyan wa kafā bi'llāhi naṣīrā*.

| Verse | Sentence | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **45** | *Wa kafā bi'llāhi **waliyyan** wa kafā bi'llāhi **naṣīrā*** | The text — a statement |
| **75** | *Wa'jʿal lanā min ladunka **waliyyan** wa'jʿal lanā min ladunka **naṣīrā*** | **Those made weak — a prayer** |

**The same two words, thirty verses apart: one a statement, the other a request.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra, and **I record it as my own reading:** the text first states the sufficiency; then it relates the voice that asks for that sufficiency.

---

## 4/77 — ألم تر إلى الذين قيل لهم كفوا أيديكم وأقيموا الصلاة وآتوا الزكاة فلما كتب عليهم القتال إذا فريق منهم يخشون الناس كخشية الله أو أشد خشية

*A-lam tara ila'lladhīna qīla lahum kuffū aydiyakum wa aqīmu'ṣ-ṣalāta wa ātu'l-zakāh, fa-lammā kutiba ʿalayhimu'l-qitālu idhā farīqun minhum yakhshawna'l-nāsa ka-khashyati'llāhi aw ashadda khashyah*

"Have you not seen those to whom it was said, 'hold back your hands, keep up the prayer and give the alms'? When fighting was prescribed for them, a group of them began to fear people as they fear God, or with a greater fear."

### كَفُّوٓا۟ أَيْدِيَكُمْ — the sequence itself

**The verse records a sequence: first *holding back the hands* was commanded, then fighting was prescribed.**

**And this can be recorded not as a piece of historical information but as the verse's own sentence:** the text says the two commands **were not given at the same time**.

**It is widely transmitted in the classical commentaries that this is tied to the difference between the Meccan period and the Medinan.** **I relate this explanation as it is transmitted.**

### خَشْيَة — and its second occurrence in the sūra

**The word was treated at the ninth verse of this sūra** (under the heading "the difference between خَشْيَة and خَوْف") **and I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is this: the verse sets up a *comparison*.**

| What is compared | Wording |
|---|---|
| Fear of people | *Yakhshawna'l-nās* |
| The measure | *Ka-khashyati'llāh* — as they fear God |
| And beyond it | *Aw **ashadda** khashyah* — **or with a greater fear** |

**The presence of the second qualification must be recorded:** the verse does not stop at equality; it writes down **the possibility of exceeding** it as well.

**قُلْ مَتَٰعُ ٱلدُّنْيَا قَلِيلٌ وَٱلْـَٔاخِرَةُ خَيْرٌ لِّمَنِ ٱتَّقَىٰ وَلَا تُظْلَمُونَ فَتِيلًا** — *fatīl*: **the thin thread in the groove of a date-stone.** The word is used to state the smallest amount.

**And it does the same work as *mithqāla dharra* in the fortieth verse.** **The sūra states the absence of injustice twice, with two separate small measures.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 4/78-79 — أينما تكونوا يدرككم الموت ولو كنتم في بروج مشيدة وإن تصبهم حسنة يقولوا هذه من عند الله وإن تصبهم سيئة يقولوا هذه من عندك قل كل من عند الله … · ما أصابك من حسنة فمن الله وما أصابك من سيئة فمن نفسك وأرسلناك للناس رسولا وكفى بالله شهيدا

*Aynamā takūnū yudrikkumu'l-mawtu wa law kuntum fī burūjin mushayyada, wa in tuṣibhum ḥasanatun yaqūlū hādhihī min ʿindi'llāh, wa in tuṣibhum sayyiʾatun yaqūlū hādhihī min ʿindik, qul kullun min ʿindi'llāh, fa-mā li-hāʾulāʾi'l-qawmi lā yakādūna yafqahūna ḥadīthā · Mā aṣābaka min ḥasanatin fa-mina'llāh, wa mā aṣābaka min sayyiʾatin fa-min nafsik, wa arsalnāka li'l-nāsi rasūlā, wa kafā bi'llāhi shahīdā*

"Wherever you may be, death will overtake you — even were you in towers built strong. If some good befalls them they say 'this is from God'; if some ill befalls them they say 'this is from you'. Say: all is from God. What is the matter with these people that they barely understand a word? — Whatever good befalls you is from God; whatever ill befalls you is from yourself. We sent you to people as a messenger. God suffices as a witness."

### First, a note on method

**There is an apparent tension between these two verses, and the tension must not be concealed. But two things are not done in this section:**

**One. No theological polemic is entered into.** These two verses have been used in the history of classical theology in the debate over destiny and the creation of human acts. **This commentary takes no side in that debate and does not relate the objections the parties direct at one another.** What is treated are the explanations that follow from the wording of the verses.

**Two. No preference is imposed.** The disagreement is given as a table, and none of it is binding.

### One: what the tension is — written plainly

| Verse | Sentence | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *Qul **kullun** min ʿindi'llāh* | **All** is from God |
| **79** | *Wa mā aṣābaka min sayyiʾatin **fa-min nafsik*** | Ill is **from yourself** |

**The two sentences come one after the other, and at first sight they look as though they answer each other back.**

### Two: first the context — what the seventy-eighth verse is answering

**And before resolving the tension, what the seventy-eighth verse says and to whom must be established, because this is the point most often skipped.**

**The verse relates an *utterance*:**

| Case | What is said |
|---|---|
| *In tuṣibhum **ḥasana*** | *Hādhihī min ʿindi'llāh* — this is from God |
| *In tuṣibhum **sayyiʾa*** | *Hādhihī **min ʿindik*** — **this is from you** |

**The answer *kullun min ʿindi'llāh* in the seventy-eighth verse, that is, comes not in order to lay down a general law of cause and effect but in order to reject a particular *ascription*:** the saying that lays the ill that has befallen one upon a person.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the sentence begins with *qul* (say):** the sentence is an answer, and the party addressed by the answer are those who said the words related.

### Three: the difference of pronoun between the two verses — a countable fact

| | **4/78** | **4/79** |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Tuṣib**hum*** — third person plural | *Aṣāba**ka*** — **second person singular** |
| **Who is being spoken of** | The group described | **The one directly addressed** |
| **The end of the sentence** | *Lā yakādūna yafqahūna ḥadīthā* | ***Wa arsalnāka li'l-nāsi rasūlā*** |

**The two verses use the same words (*ḥasana*, *sayyiʾa*) but their pronouns differ.** This is a countable fact.

**And the second half of the seventy-ninth verse must be recorded separately, because it is most often dropped:** *wa arsalnāka li'l-nāsi rasūlā* — "we sent you to people as a messenger."

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the continuation of the verse:** the place where the sentence stands is not a context debating the source of an ill but a context stating **the position of the messenger**. In the seventy-eighth verse it had been said "this is from you"; the seventy-ninth verse lifts that ascription and says what the messenger is.

### Four: a table of the disagreement — the classical explanations

**Different tendencies are transmitted in the classical commentaries on how the two verses are to be reconciled. I put them in a table; I impose no preference and none of them is binding.**

| Tendency | How it reconciles them | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **The two verses speak from two different sides:** 78 in respect of *creation and decree* ("everything is from God"), 79 in respect of *ascription and acquisition* ("an ill is ascribed to a person's own act"). This explanation is the one most widely transmitted in the classical commentaries | The difference in the word patterns of the two verses: 78 *min ʿindi'llāh* (source), 79 *min nafsik* (ascription) |
| **B** | **The words do not answer to the same thing in the two verses:** the *ḥasana/sayyiʾa* of 78 are abundance and hardship (favour and affliction), while those of 79 are good and bad conduct | That the verb *tuṣibhum* is used for what comes from outside; that the one addressed in 79 is singular |
| **C** | **79 is the lifting of the ascription made in 78:** the sentence lays down no law, it rejects the words "this is from you" and gives the responsibility back to the speaker | That the verse continues with *wa arsalnāka li'l-nāsi rasūlā* |
| **D** | **78 is general, 79 particular:** 78 states the ultimate source of all that happens, 79 what falls to a person's own share; the two are different layers | The generality of the word *kullun* (all); the singularity of the pronoun *ka* in 79 |

**All four tendencies have been defended in the classical literature. I prefer none of them.**

### Five: the barrier against generalising at `042-sura.md` 42/30 — carried over here

**And now comes the most important record in this discussion. The Qurʾān builds another verse in the same field, and there a *limit* is set.**

> **وَمَآ أَصَٰبَكُم مِّن مُّصِيبَةٍ فَبِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِيكُمْ وَيَعْفُوا۟ عَن كَثِيرٍ**
> *"Whatever affliction strikes you is for what your own hands have earned. **And He pardons much.**"* (al-Shūrā 42/30)

**This verse was treated in detail at `042-sura.md` 42/30, and the barrier set there must be carried over here as it stands. The records made there:**

> *"**The following generalisation cannot be drawn from the verse: 'every affliction is the return for a sin; therefore a person to whom something happens is in that state on account of his sin.'**… **First: the verse itself sets a *proportion*.** The sentence *wa yaʿfū ʿan kathīr* says that there is **no** one-to-one correspondence between what comes and what is earned."*

> *"**Fourth: whom the address is to.** The verse says *aṣāba**kum*** — second person plural. **Those addressed, that is, are a particular community, and the sentence is said to them.** It is not a structure that can be used for one person to pass a verdict about another."*

> *"The verse opens a door for a person to take account of **his own** condition. But this door is **one-way**… **To ask the same question about someone else does not follow from the wording of the verse.**"*

**This barrier holds for al-Nisāʾ 4/79 too, and its ground is the verse's own pronoun: *aṣābaka* — second person singular.**

**The sentence, that is, is said to the one addressed about himself.** For a person to say, of what has befallen someone else, "this is from himself" **does not follow from the wording of this verse.**

**I record this separately and plainly, because the method's rules against scolding the reader and against sweeping rulings are at work here.**

### Six: أَيْنَمَا تَكُونُوا۟ يُدْرِككُّمُ ٱلْمَوْتُ — the block's opening

**And the block's first sentence sets the frame for the whole discussion.**

***Yudrikkum* — root د-ر-ك: to catch up with, to reach, to overtake.** *Idrāk* — arriving at something; and from there, grasping it. **The concrete sense of the verb is catching up from behind.**

***Burūjin mushayyada*** — *burj*: **root ب-ر-ج: to be visible, to be out in the open.** *Tabarruj* — to make visible. **A *burj*, then, is a high structure seen from afar.**

***Mushayyada* — root ش-ي-د: to raise, to build firmly.** *Shīd* — lime, mortar. **The word states that the structure has been reinforced.**

**And the use of the two words together builds a picture: both high and reinforced.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the particle *wa law*:** the sentence gives the most sheltered case as its example and says that **even so** the outcome does not change. **The verse, that is, states not the impossibility of protection but the *limit* of protection.**

**And the position of the block needs to be recorded:** this sentence comes immediately after the verses that take as their subject holding back from war (77). **In the seventy-seventh verse people were feared; the seventy-eighth verse says how far the thing feared can protect anyone.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The saying the seventy-eighth verse relates — laying good to a source and ill to a person — describes a habit of thought. In rejecting this habit the verse **does not accuse the person**; it corrects the direction of the ascription.

**Two.** The qualification *min nafsik* in the seventy-ninth verse is a sentence framed for **a person's taking account of himself**. As was recorded in the discussion of `042-sura.md` 42/30: *"to say to a person in pain 'this is from what you did' is not consolation but the adding of a burden."* **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 4/80-81 — من يطع الرسول فقد أطاع الله ومن تولى فما أرسلناك عليهم حفيظا · ويقولون طاعة فإذا برزوا من عندك بيت طائفة منهم غير الذي تقول والله يكتب ما يبيتون فأعرض عنهم وتوكل على الله وكفى بالله وكيلا

*Man yuṭiʿi'l-rasūla fa-qad aṭāʿa'llāh, wa man tawallā fa-mā arsalnāka ʿalayhim ḥafīẓā · Wa yaqūlūna ṭāʿatun fa-idhā barazū min ʿindika bayyata ṭāʾifatun minhum ghayra'lladhī taqūl, wa'llāhu yaktubu mā yubayyitūn, fa-aʿriḍ ʿanhum wa tawakkal ʿala'llāh, wa kafā bi'llāhi Wakīlā*

"Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed God. And whoever turns away — We did not send you as a keeper over them. They say 'we obey'; but when they leave your presence, a group of them plot by night something other than what you say. God records what they plot by night. So turn away from them and rely on God. God suffices as a trustee."

### فَمَآ أَرْسَلْنَٰكَ عَلَيْهِمْ حَفِيظًا — the setting of a limit

***Ḥafīẓ* — root ح-ف-ظ: to protect, to watch over; and to keep guard.** The root had occurred at the thirty-fourth verse too (*ḥāfiẓātun li'l-ghayb*).

**And the function of the sentence must be recorded: immediately after stating obedience, the verse sets a *limit*.** The messenger's task is not to make people obey but to convey.

**The same limit was treated at `006-enam.md` 6/69** (*wa mā ʿala'lladhīna yattaqūna min ḥisābihim min shayʾ*) **and it was recorded there: the burden of calling to account is lifted. I rely on that treatment.**

### بَيَّتَ — the picture in the word

**The root ب-ي-ت: to pass the night, to spend the night.** *Bayt* — the place where one passes the night, a house. **And *bayyata* (Form II): to plan a thing by night, to devise it.**

**What the word carries is worth recording: the verb qualifies the devising *by time*** — the difference between what is said by day and what is contrived by night.

**And the word order of the verse establishes this plainly:**

| Time | What happens |
|---|---|
| **While with you** | *Yaqūlūna ṭāʿatun* — "we obey" |
| ***Idhā barazū min ʿindik*** | Once they have left |
| **By night** | *Bayyata … ghayra'lladhī taqūl* — **they contrive something else** |

***Barazū* — root ب-ر-ز: to come out into the open, to appear.** **The choice of the word must also be recorded: the departure is given with the verb "to come out".**

**This field was treated in detail in `063-munafikun.md`, and I rely on that treatment:** there too the matter was framed as **the distance between the word and the one who speaks it**.

**And a record per the method (`USLUP.md`): what is described here is an *attribute*.** The verse names a group (*ṭāʾifatun minhum*) and describes it by a behaviour. **Whoever does that thing falls within the description; this is not a ruling about a group.**

---

## 4/82 — أفلا يتدبرون القرآن ولو كان من عند غير الله لوجدوا فيه اختلافا كثيرا

*Afa-lā yatadabbarūna'l-Qurʾān, wa law kāna min ʿindi ghayri'llāhi la-wajadū fīhi'khtilāfan kathīrā*

"Do they not give the Qurʾān *tadabbur*? Had it been from other than God, they would have found in it much inconsistency."

### تَدَبُّر — `047-muhammed.md` 47/24 and `038-sad.md` 38/29

**The root د-ب-ر was treated in detail at `047-muhammed.md` 47/24. I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here. A summary of the records made there:**

> *"**Root: د-ب-ر.** And the concrete sense of the root carries the whole weight of the word: **the back, the back of a thing.**… **تَدَبُّر (*tadabbur*): to get behind a thing, to think of its end.**"*

> *"**تَدَبَّرَ** — the *tafaʿʿul* form… **That is, *tadabbur* is not something that happens at a glance.** The pattern of the word says that the work **takes time** and **requires labour**."*

> *"**The result: *tadabbur* is to advance step by step towards the back of a text, its end, the place it arrives at.** And this is different from 'reading'."*

**The second ground is `038-sad.md` 38/29, and there the word was given as a *ground*:**

> *"**And Ṣād 38/29 is striking:** there *tadabbur* is given as the **ground of the Book's being sent down** — *li-yaddabbarū*. The text, that is, ties its own reason for existing to this verb."*

**Now the three verses must be set side by side, because the same verb comes in three different patterns:**

| Place | Wording | Pattern | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `038-sad.md` **38/29** | *Li-yaddabbarū āyātih* | **Causal** — a ground | The **reason** for its being sent down |
| `047-muhammed.md` **47/24** | *Afa-lā yatadabbarūna'l-Qurʾān* | **A question** | The **diagnosis** that it is not being done |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/82** | *Afa-lā yatadabbarūna'l-Qurʾān* | **A question + a proof** | A **measure** is added to the question |

**And it must be recorded that the first half of the sentence in al-Nisāʾ and in Muḥammad is word for word identical:** *afa-lā yatadabbarūna'l-Qurʾān*. **What changes is the second half of the sentence.**

| Sūra | Second half |
|---|---|
| **Muḥammad 47/24** | *Am ʿalā qulūbin aqfāluhā* — **or are there locks upon the hearts** |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/82** | *Wa law kāna min ʿindi ghayri'llāhi la-wajadū fīhi'khtilāfan kathīrā* — **a measure is given** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording of the two verses:** in Sūrat Muḥammad the question asks **where the obstacle is**; in al-Nisāʾ the question says **what is to be looked for**.

### ٱخْتِلَٰفًا كَثِيرًا — the form of the measure

**The root خ-ل-ف: to come after a thing, to take its place.** *Khalaf* — the one who comes after; *ikhtilāf* (Form VIII) — to go one after another, to fail to agree with one another.

**And the measure the verse gives must be recorded: the sentence does not say *ikhtilāfan* but *ikhtilāfan kathīrā* — "much inconsistency".**

**This is a qualification that must be read carefully, and I give it as an observation about word order:** the verse says not that no apparent tension whatever will be found, but that **no numerous and irremovable inconsistency** will be found. The presence of the adjective shows that the measure is not absolute but **quantitative**.

**And how this measure is to be worked is tied to the verse's own verb: *tadabbur*.** **That is, as the verse gives the measure, so it gives in the same sentence the method by which the measure is to be applied.**

### The verse's place within the sūra

**And where the verse stands must be recorded, because it is most often skipped.**

**This verse stands not in the middle of a debate about a text but in the middle of a discussion about *news and trust*:** in the eighty-first verse words contrived by night were named; in the eighty-third the spreading of news will be named.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the verses:** read together, the three verses show a common subject among them — **looking behind what is said.** In the eighty-first verse there is something else behind the word; in the eighty-second it is asked that one get behind the text; in the eighty-third the verification of news is commanded.

---

## 4/83 — وإذا جاءهم أمر من الأمن أو الخوف أذاعوا به ولو ردوه إلى الرسول وإلى أولي الأمر منهم لعلمه الذين يستنبطونه منهم

*Wa idhā jāʾahum amrun mina'l-amni awi'l-khawfi adhāʿū bih, wa law raddūhu ila'l-rasūli wa ilā uli'l-amri minhum la-ʿalimahu'lladhīna yastanbiṭūnahū minhum*

"When news of security or of fear comes to them, they spread it about. Had they referred it to the Messenger and to those among them who hold command (*ulu'l-amr*), those among them who can draw out its inner sense would have known it."

### أَذَاعُوا۟ — the analysis of the word

**The root ذ-ي-ع: to spread, to be heard about.** *Adhāʿa* (Form IV) — to spread, to make known.

**And the object of the verb must be recorded: *amrun mina'l-amni awi'l-khawf* — "news of security or of fear".** **The verse counts both directions:** what is spread may be not only bad news but good news too.

**I record this as an observation about word order:** what is forbidden is not the **content** of the news but **its being spread without verification**.

### يَسْتَنۢبِطُونَهُۥ — the concrete sense of the word

**The root ن-ب-ط. And its concrete sense is the most striking side of this verse: *nabṭ* — the first water that comes up when a well is dug.**

***Istinbāṭ* (Form X) — to bring that water out; and from there, to draw a hidden sense out of an utterance.**

**The picture in the word must be recorded: knowledge is drawn not as something lying on the surface but as something brought up by digging.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** the verse names the understanding of the inner side of a report as **work that requires labour**. **And this is in the same direction as *tadabbur* one verse earlier:** both verbs state a going beneath the surface.

| Verse | Verb | The picture in the root |
|---|---|---|
| **82** | *Tadabbur* | To get **behind** a thing |
| **83** | *Istinbāṭ* | To bring a thing up **from the depth** |

**Two verses one after the other, and two verbs in the same direction.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

### أُو۟لِى ٱلْأَمْرِ — the second occurrence

**The word occurs for the second time in this sūra: at the fifty-ninth verse and the eighty-third.**

**And the difference between the two occurrences must be recorded:**

| Verse | Context | What is asked |
|---|---|---|
| **59** | A dispute | *Aṭīʿū* — obedience |
| **83** | **News** | *Raddūhu ilayhim* — **to refer it** |

**And the eighty-third verse qualifies the *ulu'l-amr* with an attribute: *alladhīna yastanbiṭūnahū minhum* — "those among them who can draw out its inner sense".**

**This was named in the table of disagreement at the fifty-ninth verse as one of the grounds of reading A. I rely on that, and here too I impose no preference.**

**But it must be recorded as a datum of the text:** in the second occurrence a **description of a task** is set beside the word.

---

## 4/84-87 — فقاتل في سبيل الله لا تكلف إلا نفسك وحرض المؤمنين · من يشفع شفاعة حسنة يكن له نصيب منها ومن يشفع شفاعة سيئة يكن له كفل منها · وإذا حييتم بتحية فحيوا بأحسن منها أو ردوها إن الله كان على كل شيء حسيبا · الله لا إله إلا هو ليجمعنكم إلى يوم القيامة لا ريب فيه

*Fa-qātil fī sabīli'llāh, lā tukallafu illā nafsak, wa ḥarriḍi'l-muʾminīn · Man yashfaʿ shafāʿatan ḥasanatan yakun lahū naṣībun minhā, wa man yashfaʿ shafāʿatan sayyiʾatan yakun lahū kiflun minhā … · Wa idhā ḥuyyītum bi-taḥiyyatin fa-ḥayyū bi-aḥsana minhā aw ruddūhā, inna'llāha kāna ʿalā kulli shayʾin Ḥasībā*

"Fight in the way of God; you are answerable only for yourself. And urge on those who believe… Whoever intercedes with a good intercession shall have a share of it; whoever intercedes with a bad intercession shall have a burden of it… When you are greeted with a greeting, answer with a finer one or return the same. God keeps count of all things."

### لَا تُكَلَّفُ إِلَّا نَفْسَكَ — the limit of responsibility

**The root ك-ل-ف: to take up a task with difficulty; and to lay a burden on someone.** *Kulfa* — trouble.

**And the function of the sentence must be recorded: immediately after a command, the *scope* of that command is narrowed.**

| Clause | What it does |
|---|---|
| *Fa-qātil fī sabīli'llāh* | **The command** |
| *Lā tukallafu illā nafsak* | **The limit** — responsibility does not pass beyond oneself |
| *Wa ḥarriḍi'l-muʾminīn* | **What remains** — urging on |

**Three clauses one after another, and the middle one separates the first from the third.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the clauses:** the verse lifts the burden of making others do what they ought and puts **urging on** in its place. **This is the same as the qualification *fa-mā arsalnāka ʿalayhim ḥafīẓā* in the eightieth verse.**

### شَفَاعَة — the root ش-ف-ع

**The concrete sense of the root: to make a thing double.** *Shafʿ* — even, paired; *watr* — odd, single. **So *shafāʿa* is the joining of a second party to the one who is alone.**

**And this concrete sense of the word explains the ruling the verse gives:** the one who intercedes becomes **the second party** to the business — and for that reason takes a share of the outcome.

**The verse gives the two returns with two separate words, and the difference must be recorded:**

| Intercession | Return | Word |
|---|---|---|
| *Shafāʿatan **ḥasana*** | *Naṣīb* | **A share** |
| *Shafāʿatan **sayyiʾa*** | *Kifl* | **A burden** |

***Kifl* — root ك-ف-ل: to take a thing upon oneself, to stand surety; and the saddle-cloth put on a camel's back.** **The concrete sense of the word is a *load*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two words:** for the good the verse chooses the word **share**, for the bad the word **burden**. **Two separate names are given to the same structure.**

**And the word *naṣīb* occurs here for the sixth time in this sūra** (twice in 7, twice in 32, 33, 85). **The word's distribution in the sūra will be set out in a table in the concluding section.**

### وَإِذَا حُيِّيتُم بِتَحِيَّةٍ — the reckoning of a greeting

**The root ح-ي-ي: life.** *Taḥiyya* — to wish life to someone; and from there, a greeting.

**And the measure the verse gives must be recorded, because it has two steps:**

| Step | Wording | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **Upper** | *Fa-ḥayyū bi-**aḥsana** minhā* | With a finer one |
| **Lower** | *Aw **ruddūhā*** | **At the least, with the same** |

**The verse, that is, lays down a floor: one does not fall below the greeting received.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the particle *aw* (or) in the sentence states an alternative, and both alternatives include answering. **Leaving it unanswered is not among the options.**

**And the verse-ending must be recorded: *inna'llāha kāna ʿalā kulli shayʾin Ḥasībā*.** This verse was included in the table of verse-endings set out in the sūra's introductory section. **A keeper of reckoning is set beside a small act of conduct.**

---

## 4/88-91 — فما لكم في المنافقين فئتين والله أركسهم بما كسبوا … إلا الذين يصلون إلى قوم بينكم وبينهم ميثاق أو جاءوكم حصرت صدورهم أن يقاتلوكم … فإن اعتزلوكم فلم يقاتلوكم وألقوا إليكم السلم فما جعل الله لكم عليهم سبيلا

*Fa-mā lakum fi'l-munāfiqīna fiʾatayni wa'llāhu arkasahum bimā kasabū … Illa'lladhīna yaṣilūna ilā qawmin baynakum wa baynahum mīthāqun aw jāʾūkum ḥaṣirat ṣudūruhum an yuqātilūkum … Fa-ini'tazalūkum fa-lam yuqātilūkum wa alqaw ilaykumu'l-salama fa-mā jaʿala'llāhu lakum ʿalayhim sabīlā*

"What is the matter with you, that you have split into two groups over the hypocrites? God has turned them back for what they earned… Except those who join a people with whom you have a treaty, or who come to you with hearts too constrained to fight you… If they keep away from you, do not fight you, and offer you peace, then God has given you no way against them."

### First, a note on method — the bringing forward of the exceptions

**This block is one of the places where the method's rule that "no sweeping ruling is made about a group" works most plainly. And the reason is not an interpretation but the verses' own wording: after giving a ruling, the block lays down three separate exceptions.**

| Order | Exception | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Those who take refuge with a people bound by treaty | *Illa'lladhīna yaṣilūna ilā qawmin baynakum wa baynahum mīthāq* |
| **2** | Those who come with hearts too constrained to fight | *Aw jāʾūkum ḥaṣirat ṣudūruhum* |
| **3** | **Those who keep away, do not fight, and offer peace** | *Fa-ini'tazalūkum fa-lam yuqātilūkum wa alqaw ilaykumu'l-salama* |

**And the outcome of the third exception is written out plainly: *fa-mā jaʿala'llāhu lakum ʿalayhim sabīlā* — "God has given you no way against them."**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the three rows above:** having laid down a ruling, the block counts out in detail **whom that ruling does not cover**. **The verses, that is, themselves block a sweeping generalisation.**

### أَرْكَسَهُم بِمَا كَسَبُوا۟ — the picture in the word

**The root ر-ك-س: to turn a thing upside down, to reverse it.** *Riks* — filth, a thing turned back.

**And the ground must be recorded: *bimā kasabū* — "for what they earned".**

**The root ك-س-ب had occurred in this sūra at the thirty-second verse (*iktasabū / iktasabna*) and was treated there. I rely on that treatment.** **The same root in two separate places, one stating a share, the other an outcome.**

**And how the same pattern (*bimā kasabat aydīkum*) is to be read was treated in detail at `042-sura.md` 42/30; the barrier against generalising set out there was carried over here in the discussion of 4/79. The same record holds here.**

### فِئَتَيْنِ — splitting in two

**The root ف-ي-أ: to turn, to come back.** *Fiʾa* — a company, a party.

**And the form of the sentence must be recorded: before giving a ruling, the verse asks a *question* — *fa-mā lakum* ("what is the matter with you?").**

**The same pattern of question was present at the seventy-fifth verse: *wa mā lakum lā tuqātilūna fī sabīli'llāh*.** **In both places the question takes as its subject the state of the one addressed.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

### حَصِرَتْ صُدُورُهُمْ — the analysis of the word

**The root ح-ص-ر: to encircle, to press in, to confine in a place.** *Ḥaṣr* — a besieging.

***Ḥaṣirat ṣudūruhum* — "their breasts constrained".** **And the function of the expression must be recorded: the verse describes an unwillingness to fight as an *inward state* and counts it a ground of exception.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the exception itself:** the verse lays down a qualification that looks not at the outwardly visible behaviour but at the state behind that behaviour.

---

## 4/92-93 — وما كان لمؤمن أن يقتل مؤمنا إلا خطأ ومن قتل مؤمنا خطأ فتحرير رقبة مؤمنة ودية مسلمة إلى أهله إلا أن يصدقوا … فمن لم يجد فصيام شهرين متتابعين توبة من الله · ومن يقتل مؤمنا متعمدا فجزاؤه جهنم خالدا فيها وغضب الله عليه ولعنه وأعد له عذابا عظيما

*Wa mā kāna li-muʾminin an yaqtula muʾminan illā khaṭaʾā, wa man qatala muʾminan khaṭaʾan fa-taḥrīru raqabatin muʾminatin wa diyatun musallamatun ilā ahlihī illā an yaṣṣaddaqū … fa-man lam yajid fa-ṣiyāmu shahrayni mutatābiʿayni tawbatan mina'llāh · Wa man yaqtul muʾminan mutaʿammidan fa-jazāʾuhū jahannamu khālidan fīhā wa ghaḍiba'llāhu ʿalayhi wa laʿanahū wa aʿadda lahū ʿadhāban ʿaẓīmā*

"It is not for a believer to kill a believer — except by mistake. Whoever kills a believer by mistake must free a believing slave and pay compensation delivered to his family, unless they remit it as a charity… Whoever cannot find the means shall fast two consecutive months, as a turning back granted by God. And whoever kills a believer deliberately, his recompense is Hell, to remain there; God has been angry with him, has cursed him, and has prepared for him a great punishment."

### First, a note on method

**From these two verses arose one of the widest discussions in classical jurisprudence: retaliation, blood-money, expiation, the kinds of intent and their conditions. That discussion is not entered into in this section and no legal ruling is issued.**

**What is done is to put in a table the gradations laid down by the verse's own wording.**

### The verse's own gradations — a table

**The ninety-second verse separates a single act (killing by mistake) into five different cases. The table gives the wording of the verse; it establishes no ruling.**

| Case | The wording in the verse | The return the verse counts |
|---|---|---|
| **The general case** | *Wa man qatala muʾminan khaṭaʾā* | *Taḥrīru raqabatin muʾmina* **and** *diyatun musallamatun ilā ahlihī* |
| **The family remits it** | *Illā an yaṣṣaddaqū* | The compensation lapses |
| **If he was of a people hostile to you and himself a believer** | *Fa-in kāna min qawmin ʿaduwwin lakum wa huwa muʾmin* | **Only** *taḥrīru raqabatin muʾmina* — no compensation is named |
| **If he was of a people with whom you have a treaty** | *Wa in kāna min qawmin baynakum wa baynahum mīthāq* | *Diyatun musallamatun ilā ahlihī* **and** *taḥrīru raqabatin muʾmina* |
| **One who cannot find the means** | *Fa-man lam yajid* | *Ṣiyāmu shahrayni mutatābiʿayn* |

**Two things follow from the table and are not in dispute:**

**One. The verse counts five separate cases for a single act.** This is a countable fact.

**Two. All the returns counted are in the verse's own wording**, and nothing outside the wording has been added to the table.

### دِيَة and تَحْرِير — the direction of the two words

***Diya* — root و-د-ي: to pay a sum, to give the equivalent.**

***Taḥrīr* — root ح-ر-ر: to set free, to make free.** *Ḥurr* — free.

**And the direction of the two returns must be recorded:**

| Return | To whom it goes |
|---|---|
| ***Diya*** | **To his family** — *ilā ahlihī* |
| ***Taḥrīru raqaba*** | **To a third person** — the one who gains his freedom |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the recipients of the two returns:** the verse does not confine the making good to the injured party alone; **it makes one person's freedom part of the return as well.**

**And the word's place in the sūra must be recorded:** *raqaba* (a slave) had been named in this sūra at the twenty-fifth and thirty-sixth verses too. **In all three occurrences the direction is the same: marriage, the list of kindness, and setting free.**

### إِلَّآ أَن يَصَّدَّقُوا۟ — the door of remission

***Taṣadduq*, from the root ص-د-ق: to give as a charitable gift.** **And the choice of the word must be recorded: the verse does not say "if they waive it", it says "if they remit it as a charity".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** to give up one's right is named in the verse with a verb of **giving**.

### تَوْبَةً مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ — the naming of the expiation

**The verse qualifies the option of fasting with one phrase: *tawbatan mina'llāh*.**

**And the direction of the particle *min* must be recorded: the turning back is *from* God.** **The root ت-و-ب: to turn back.** The root was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/37 and at the seventeenth and eighteenth verses of this sūra. **I rely on those treatments.**

### The ninety-third verse — deliberate killing

**The verse counts four things, and the enumeration must be recorded:**

| Order | Wording |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Jazāʾuhū jahannamu **khālidan fīhā*** |
| 2 | *Wa **ghaḍiba'llāhu** ʿalayh* |
| 3 | *Wa **laʿanahū*** |
| 4 | *Wa aʿadda lahū **ʿadhāban ʿaẓīmā*** |

**Four items in a single verse.** This is a countable fact, and an enumeration of this density is rare in the Qurʾān.

**And there is disagreement in the classical literature over the expression *khālidan fīhā*. I put it in a table; I impose no preference, I enter no theological debate, and none of it is binding.**

| Reading | How it understands it | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | It states perpetuity | The direct sense of the word |
| **B** | It states **a long time**; in Arabic *khulūd* is used both for absolute endlessness and for a long duration | The word's use in the language |
| **C** | The ruling holds for one who commits the act **while holding it lawful** | The qualification *wa yaghfiru mā dūna dhālika li-man yashāʾ* at 4/48 and 4/116 |
| **D** | The verse is a sentence of **threat**, and the force of sentences of threat does not exclude the possibility of pardon | The verses of forgiveness in the same sūra |

**All four readings are transmitted. I prefer none of them.**

**And what must be recorded as a datum of the text is this:** the same sūra says at the forty-eighth and hundred and sixteenth verses that everything other than associating partners with God may be forgiven. **These two data stand side by side in the text, and this commentary records both.**

### What this says to the present

**What the ninety-second verse does is to refuse to tie a single act to a single return.** For an act producing the same outcome, the verse takes separately into account the situation of the doer, the community to which the victim belonged, and the doer's means. **This is a structure visible in the wording of the verse.**

---

## 4/94-96 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا إذا ضربتم في سبيل الله فتبينوا ولا تقولوا لمن ألقى إليكم السلام لست مؤمنا تبتغون عرض الحياة الدنيا فعند الله مغانم كثيرة كذلك كنتم من قبل فمن الله عليكم فتبينوا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū idhā ḍarabtum fī sabīli'llāhi fa-tabayyanū wa lā taqūlū li-man alqā ilaykumu'l-salāma lasta muʾminan tabtaghūna ʿaraḍa'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā, fa-ʿinda'llāhi maghānimu kathīra, kadhālika kuntum min qablu fa-manna'llāhu ʿalaykum fa-tabayyanū*

"O you who believe! When you set out in the way of God, ascertain the facts. Do not say to one who offers you peace, seeking the passing gain of this world, 'you are not a believer'. With God there is abundant gain. You yourselves were like that before, and God bestowed His favour upon you. So ascertain the facts."

### فَتَبَيَّنُوا۟ — a command repeated twice

**The root ب-ي-ن: to separate two things, to bring to clarity.** *Bayn* — the space between; *tabayyun* (Form V) — **to wait for and to search out** the coming to light of a thing.

**And that the command occurs twice in the verse must be recorded: the sentence opens with *fa-tabayyanū* and closes with *fa-tabayyanū*.**

**This is a countable fact, and I record it as my own reading:** the verse places all the intervening grounds between the two commands. **The command to ascertain, that is, stands both before the grounds and after them.**

**The same root was treated at `049-hucurat.md` 49/6 (*in jāʾakum fāsiqun bi-nabaʾin fa-tabayyanū*), and there the discussion was the verification of news. I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | What is verified |
|---|---|
| `049-hucurat.md` 49/6 | **News** |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/94** | **The condition of the person opposite** |

**The two verses use the same verb on two separate subjects.**

### لَسْتَ مُؤْمِنًا — the forbidden sentence

**The verse relates directly a sentence that is forbidden to be spoken: *lasta muʾminan* — "you are not a believer".**

**And the ground of the prohibition is given right beside it: *tabtaghūna ʿaraḍa'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā*.**

***ʿAraḍ* — root ع-ر-ض: to come crosswise, to come up in front of one.** **The word means "what comes up in one's path, what is passing"**, and it is used as the opposite of *jawhar* (what endures).

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse says that behind a judgement passed about someone else there may lie **an interest** — and it sets this down as an indication that puts the validity of the judgement in question.

### كَذَٰلِكَ كُنتُم مِّن قَبْلُ — the weight of the sentence

**"You yourselves were like that before."**

**And where the sentence stands must be recorded: immediately after a prohibition against passing judgement.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the sentence:** by reminding the one addressed of **his own past**, the verse blocks his judging the person before him. **This is the same method treated at `008-enfal.md` 8/26** — there too, in the middle of a dispute over spoils, a past weakness was recalled. **I rely on that treatment.**

### غَيْرُ أُو۟لِى ٱلضَّرَرِ — the qualification in the ninety-fifth verse

**While comparing those who stay behind with those who strive, the verse lays down an exception: *ghayru uli'l-ḍarar* — "except those with an impediment".**

**The root ض-ر-ر: harm; and from there, an obstacle, an excuse.**

**And the presence of the exception must be recorded:** while stating a superiority, the verse **takes the one without means out of the comparison.**

**This will be done once more, and in more detail, in the ninety-seventh verse — there the place where an excuse is accepted will be counted out plainly.**

---

## 4/97-100 — إن الذين توفاهم الملائكة ظالمي أنفسهم قالوا فيم كنتم قالوا كنا مستضعفين في الأرض قالوا ألم تكن أرض الله واسعة فتهاجروا فيها … · إلا المستضعفين من الرجال والنساء والولدان لا يستطيعون حيلة ولا يهتدون سبيلا · فأولئك عسى الله أن يعفو عنهم … · ومن يهاجر في سبيل الله يجد في الأرض مراغما كثيرا وسعة

*Inna'lladhīna tawaffāhumu'l-malāʾikatu ẓālimī anfusihim qālū fīma kuntum, qālū kunnā mustaḍʿafīna fi'l-arḍ, qālū a-lam takun arḍu'llāhi wāsiʿatan fa-tuhājirū fīhā … · Illa'l-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildāni lā yastaṭīʿūna ḥīlatan wa lā yahtadūna sabīlā · Fa-ulāʾika ʿasa'llāhu an yaʿfuwa ʿanhum … · Wa man yuhājir fī sabīli'llāhi yajid fi'l-arḍi murāghaman kathīran wa saʿa*

"To those whose souls the angels take while they are wronging themselves, they say: 'What state were you in?' They say: 'We were made weak in the land.' They say: 'Was God's earth not wide, that you might have migrated in it?'… Except the men, women and children who were truly made weak, who could find no means and knew no way. It is these whom God may well pardon… Whoever migrates in the way of God will find in the earth many a place of refuge and abundance."

### The construction of the block — an excuse is rejected, then an excuse is accepted

**The most important side of this block is that it requires the two verses to be read together. And in this commentary the two are given together.**

| Verse | What happens | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **97** | The excuse *kunnā mustaḍʿafīna fi'l-arḍ* is **not accepted** | Those who could have gone and did not |
| **98** | *Illa'l-mustaḍʿafīn … lā yastaṭīʿūna ḥīlatan wa lā yahtadūna sabīlā* — **an exception** | Those who truly could find no means |
| **99** | *Fa-ulāʾika ʿasa'llāhu an yaʿfuwa ʿanhum* | The return for the exception |

**I bring this forward separately, because when the ninety-seventh verse is read on its own the measure the verse lays down is lost.**

**And the measure itself is in the wording of the ninety-eighth verse, and it has two items:**

| Item | Wording | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **One** | *Lā yastaṭīʿūna **ḥīlatan*** | To find no **means** |
| **Two** | *Wa lā yahtadūna **sabīlā*** | To know no **way** |

***Ḥīla* — root ح-و-ل: to turn, to pass from one state to another.** *Taḥawwul* — change; *ḥawl* — power, a turning. **So *ḥīla*, at the root of the word, is "the possibility of changing one's situation".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** the verse describes the excuse not as a matter of intention but as **whether or not the possibility of changing one's situation exists**.

**And the three items counted in the ninety-eighth verse — *al-rijāl*, *al-nisāʾ*, *al-wildān* — are word for word the same as in the seventy-fifth.**

| Verse | Wording | Function |
|---|---|---|
| **75** | *Al-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildān* | **The ground for war** |
| **98** | *Al-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-rijāli wa'l-nisāʾi wa'l-wildān* | **The place where an excuse is accepted** |

**The same threefold enumeration, in two separate functions.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sūra says, for the same group, both that they are to be **fought for** and that their **excuses are to be accepted**. **The two do not conflict: one looks at those outside, the other at the people themselves.**

### أَلَمْ تَكُنْ أَرْضُ ٱللَّهِ وَٰسِعَةً — three verses together

**"The wideness of the earth" occurs in three verses in the Qurʾān, and the three must be set side by side. The table compares the wording of the three verses.**

| | **al-Nisāʾ 4/97** | **`039-zumer.md` 39/10** | **`029-ankebut.md` 29/56** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Wording** | *A-lam takun **arḍu'llāhi** wāsiʿa* | *Wa **arḍu'llāhi** wāsiʿa* | *Inna **arḍī** wāsiʿa* |
| **Mood** | **A question** — *a-lam takun* | **A statement** | **A statement** |
| **Tense** | **Past** — *takun* | Present | Present |
| **The genitive** | *Arḍu'llāh* — God's earth | *Arḍu'llāh* | ***Arḍī*** — **My earth** |
| **Address** | Third person — it is being recounted | *Yā ʿibādi'lladhīna āmanū* | ***Yā ʿibādī*** — first person |
| **Continuation** | *Fa-tuhājirū fīhā* | *Innamā yuwaffa'l-ṣābirūna ajrahum bi-ghayri ḥisāb* | *Fa-iyyāya fa'ʿbudūn* |
| **Function** | **Interrogation** — a question asked afterwards | **Consolation** — a return for the one who endures | **A summons** — the command to serve |

**The difference between al-Zumar and al-ʿAnkabūt was already set out in a table in the discussion of `029-ankebut.md` 29/56, and it was recorded there:**

> *"The difference must be recorded: in al-Zumar 'God's earth', here 'My earth' — the first person. And the address is first person too: *yā ʿibādī* — 'O My servants.'"*

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it. What belongs here is the difference of the third verse — al-Nisāʾ's.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** in al-Zumar and al-ʿAnkabūt the sentence is a **summons**, spoken beforehand. In al-Nisāʾ the same sentence is a **question**, and it is built in the **past tense** — that is, it is asked after the matter is over.

**And the following observation recorded at `029-ankebut.md` 29/56-57 connects here too:**

> *"That is, changing one's place is not escaping death — it is choosing in what state one dies."*

**The verse in al-Nisāʾ stands at exactly this point: the sentence is spoken at the moment of death.**

### مُرَٰغَمًا كَثِيرًا وَسَعَةً — the analysis of the word

**The root ر-غ-م: the nose; and from there *raghām* — to rub the nose in the dust.** *Raghman* — unwillingly.

***Murāgham* — the lexicographers explain the word in two ways:** (a) a place to go to, a place of refuge; (b) a position that leaves the other party without recourse. **I relate both explanations; I impose no preference.**

**And the coming together of the two words must be recorded: *murāghaman kathīran wa saʿa*.**

***Saʿa* — root و-س-ع: wideness.** **And this word is from the same root as *wāsiʿa* in the ninety-seventh verse.**

| Verse | Word | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| **97** | *Wāsiʿa* | The wideness of **the earth** — as a question |
| **100** | *Saʿa* | The abundance **the migrant** will find — as a promise |

**The same root, three verses apart: one in an interrogation, the other in a return.** I record this as an observation about word order.

### وَمَن يَخْرُجْ مِنۢ بَيْتِهِۦ مُهَاجِرًا إِلَى ٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ ثُمَّ يُدْرِكْهُ ٱلْمَوْتُ

**The second half of the hundredth verse must be recorded: the person who sets out and dies before arriving.**

***Thumma yudrikhu'l-mawt* — and the verb is the same as the verb in the seventy-eighth verse: *yudrikkumu'l-mawt*.**

| Verse | Sentence | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *Aynamā takūnū **yudrikkum**u'l-mawt* | Everyone |
| **100** | *Thumma **yudrikhu**'l-mawt* | **The one who has set out** |

**The same verb, in two separate places.** And the verse states that the reward of the one who dies on the road **does not lapse**: *fa-qad waqaʿa ajruhū ʿala'llāh*.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb the two verses share:** the seventy-eighth verse said that death overtakes one everywhere; the hundredth says that **where it overtakes one does not change the reward**.

---

## 4/101-104 — وإذا ضربتم في الأرض فليس عليكم جناح أن تقصروا من الصلاة … · وإذا كنت فيهم فأقمت لهم الصلاة فلتقم طائفة منهم معك وليأخذوا أسلحتهم … · فإذا قضيتم الصلاة فاذكروا الله قياما وقعودا وعلى جنوبكم … إن الصلاة كانت على المؤمنين كتابا موقوتا · ولا تهنوا في ابتغاء القوم إن تكونوا تألمون فإنهم يألمون كما تألمون

*Wa idhā ḍarabtum fi'l-arḍi fa-laysa ʿalaykum junāḥun an taqṣurū mina'ṣ-ṣalāh … · Wa idhā kunta fīhim fa-aqamta lahumu'ṣ-ṣalāta fa'l-taqum ṭāʾifatun minhum maʿaka wa'l-yaʾkhudhū asliḥatahum … · Fa-idhā qaḍaytumu'ṣ-ṣalāta fa'dhkuru'llāha qiyāman wa quʿūdan wa ʿalā junūbikum … inna'ṣ-ṣalāta kānat ʿala'l-muʾminīna kitāban mawqūtā · Wa lā tahinū fi'btighāʾi'l-qawm, in takūnū taʾlamūna fa-innahum yaʾlamūna kamā taʾlamūn*

"When you journey in the land there is no fault upon you in shortening the prayer… When you are among them and lead them in prayer, let one group of them stand with you and take up their weapons… When you have finished the prayer, remember God standing, sitting and lying on your sides… Prayer is an obligation upon the believers at appointed times. And do not grow weak in seeking out the enemy. If you are suffering, they are suffering as you suffer."

### First, a note on method

**From these verses arose the discussions of classical jurisprudence on travel, the shortening of prayer and the forms of the prayer of fear. Those discussions are not entered into in this section and no legal ruling is issued.** What is treated is the **linguistic side** of the verses.

### كِتَٰبًا مَّوْقُوتًا — the analysis of the expression

**The root و-ق-ت: the appointed time of a task.** *Waqt* — a definite span of time; *mawqūt* — with its time appointed.

**And the place where the expression stands must be recorded: in the very middle of a context of war.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the verses:** the hundred and second verse shows that the **form** of prayer can change; the hundred and third says that its **time** is not lifted. **The block, that is, states two things at once: the form gives, the time stands.**

### قِيَٰمًا وَقُعُودًا وَعَلَىٰ جُنُوبِكُمْ — three postures

**The verse counts the remembrance to be made after prayer in three bodily postures:** standing, sitting and lying on one's side.

**And the root ق-و-م returns here once more.** The root's occurrences in this sūra will be set out in a table in the concluding section.

**The root ج-ن-ب also returns once more.** The root occurs at the thirty-first verse as *ijtanibū* (keep away), at the thirty-sixth as *al-junub* and *bi'l-janb* (the neighbour and the companion), and here as *junūbikum* (your sides). **The same root in three separate senses within the sūra.** This is a countable fact.

### إِن تَكُونُوا۟ تَأْلَمُونَ فَإِنَّهُمْ يَأْلَمُونَ كَمَا تَأْلَمُونَ — the weight of the sentence

**This sentence in the hundred and fourth verse is one of the most striking places in the sūra and must be recorded separately.**

**The root ا-ل-م: to feel pain.** *Alam* — pain.

**And what the sentence does must be recorded: in the middle of a command to fight, it is said that the other side is suffering too.**

**The sentence could have been built like this, but it is not:**

| It could have been | It is |
|---|---|
| You are suffering, but there is a reward for it | If you are suffering, **they too are suffering as you suffer** |

**The verb is repeated three times: *taʾlamūn*, *yaʾlamūn*, *taʾlamūn*.** And the third is set as a **measure** for the second: *kamā taʾlamūn*.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the repetition of the verb:** the sentence makes the one addressed measure the other side's pain by his own. **And what is given as the ground is not a piece of information but the addressee's own experience.**

**And the continuation of the sentence must be recorded: *wa tarjūna mina'llāhi mā lā yarjūn* — "and you hope from God what they do not hope."** **The verse, that is, sets the difference between the two sides not in the pain but in the hope.**

---

## 4/105-113 — إنا أنزلنا إليك الكتاب بالحق لتحكم بين الناس بما أراك الله ولا تكن للخائنين خصيما … · يستخفون من الناس ولا يستخفون من الله وهو معهم … · ومن يكسب خطيئة أو إثما ثم يرم به بريئا فقد احتمل بهتانا وإثما مبينا

*Innā anzalnā ilayka'l-kitāba bi'l-ḥaqqi li-taḥkuma bayna'l-nāsi bimā arāka'llāh, wa lā takun li'l-khāʾinīna khaṣīmā … · Yastakhfūna mina'l-nāsi wa lā yastakhfūna mina'llāhi wa huwa maʿahum … · Wa man yaksib khaṭīʾatan aw ithman thumma yarmi bihī barīʾan fa-qadi'ḥtamala buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā*

"We have sent down the Book to you with the truth, that you may judge between people by what God has shown you. Do not be an advocate for the treacherous… They hide themselves from people but do not hide themselves from God, and He is with them… Whoever commits a fault or a sin and then casts it upon an innocent person has taken upon himself a slander and a manifest sin."

### First, a note on method

**It is transmitted in the sources that this block points to a judicial episode. In this commentary the details of that report are not related and no name of a person is given.**

**The reason has two layers:**

**One. The verses themselves give no name at all.** *Al-khāʾinīn* (the treacherous), *alladhīna yakhtānūna anfusahum* (those who betray themselves), *ṭāʾifatun minhum* (a group of them), *barīʾan* (an innocent person) — **the text is nameless from beginning to end.**

**Two. The general stance of this commentary.** The same record was entered in the discussion of `024-nur.md` 24/11-20, and the ground was written there thus:

> *"**This very sūra takes as its subject what it is to talk about named persons.** For a section to be written by doing the very thing the verse it treats forbids would be an inconsistency."*

**The same ground holds here: the block takes as its subject the casting of an offence upon an innocent person.**

### وَلَا تَكُن لِّلْخَآئِنِينَ خَصِيمًا — the block's key sentence

***Khaṣīm* — root خ-ص-م: to contend with the other party in a suit.** *Khaṣm* — a party to a case; *khuṣūma* — contention. **And *khaṣīm* is on the intensive pattern: one who takes up a case and pleads it.**

**And the place where the sentence stands must be recorded: immediately after the command to judge.**

| Order | Clause | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Li-taḥkuma bayna'l-nāsi bimā arāka'llāh* | **The command** — judge |
| 2 | *Wa lā takun li'l-khāʾinīna khaṣīmā* | **The qualification** — do not take a side |
| 3 | *Wa lā tujādil ʿani'lladhīna yakhtānūna anfusahum* (107) | **The repetition of the qualification** — pleading |

**Three clauses in a single block, and two of them in the same direction.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the fifty-eighth verse:** there it said *idhā ḥakamtum bayna'l-nāsi an taḥkumū bi'l-ʿadl*. **The hundred and fifth verse repeats the same command and sets beside it the concrete place where justice breaks down: taking a side.**

**And the same field was treated at `005-maide.md` 5/8** (*wa lā yajrimannakum shanaʾānu qawmin ʿalā allā taʿdilū*) **and it was recorded there: hatred pushes a person either to do too much or to do too little. I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | What breaks justice | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| `005-maide.md` **5/8** | *Shanaʾān* — **hatred** | Against the other party |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/105-107** | *Khuṣūma* — **taking a side** | In favour of one's own party |

**The two verses take as their subject the breaking of justice from two different directions.** I record this as my own reading; its ground is the wording of the two verses.

### يَسْتَخْفُونَ مِنَ ٱلنَّاسِ وَلَا يَسْتَخْفُونَ مِنَ ٱللَّهِ — the symmetry of the sentence

**The root خ-ف-ي: to hide, to conceal.** Form X (*istakhfā*) — to try to hide oneself.

**And the sentence is built twice, with a single difference:**

| | The first | The second |
|---|---|---|
| **Verb** | *Yastakhfūn* | *Lā yastakhfūn* |
| **From whom** | *Mina'l-nās* | *Mina'llāh* |

**And the qualification at the end of the sentence breaks the symmetry: *wa huwa maʿahum* — "and He is with them".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the sentence says not that hiding is **impossible** but that the one hidden from **is already beside them**. **What is removed is not the act but the distance.**

### يَبِيتُونَ مَا لَا يَرْضَىٰ مِنَ ٱلْقَوْلِ — and the link with the eighty-first verse

**In the hundred and eighth verse the verb *yubayyitūn* occurs, and the verb is used for the second time in the sūra.**

| Verse | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **81** | *Bayyata ṭāʾifatun minhum ghayra'lladhī taqūl … wa'llāhu yaktubu mā **yubayyitūn*** |
| **108** | *Idh **yubayyitūna** mā lā yarḍā mina'l-qawl* |

**The same verb twice, twenty-seven verses apart.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra. **The picture in the word — contriving by night — is the same in both places.**

### وَمَن يَكْسِبْ خَطِيٓـَٔةً أَوْ إِثْمًا ثُمَّ يَرْمِ بِهِۦ بَرِيٓـًٔا — the centre of the block

**This verse is the heaviest sentence in the block and must be treated in detail.**

**First the order of the verbs must be recorded, because the verse describes a *process*:**

| Order | Verb | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yaksib khaṭīʾatan aw ithman* | The act is committed |
| **2** | *Thumma yarmi bihī barīʾan* | **It is cast upon an innocent person** |
| **3** | *Fa-qadi'ḥtamala buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā* | **Two burdens at once** are taken up |

**And it must be compared with the verse before it. The hundred and eleventh verse runs thus:**

> *Wa man yaksib ithman fa-innamā yaksibuhū **ʿalā nafsih*** — "Whoever earns a sin earns it only against himself." (4/111)

| Verse | Verb | Where the burden goes |
|---|---|---|
| **111** | *Yaksib ithman* | ***ʿAlā nafsih*** — upon himself |
| **112** | *Yaksib … thumma yarmi bihī barīʾan* | **It doubles** — *buhtānan **wa** ithman mubīnā* |

**Two verses one after the other, and the second adds one further item on top of the first.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording of the two verses:** when a sin stays upon oneself it is a single item; when it is cast upon another it becomes **two items** — the sin cast and the slander itself.

### بُهْتَٰن — the analysis of the word and `024-nur.md` 24/11-20

**The root ب-ه-ت: to astound, to shake to the point of stupefaction.** *Buhita* — he was struck dumb. **So *buhtān* is the slander that freezes the one it is aimed at: what is said is so far off that no answer can be made.**

**The word was given within a table in the discussion of `024-nur.md` 24/11-20, and I carry it over here:**

| Word | Root | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **كَذِب** (*kidhb*) | ك-ذ-ب | A statement that does not accord with the truth |
| **إِفْك** (*ifk*) | أ-ف-ك | What has been **turned upside down** — its direction reversed |
| **بُهْتَان** (*buhtān*) | ب-ه-ت | The slander that **freezes** the one it is aimed at |

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it.**

**And at `024-nur.md` 24/16 the same word occurs with an adjective: *hādhā buhtānun ʿaẓīm*.** **Two sūras use the same word on the same subject.**

| Place | Expression | Position |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/112** | *Fa-qadi'ḥtamala **buhtānan** wa ithman mubīnā* | What the slanderer **takes upon himself** |
| `024-nur.md` **24/16** | *Subḥānaka hādhā **buhtānun ʿaẓīm*** | The **words** that ought to be said |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording of the two verses:** al-Nisāʾ says what happens to the **doer** of the slander; al-Nūr says what the one who **hears** it ought to say. **The two verses stand on two different sides of the same phenomenon.**

### بُهْتَٰنًا وَإِثْمًا مُّبِينًا — an exact repetition within the sūra

**And now comes a datum of the text that can be verified: this two-word construction occurs for the second time in the sūra.**

| Verse | Sentence | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **4/20** | *A-taʾkhudhūnahū **buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā*** | **The taking back of a bridal gift** |
| **4/112** | *Fa-qadi'ḥtamala **buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā*** | **Slandering an innocent person** |

**The same three words in both verses, in the same order.** This is a countable fact.

**In the twentieth verse this construction was treated under the heading "the form of the question". I rely on that.**

**And I record this as my own reading:** the sūra uses the same heavy description in two separate places — one concerns taking something unjustly from **property**, the other from **a person's good name**. **Both acts are the taking of something from someone who cannot defend it.**

### احْتَمَلَ — the choice of verb

**The root ح-م-ل: to shoulder, to carry.** Form VIII (*iḥtamala*) — **to take upon oneself.**

**And its link with the use at `033-ahzab.md` 33/72 must be recorded:** there *wa ḥamalaha'l-insān* (the human being took it up) occurred, and that verse was about the trust.

| Place | What is taken up | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `033-ahzab.md` **33/72** | *Ḥamala**ha**'l-insān* | **The trust** |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/112** | *Iḥtamala buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā* | **Slander** |

**The same root, once for a trust and once for a slander.** I record this as an observation about word order; **I set up no link of ruling between the two verses, I merely record the two uses of the root.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** In giving judgement, the block sets down taking a side not as an **error** but as something that nullifies the judgement itself: the sentence *wa lā takun li'l-khāʾinīna khaṣīmā* stands right beside the command to judge.
