**Two.** The hundred and twelfth verse counts the shifting of an offence onto someone else as two separate burdens. **And the measure is not whether what is cast is true, but that the person it is cast upon is *barīʾ*.**

**Three.** The sentence *yastakhfūna mina'l-nās* takes as its subject the party from whom one hides. **By making one ask from whom the hiding is done, the verse makes the act itself visible.**

---

## 4/114-115 — لا خير في كثير من نجواهم إلا من أمر بصدقة أو معروف أو إصلاح بين الناس ومن يفعل ذلك ابتغاء مرضات الله فسوف نؤتيه أجرا عظيما · ومن يشاقق الرسول من بعد ما تبين له الهدى ويتبع غير سبيل المؤمنين نوله ما تولى ونصله جهنم وساءت مصيرا

*Lā khayra fī kathīrin min najwāhum illā man amara bi-ṣadaqatin aw maʿrūfin aw iṣlāḥin bayna'l-nās, wa man yafʿal dhālika'btighāʾa marḍāti'llāhi fa-sawfa nuʾtīhi ajran ʿaẓīmā · Wa man yushāqiqi'l-rasūla min baʿdi mā tabayyana lahu'l-hudā wa yattabiʿ ghayra sabīli'l-muʾminīna nuwallihī mā tawallā wa nuṣlihī jahannam, wa sāʾat maṣīrā*

"There is no good in much of their secret talk, except in one who enjoins a charitable gift, or a recognised good, or the setting right of what is between people. Whoever does this seeking God's good pleasure, We shall give him a great reward. And whoever breaks with the Messenger after the guidance has become clear to him, and follows a way other than that of the believers, We shall turn him to what he has turned to and cast him into Hell. What an evil destination!"

### نَجْوَىٰ — the picture in the word

**The root ن-ج-و: to save; and to set a thing apart, to draw it aside.** *Najāt* — deliverance; *najwa* — a place slightly raised from the ground, standing apart.

**And *najwā*, at the root of the word, is "to draw aside to a separate place and talk".** **The word, that is, names not the content of the speech but the manner of speaking.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verse's exception:** the verse leaves outside not the whole of secret talk but *much* of it (*kathīrin min najwāhum*) — **and it gives the exception, again, according to content.**

### The three items of the exception

| Order | Wording | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Ṣadaqa* | **Property** |
| 2 | *Maʿrūf* | **A recognised good** |
| 3 | ***Iṣlāḥin bayna'l-nās*** | **What is between people** |

**And the third item must be recorded: the root ص-ل-ح occurs in this sūra for the fourth time, as a *remedy*.**

| Verse | Wording | Between whom |
|---|---|---|
| **16** | *Fa-in tābā wa **aṣlaḥā*** | Two persons |
| **35** | *In yurīdā **iṣlāḥan** yuwaffiqi'llāhu baynahumā* | Spouses |
| **114** | ***Iṣlāḥin bayna'l-nās*** | **People — in general** |
| **128** | *An **yuṣliḥā** baynahumā ṣulḥan wa'l-**ṣulḥu** khayr* | Spouses |

**The same root in four verses, and in all four the setting right of a breach.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be tabled again in the concluding section.

**I record this as my own reading:** the sūra uses the same word from a disagreement inside the family (35) to human relations in general (114). **The scale changes, the verb does not.**

### يُشَاقِقِ ٱلرَّسُولَ — the root ش-ق-ق

**The root occurred at the thirty-fifth verse as *shiqāq* and was treated there: *the splitting of a whole in two.* I rely on that treatment.**

| Verse | Wording | Between whom the split runs |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *Shiqāqa **baynihimā*** | Spouses |
| **115** | *Yushāqiqi'**l-rasūl*** | Between the Messenger and a person |

**The same root, at two separate scales.** I record this as an observation about word order.

**And the qualification in the conditional clause must be recorded: *min baʿdi mā tabayyana lahu'l-hudā* — "after the guidance has become clear to him".** **The ruling is tied not to ignorance but to what is done with what is known.**

**This is the same line recorded at `002-bakara.md` 2/75:** *"the matter is not ignorance but what is done with what is known."* **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 4/116-121 — إن الله لا يغفر أن يشرك به ويغفر ما دون ذلك لمن يشاء … · لعنه الله وقال لأتخذن من عبادك نصيبا مفروضا · ولأضلنهم ولأمنينهم … · يعدهم ويمنيهم وما يعدهم الشيطان إلا غرورا

*Inna'llāha lā yaghfiru an yushraka bihī wa yaghfiru mā dūna dhālika li-man yashāʾ … · Laʿanahu'llāh, wa qāla la-attakhidhanna min ʿibādika naṣīban mafrūḍā · Wa la-uḍillannahum wa la-umanniyannahum … · Yaʿiduhum wa yumannīhim, wa mā yaʿiduhumu'l-shayṭānu illā ghurūrā*

"God does not forgive that partners be associated with Him; what is other than that He forgives for whom He wills… God cursed him. And he said: 'I shall take an appointed share of Your servants. I shall lead them astray, I shall fill them with vain hopes…' Satan promises them and fills them with vain hopes; and Satan promises them nothing but delusion."

### نَصِيبًا مَّفْرُوضًا — the sūra's phrase turned inside out

**And here is one of the most striking uses of a word in the sūra.**

**The sūra's seventh verse ran: *naṣīban mafrūḍā* — "an appointed share". And there the construction stated a right of inheritance.**

**In the hundred and eighteenth verse the same construction occurs inside a claim: *la-attakhidhanna min ʿibādika naṣīban mafrūḍā*.**

| Verse | Construction | Who takes | From whom |
|---|---|---|---|
| **7** | *Naṣīban mafrūḍā* | **Men and women** | From what is left |
| **118** | *Naṣīban mafrūḍā* | **The one who makes the claim** | *Min ʿibādik* — from the servants |

**The same two words, word for word, in two verses.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the exact repetition of the construction:** the sūra repeats, in the mouth of a claim to seize, the very construction with which it stated the fixing of a right. **The same words, once a right and once a demand.**

### ٱلْأَمَانِىّ — the root م-ن-ي

**The root was treated at the thirty-second verse of this sūra: *lā tatamannaw* — do not covet. I rely on that treatment.**

**And the word returns here as a verb: *la-umanniyannahum* (I shall fill them with vain hopes) and *yumannīhim*.**

| Verse | Word | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **32** | *Lā tatamannaw* — **a prohibition** | Those addressed |
| **119, 120** | *Umanniyannahum*, *yumannīhim* | **The other party makes them do it** |
| **123** | *Laysa bi-**amāniyyikum*** | **The rejection of resting on vain hope** |

**The same root in three positions within the sūra: a prohibition, a prompting, and a rejection.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

### فَلَيُغَيِّرُنَّ خَلْقَ ٱللَّهِ — a record of disagreement

**There is disagreement in the classical commentaries over what this expression in the hundred and nineteenth verse covers, and the explanations differ from one another.**

**Nothing definite is said in this commentary about the scope of the expression, and no list is given.** The reason is the method's rule of accuracy: **to fill in the content of an expression whose scope is not written in the verse would be to add to the verse.**

**What can be said from the text is this: the expression occurs inside a sentence of *promise*, and the speaker is not the verse itself but the one whose words are being related.** **The sentence, that is, stands not as a ruling but as the relating of a claim.**

### غُرُور — the analysis of the word

**The root غ-ر-ر was treated in detail at `035-fatir.md` 35/5, and it was recorded there: an outward appearance that dazzles the eye while the inside is empty. I rely on that treatment.**

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: *wa mā yaʿiduhumu'l-shayṭānu illā ghurūrā*.** The sentence says not that the promise is **a lie** but that **the promise itself is a delusion** — that is, what is empty is not the content of the promise but the act of promising.

**I record this as an observation about word order; its ground is the position of the particle *illā*.**

---

## 4/122-126 — والذين آمنوا وعملوا الصالحات سندخلهم جنات تجري من تحتها الأنهار … · ليس بأمانيكم ولا أماني أهل الكتاب من يعمل سوءا يجز به ولا يجد له من دون الله وليا ولا نصيرا · ومن يعمل من الصالحات من ذكر أو أنثى وهو مؤمن فأولئك يدخلون الجنة ولا يظلمون نقيرا · ومن أحسن دينا ممن أسلم وجهه لله وهو محسن واتبع ملة إبراهيم حنيفا واتخذ الله إبراهيم خليلا

*Wa'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti sa-nudkhiluhum jannātin tajrī min taḥtiha'l-anhār … · Laysa bi-amāniyyikum wa lā amāniyyi ahli'l-kitāb, man yaʿmal sūʾan yujza bihī wa lā yajid lahū min dūni'llāhi waliyyan wa lā naṣīrā · Wa man yaʿmal mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti min dhakarin aw unthā wa huwa muʾminun fa-ulāʾika yadkhulūna'l-jannata wa lā yuẓlamūna naqīrā · Wa man aḥsanu dīnan mimman aslama wajhahū li'llāhi wa huwa muḥsinun wa'ttabaʿa millata Ibrāhīma ḥanīfā, wa'ttakhadha'llāhu Ibrāhīma khalīlā*

"Those who believe and do righteous deeds We shall admit into gardens beneath which rivers flow… It goes neither by your vain hopes nor by the vain hopes of the People of the Book. Whoever does an evil will be repaid for it, and will find for himself no protector or helper besides God. And whoever does righteous deeds, male or female, and is a believer — these enter the Garden and are not wronged by so much as the groove of a date-stone. And who is finer in religion than one who surrenders his face to God, does good, and follows the way of Abraham (Ibrāhīm) as a *ḥanīf*? God took Abraham as a friend."

### لَّيْسَ بِأَمَانِيِّكُمْ وَلَآ أَمَانِىِّ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ — a qualification laid on both sides at once

**This sentence is one of the plainest textual grounds for the method's rule that "no sweeping ruling is made about a group", and it must be recorded separately.**

**The sentence counts both parties at the same time:**

| Party | Wording |
|---|---|
| **Those addressed** | *Bi-**amāniyyikum*** — your vain hopes |
| **The People of the Book** | *Wa lā **amāniyyi ahli'l-kitāb*** |

**And the structure of the sentence must be recorded: the same rejection, in the same word, is applied to both parties at once.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the sentence is built twice:** the verse says that a belonging does not by itself produce a return — **and it says this while taking in the very community it is addressing.**

**The same measure was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/62, where the section was headed: "A label does not save." I rely on that treatment.**

**And the continuation of the verse ties the measure to an act: *man yaʿmal sūʾan yujza bih* — "whoever does an evil will be repaid for it."** **The measure is not a name but an act.**

### مِن ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنثَىٰ — a second symmetry in the sūra

**The hundred and twenty-fourth verse ties the return plainly to both parties: *min dhakarin aw unthā wa huwa muʾmin*.**

**And this happens for the third time in the sūra.**

| Verse | The subject of the symmetry | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | **Inheritance** | *Li'l-rijāli naṣīb … wa li'l-nisāʾi naṣīb* |
| **32** | **Earnings** | *Mimma'ktasabū … mimma'ktasabna* |
| **124** | **The return for one's deeds** | *Min dhakarin aw unthā* |

**In all three verses the same structure: in the counting of a right or a return, the two parties are named separately.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and it will be tabled again in the concluding section.

***Naqīr* — the small hollow on the back of a date-stone.** **And the word is the third "smallest measure" word in the sūra:**

| Verse | Word | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **40** | *Mithqāla dharra* | The smallest weight |
| **77** | *Fatīl* | The thread in the groove of a date-stone |
| **124** | *Naqīr* | The hollow on the back of a date-stone |

**Three separate small measures in three verses, and all three state the absence of injustice.** This is a countable fact.

### خَلِيلًا — the analysis of the word

**The root خ-ل-ل: the gap between things, an interval.** *Khilāl* — an interval; *khalal* — a gap, a deficiency. **And *khalīl* — according to what the lexicographers record, the friend whose friendship has worked its way into a person's inner gaps.**

**This concrete sense of the word is worth recording:** friendship is drawn as something that fills the gaps.

**And the word *ḥanīf* must be recorded: the root ح-ن-ف — to incline towards one direction, to lean.** **The root was treated at `030-rum.md` 30/30 and in `002-bakara.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

---

## 4/127 — ويستفتونك في النساء قل الله يفتيكم فيهن وما يتلى عليكم في الكتاب في يتامى النساء اللاتي لا تؤتونهن ما كتب لهن وترغبون أن تنكحوهن والمستضعفين من الولدان وأن تقوموا لليتامى بالقسط

*Wa yastaftūnaka fi'l-nisāʾ, quli'llāhu yuftīkum fīhinna wa mā yutlā ʿalaykum fi'l-kitābi fī yatāma'l-nisāʾi'llātī lā tuʾtūnahunna mā kutiba lahunna wa targhabūna an tankiḥūhunna wa'l-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-wildāni wa an taqūmū li'l-yatāmā bi'l-qisṭ*

"They ask you for a ruling concerning women. Say: God gives you a ruling concerning them — and so does what is recited to you in the Book: concerning the orphan girls to whom you do not give what has been prescribed for them and whom you desire to marry, concerning the children who have been made weak, and concerning your standing by the orphans in equity."

### A return to the sūra's first block

**This verse returns to the second and third verses of the sūra, and this was recorded in the introductory section. I rely on that.**

**And the detail of the return must be recorded: the verse counts three items, and all three come from the sūra's first block.**

| Item | Wording | Its counterpart in the first block |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yatāma'l-nisāʾ* … *lā tuʾtūnahunna mā kutiba lahunn* | 4/2-3 — the property and marriage of orphan girls |
| **2** | *Al-mustaḍʿafīna mina'l-wildān* | 4/9 — *dhurriyyatan ḍiʿāfan* |
| **3** | *An taqūmū li'l-yatāmā **bi'l-qisṭ*** | 4/3 — *allā tuqsiṭū fi'l-yatāmā* |

**Three items, tied back to three separate verses.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

### وَٱلْمُسْتَضْعَفِينَ مِنَ ٱلْوِلْدَٰنِ — the word's third occurrence

**The word occurs for the third time in this sūra: at the seventy-fifth, ninety-eighth and hundred and twenty-seventh verses.**

| Verse | Who is counted | Context |
|---|---|---|
| **75** | *Al-rijāl*, *al-nisāʾ*, *al-wildān* | The ground for war |
| **98** | *Al-rijāl*, *al-nisāʾ*, *al-wildān* | The acceptance of an excuse |
| **127** | **Only *al-wildān*** | **A ruling** |

**And it must be recorded that in the third occurrence the list narrows: only children are named.** This is a countable fact.

### أَن تَقُومُوا۟ لِلْيَتَٰمَىٰ بِٱلْقِسْطِ — and the preparation for the hundred and thirty-fifth verse

**And the last clause of the verse must be recorded, because it carries the words of a sentence that will come eight verses later.**

| Verse | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **127** | *An **taqūmū** li'l-yatāmā **bi'l-qisṭ*** |
| **135** | *Kūnū **qawwāmīna bi'l-qisṭ*** |

**In both sentences the root ق-و-م and the root ق-س-ط stand side by side.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the words the two verses share:** the sūra builds the same command first for **orphans** and then for **everyone**. **The hundred and thirty-fifth verse is the generalised form of the sentence in the hundred and twenty-seventh.**

---

## 4/128-130 — وإن امرأة خافت من بعلها نشوزا أو إعراضا فلا جناح عليهما أن يصلحا بينهما صلحا والصلح خير وأحضرت الأنفس الشح … · ولن تستطيعوا أن تعدلوا بين النساء ولو حرصتم فلا تميلوا كل الميل فتذروها كالمعلقة وإن تصلحوا وتتقوا فإن الله كان غفورا رحيما · وإن يتفرقا يغن الله كلا من سعته

*Wa ini'mraʾatun khāfat min baʿlihā nushūzan aw iʿrāḍan fa-lā junāḥa ʿalayhimā an yuṣliḥā baynahumā ṣulḥā, wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr, wa uḥḍirati'l-anfusu'l-shuḥḥ … · Wa lan tastaṭīʿū an taʿdilū bayna'l-nisāʾi wa law ḥaraṣtum, fa-lā tamīlū kulla'l-mayli fa-tadharūhā ka'l-muʿallaqa, wa in tuṣliḥū wa tattaqū fa-inna'llāha kāna Ghafūran Raḥīmā · Wa in yatafarraqā yughni'llāhu kullan min saʿatih*

"If a woman fears *nushūz* or turning away from her husband, there is no fault upon the two of them in setting things right between themselves by a settlement. And settlement is better. Souls are prone to grasping… You will never be able to deal equitably among women, however much you desire it. So do not incline wholly to one side and leave the other as though suspended. If you set things right and are mindful, God is forgiving, merciful. And if they part, God will enrich each of them out of His abundance."

### One: نُشُوز — reading it together with 4/34

**This verse uses the same root as the thirty-fourth, and the symmetry was set out in a table in the discussion of the thirty-fourth and thirty-fifth verses. I rely on that and do not repeat the table.**

**What belongs here are two things not found in the thirty-fourth verse:**

| What | Wording | In the thirty-fourth verse |
|---|---|---|
| **A second word** | *Nushūzan **aw iʿrāḍā*** | Absent — only *nushūz* |
| **Who does the fearing** | *Imraʾatun **khāfat*** — the woman herself | *Takhāfūna* — those addressed |

***Iʿrāḍ* — root ع-ر-ض: to turn aside, to turn one's face away.** **The root was treated at `033-ahzab.md` 33/72, and it was recorded there: *"Iʿrāḍ* — turning away (turning aside)." I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording of the verse:** the hundred and twenty-eighth verse adds a second word to the word in the thirty-fourth and describes the situation **in two items**.

### Two: وَٱلصُّلْحُ خَيْرٌ — a sentence of three words

**The root ص-ل-ح occurs four times in this sūra as a remedy, and the table was given in the discussion of the hundred and fourteenth verse. I rely on that.**

**What belongs here is the form of the sentence: *wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr* — three words, a nominal sentence.**

**In Arabic a nominal sentence states continuance and fixity; a verbal sentence states a coming-to-be.** **That the sentence is built as a nominal sentence shows that it states not an observation about a situation but a principle.**

**And the sentence's place in the sūra must be recorded:** it is the second row of the trio set out in a table in the discussion of the twenty-fifth verse (*wa an taṣbirū khayrun lakum* — *wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr* — *in tubdū khayran aw tukhfūhu aw taʿfū ʿan sūʾin*). **I rely on that.**

### Three: وَأُحْضِرَتِ ٱلْأَنفُسُ ٱلشُّحَّ — the analysis of the word

**The root ش-ح-ح: miserliness; and the distinction the lexicographers record is this — *bukhl* is not giving what one has, while *shuḥḥ* is that with greed added to it.**

**And the pattern of the verb must be recorded: *uḥḍirat* — passive, Form IV.** **The sentence, that is, does not say "souls are miserly"; it says "grasping has been brought close to souls".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the passive pattern of the verb:** the sentence sets up not an accusation but an **observation** — and the observation stands immediately after the call to settlement.

**What the verse does, that is, is this: it proposes agreement, then names by its name the thing that makes agreement hard.**

### Four: وَلَن تَسْتَطِيعُوٓا۟ أَن تَعْدِلُوا۟ — a comparison with 4/3

**This comparison was set out in a table in the discussion of the third verse, under the heading "Seven: 4/3 together with 4/129". I rely on that and do not repeat the table here.**

**Two things belong here.**

**One. What the table looks like seen from this side — I keep it short and rest only on the wording:**

| | **4/3** | **4/129** |
|---|---|---|
| **Sentence** | *Fa-in khiftum allā taʿdilū fa-wāḥida* | *Wa lan tastaṭīʿū an taʿdilū bayna'l-nisāʾi wa law ḥaraṣtum* |
| **Mood** | Conditional | *Lan* — the strongest negation of the future |
| **Added qualification** | — | *Wa law ḥaraṣtum* — however much you desire it |
| **Outcome** | *Fa-wāḥida* | *Fa-lā tamīlū kulla'l-mayl* |

**In the discussion of the third verse three tendencies (a, b, c) on how the two verses are to be reconciled were given in a table, and none of them was preferred. That record holds here too.**

**Two. The hundred and twenty-ninth verse's *own* measure must be recorded separately.**

**Having stated the impossibility, the verse gives a command: *fa-lā tamīlū kulla'l-mayl*.**

**And the word order must be recorded: what is forbidden is not inclining but inclining *wholly*.** *Kulla'l-mayl* — an absolute object; **"the whole of the inclining".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse does not ask of a person what he cannot do; **it sets a limit he can keep.**

**And the root م-ي-ل occurs for the second time in the sūra.** In the twenty-seventh verse there was *maylan ʿaẓīmā*, and it was tabled there (27 and 129). **I rely on that.**

### Five: كَٱلْمُعَلَّقَةِ — the picture in the word

**The root ع-ل-ق: to hang on to something, to cling.** *ʿAlaq* — what hangs, what clings; *taʿlīq* — to hang up.

***Al-muʿallaqa* — passive participle: hung up, left suspended.**

**And the picture in the word must be recorded: neither come down to the ground nor up above; caught in between.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the pattern of the word:** the verse names the state of being neither married nor separated **with a single word**. **And it says that this is a harm by making the word the ground of a prohibition.**

### Six: وَإِن يَتَفَرَّقَا يُغْنِ ٱللَّهُ كُلًّا مِّن سَعَتِهِۦ — the close of the block

**The verse does not close off the possibility of separation either, and it enters a record.**

**And the root و-س-ع occurs for the third time in the sūra:**

| Verse | Word | Whose wideness |
|---|---|---|
| **97** | *Wāsiʿa* | **The earth's** |
| **100** | *Saʿa* | **What the migrant will find** |
| **130** | *Min **saʿatih*** | **What will be given to the two who part** |

**The same root in three verses, and in all three a possibility of a way out.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the sūra shows the person hemmed in a wideness, in three separate contexts and with the same word — a homeland, a road, and a livelihood.

### What this says to the present

**One.** The hundred and twenty-eighth verse begins the dispute with **the woman's own fear**: *imraʾatun khāfat*. And it ties the remedy not to a sanction but to an agreement between the two parties.

**Two.** The sentence *uḥḍirati'l-anfusu'l-shuḥḥ* does not lay the obstacle to agreement upon one of the parties; **it uses the plural: *al-anfus*.**

**Three.** The word *ka'l-muʿallaqa* counts the **leaving of a relationship undecided** plainly as a harm. The verse names a state that chooses neither to continue nor to end as a matter in its own right.

---

## 4/131-134 — ولله ما في السماوات وما في الأرض ولقد وصينا الذين أوتوا الكتاب من قبلكم وإياكم أن اتقوا الله … · من كان يريد ثواب الدنيا فعند الله ثواب الدنيا والآخرة وكان الله سميعا بصيرا

*Wa li'llāhi mā fi's-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍ, wa laqad waṣṣayna'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba min qablikum wa iyyākum ani'ttaqu'llāh … · Man kāna yurīdu thawāba'l-dunyā fa-ʿinda'llāhi thawābu'l-dunyā wa'l-ākhira, wa kāna'llāhu Samīʿan Baṣīrā*

"To God belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. We enjoined upon those given the Book before you, and upon you, that you be mindful of God… Whoever wants the reward of this world — with God is the reward of this world and of the hereafter. God is Hearing, Seeing."

### وَصَّيْنَا — a link with the sūra's first block

**The root و-ص-ي occurred at the eleventh and twelfth verses of this sūra** (*yūṣīkumu'llāh*, *waṣiyya*) **and was treated there. I rely on that treatment.**

| Verse | Word | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **11** | *Yūṣīkumu'llāhu fī awlādikum* | **Inheritance** |
| **131** | *Waṣṣayna'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba … wa iyyākum* | **Mindfulness of God** |

**The same root, once for the division of property and once for mindfulness of God.**

**And that in the hundred and thirty-first verse the address is made to both parties at once must be recorded: *alladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba min qablikum wa iyyākum*.**

**This is in the same direction as the qualification in the hundred and twenty-third verse** (*laysa bi-amāniyyikum wa lā amāniyyi ahli'l-kitāb*): **the same thing is said to both parties at once.** This is a repetition that can be verified within the sūra and **is the textual ground of the method's rule against sweeping rulings.**

### ثَوَابَ ٱلدُّنْيَا وَٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ — the form of the answer

**The root ث-و-ب: to return, to come back.** *Thawb* — a garment (what returns to the body); *thawāb* — the return that comes back for what one has done.

**And the form of the sentence must be recorded: the question is about "one who wants the reward of this world"; the answer names both together.**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| *Man kāna yurīdu **thawāba'l-dunyā*** | *Fa-ʿinda'llāhi thawābu'**l-dunyā wa'l-ākhira*** |

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the sentence does not reject the wanting; **it says that the wanting has been kept too narrow.**

---

## 4/135 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا كونوا قوامين بالقسط شهداء لله ولو على أنفسكم أو الوالدين والأقربين إن يكن غنيا أو فقيرا فالله أولى بهما فلا تتبعوا الهوى أن تعدلوا وإن تلووا أو تعرضوا فإن الله كان بما تعملون خبيرا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū kūnū qawwāmīna bi'l-qisṭi shuhadāʾa li'llāhi wa law ʿalā anfusikum awi'l-wālidayni wa'l-aqrabīn, in yakun ghaniyyan aw faqīran fa'llāhu awlā bihimā, fa-lā tattabiʿu'l-hawā an taʿdilū, wa in talwū aw tuʿriḍū fa-inna'llāha kāna bimā taʿmalūna Khabīrā*

"O you who believe! Be upholders of equity, witnesses for God, even against yourselves or your parents and near kin. Whether a man be rich or poor, God is closer to them both. So do not follow desire, and so swerve from justice. And if you twist your words or turn away, God is aware of what you do."

### This verse will be treated in detail

**This verse opens the sūra's thirteenth block and is the sharpest sentence in the sūra's discussion of justice.**

### One: كُونُوا۟ قَوَّٰمِينَ بِٱلْقِسْطِ — the analysis of the words

**The word *qawwām* and the فَعَّال pattern were set out in a table in the discussion of the thirty-fourth and thirty-fifth verses, resting on `005-maide.md` 5/8. I rely on that and do not repeat it.**

**The record made there must be carried over here:**

> *"That is, a *qawwām* is not 'one who acts justly on one occasion' but one who makes it his business to keep justice standing. The command asks not for a behaviour but for **a character**. And the verb's coming as *kūnū* (be) supports this: not 'do' but '**be**'."*

**The root ق-س-ط was treated in detail at `049-hucurat.md` 49/9 and summarised at `005-maide.md` 5/8. The records made there:**

> *"**Its primary sense is a portion, a share; and from there, giving a portion in full.** *Qisṭ* — a share; *muqsiṭ* — one who gives shares rightly. **The interesting side of the root:** in Form IV (*aqsaṭa*) it conveys acting justly, while in its bare form (*qasaṭa*) it conveys **acting wrongfully** — it is one of the instances of *aḍdād*."*

> *"*ʿAdl* is the picture of scales and looks to the whole; *qisṭ* is the picture of a share and looks to a party."*

**I rely on those treatments.**

**And the occurrences of the word *qisṭ* in this sūra must be recorded:**

| Verse | Wording | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | *Allā **tuqsiṭū** fi'l-yatāmā* | Orphans |
| **127** | *An taqūmū li'l-yatāmā **bi'l-qisṭ*** | Orphans |
| **135** | *Kūnū qawwāmīna **bi'l-qisṭ*** | **Everyone** |

**The same root in three verses, and the first two concern orphans.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra, and **I record it as my own reading:** the sūra uses the word for justice first for the most unprotected group, then generalises it.

### Two: a comparison with `005-maide.md` 5/8 — the same words, the order reversed

**There is another, very similar form of this command in the Qurʾān, and when the two sentences are set side by side an exchange of places becomes visible. This comparison was made at `005-maide.md` 5/8, and I carry it over here.**

| Place | Sentence |
|---|---|
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/135** | *Kūnū **qawwāmīna bi'l-qisṭi** **shuhadāʾa li'llāh*** |
| **al-Māʾida 5/8** | *Kūnū **qawwāmīna li'llāhi** **shuhadāʾa bi'l-qisṭ*** |

**The two phrases have changed places: *bi'l-qisṭ* and *li'llāh* have taken one another's position.** This is a countable fact.

**Two explanations of this difference were set out in a table at `005-maide.md` 5/8, and I carry them over as they stand:**

| Explanation | How |
|---|---|
| **First** | In each verse the element **placed first** answers to the real difficulty of that verse. In bearing witness against one's kin what is hard is **justice itself**, and so *bi'l-qisṭ* is placed first in al-Nisāʾ; in bearing witness in favour of an enemy what is hard is **that the intention be for God**, and so *li'llāh* is placed first in al-Māʾida |
| **Second** | The two sentences complete one another; the difference of order is a difference of emphasis, and the two verses together give both the ground and the measure of justice |

**As there, so here I impose no preference.** **And the record entered there holds here too:** that the second explanation is the safer of the two so far as the text goes was added **as my own reading**; its ground is that the same four elements are present in both verses.

**And the continuations of the two verses must be compared too, because the ground of the explanations lies there:**

| | **al-Nisāʾ 4/135** | **al-Māʾida 5/8** |
|---|---|---|
| **What makes justice hard** | **Closeness** — *anfusikum*, *al-wālidayn*, *al-aqrabīn* | **Hatred** — *shanaʾānu qawm* |
| **Direction** | Swerving **in favour of** one's own side | Swerving **against** the other side |
| **Closing** | *Inna'llāha kāna bimā taʿmalūna **Khabīrā*** | *Inna'llāha **Khabīrun** bimā taʿmalūn* |

**And it must be recorded that the closings are almost identical: both verses end with an attribute from the root خ-ب-ر.** **At `005-maide.md` 5/8 it was recorded of this root:** *"The root خ-ب-ر conveys knowing the inside of a thing, its hidden side; *khabīr* is one who knows not the outside but the inside."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the two verses close off the two opposite directions in which justice breaks down, and **both end with the same attribute** — because in both the breaking down takes place **inside**.

### Three: وَلَوْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ — the sequence itself

**The verse counts three items, and the order must be recorded:**

| Order | Wording | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *ʿAlā **anfusikum*** | **Yourselves** |
| **2** | *Awi'l-**wālidayn*** | Parents |
| **3** | *Wa'l-**aqrabīn*** | Near kin |

**And the order is the reverse of the list in the thirty-sixth verse.** There the list began with the parents and widened outward; here it begins with oneself and widens outward — **but this time *against*.**

| Verse | Order | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **36** | Parents → near kin → … → those under one's hand | **In favour** — kindness |
| **135** | **Oneself** → parents → near kin | **Against** — testimony |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the comparison of the two lists:** the sūra counts out the same circles of closeness twice — once as the address of kindness, once as the place where justice will be tested.

**And the word *al-arḥām* should be recalled: the sūra's first verse set the tie of kinship beside mindfulness of God.** **The hundred and thirty-fifth verse says that this tie cannot take precedence over justice.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the word the two verses share (*al-aqrabīn* / *al-arḥām*):** the sūra both keeps the tie and limits it — **the tie it asks to be kept is not a reason for favour in testimony.**

### Four: إِن يَكُنْ غَنِيًّا أَوْ فَقِيرًا فَٱللَّهُ أَوْلَىٰ بِهِمَا

**The verse closes off a second cause of swerving too: the material condition of the parties.**

**And the form of the sentence must be recorded: both possibilities are counted — *ghaniyyan aw faqīrā*.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse takes in not only currying favour with the rich but **bending out of pity for the poor**. **Both directions in a single sentence.**

**And the ground must be recorded: *fa'llāhu awlā bihimā* — "God is closer to them both".** **Neither the power of the rich nor the condition of the poor, that is, counts as information that should determine the judgement.**

### Five: أَن تَعْدِلُوا۟ — a disagreement of word order

**The sentence is this: *fa-lā tattabiʿu'l-hawā an taʿdilū*.**

**Two readings of this construction are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Reading | How it understands it | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | *An taʿdilū* — **"so as to swerve from justice"**; that is, "do not follow desire, and so swerve" | The use of the root ع-د-ل in this context; the sentence as a whole |
| **B** | A negation is understood: *allā taʿdilū* — "in a way that would lead to injustice" | Instances of such ellipsis in Arabic |

**Both readings are transmitted; I impose no preference.** **And both arrive at the same place: following desire breaks justice.**

***Hawā* — root ه-و-ي: to fall, to come down; and from there, to incline, to want.** **At the root of the word there is a *going downward*, and this is worth recording as a matter of word choice.**

### Six: وَإِن تَلْوُۥٓا۟ أَوْ تُعْرِضُوا۟ — two verbs

**The verse counts two ways in which justice breaks down, and both are verbs:**

| Verb | Root | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| *Talwū* | ل-و-ي | **To twist** — to bend one's words |
| *Tuʿriḍū* | ع-ر-ض | **To turn away** — not to speak at all |

**The root ل-و-ي occurs for the second time in this sūra: in the forty-sixth verse there was *layyan bi-alsinatihim*, and it was treated there. I rely on that treatment.**

**The root ع-ر-ض also occurs several times in the sūra** (16, 63, 81, 94, 128). **In the hundred and twenty-eighth verse *iʿrāḍ* stated a turning away from a relationship.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the standing of the two verbs side by side:** the verse describes the breaking down of testimony in two separate ways — **saying it wrongly and not saying it at all.** **The second is counted as plainly as the first.**

### What this says to the present

**One.** The verse counts three causes that break justice, and all three produce **partiality**: closeness, material condition and desire. And against all three the same thing is set: *shuhadāʾa li'llāh*.

**Two.** The qualification *wa law ʿalā anfusikum* stands first in the list. **The verse begins from the nearest possibility of favour.**

**Three.** That keeping silent is counted as a form of breaking down too (*aw tuʿriḍū*) sets testimony up as an **obligation**. The same field was treated at `002-bakara.md` 2/283 as well (*wa lā taktumu'l-shahāda*). **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 4/136-139 — يا أيها الذين آمنوا آمنوا بالله ورسوله والكتاب الذي نزل على رسوله والكتاب الذي أنزل من قبل … · إن الذين آمنوا ثم كفروا ثم آمنوا ثم كفروا ثم ازدادوا كفرا لم يكن الله ليغفر لهم … · الذين يتخذون الكافرين أولياء من دون المؤمنين أيبتغون عندهم العزة فإن العزة لله جميعا

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū āminū bi'llāhi wa rasūlihī wa'l-kitābi'lladhī nazzala ʿalā rasūlihī wa'l-kitābi'lladhī anzala min qabl … · Inna'lladhīna āmanū thumma kafarū thumma āmanū thumma kafarū thumma'zdādū kufran lam yakuni'llāhu li-yaghfira lahum … · Alladhīna yattakhidhūna'l-kāfirīna awliyāʾa min dūni'l-muʾminīn, a-yabtaghūna ʿindahumu'l-ʿizzata fa-inna'l-ʿizzata li'llāhi jamīʿā*

"O you who believe! Believe in God, in His Messenger, in the Book He has sent down upon His Messenger, and in the Book He sent down before… Those who believe, then deny, then believe, then deny, then increase in denial — God will not forgive them… Those who take the deniers as allies instead of the believers — is it honour they seek with them? All honour belongs to God."

### First, a note on method — and this note holds for 136-152

**This block describes a group, and per the method (`USLUP.md`) the description is read thus: what is at issue is not a group but a series of acts.**

**And this is not an interpretation but the verses' own construction:**

| Verse | Wording | What it states |
|---|---|---|
| **137** | *Āmanū **thumma** kafarū **thumma** āmanū **thumma** kafarū* | A **process** — verbs are set out in order |
| **139** | *Alladhīna **yattakhidhūna**'l-kāfirīna awliyāʾa* | A **behaviour** |
| **142** | *Wa idhā qāmū ila'ṣ-ṣalāti qāmū kusālā* | A **state** |
| **146** | ***Illa'lladhīna tābū wa aṣlaḥū*** | **An exception — the door is open** |

**The fourth row must be recorded separately: the hundred and forty-sixth verse writes out plainly the way out of the description.** **The verses, that is, themselves block a sweeping generalisation.**

**This field was treated in detail in `063-munafikun.md`, and I rely on that treatment.** **The frame established there was this: the matter is not the truth of the statement but the speaker's relation to what he says.**

### آمَنُوا۟ ثُمَّ كَفَرُوا۟ — four steps and a fifth

**The hundred and thirty-seventh verse sets out five verbs with *thumma*.** And in Arabic *thumma* states **a sequence with intervals**: there is time between the verbs.

| Order | Verb |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Āmanū* |
| 2 | *Thumma kafarū* |
| 3 | *Thumma āmanū* |
| 4 | *Thumma kafarū* |
| 5 | ***Thumma'zdādū kufrā*** |

**And it must be recorded that the fifth step differs from those before it: what is named is not a going back and forth but an *increase*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the pattern of the fifth verb (*izdād* — Form VIII, to increase):** the verse counts as the last step not the going back and forth itself but **the settling in one direction**.

**The same field was treated at `063-munafikun.md` 63/3** (*āmanū thumma kafarū fa-ṭubiʿa ʿalā qulūbihim*) **and was set out there in a table under the heading "the four steps of a process". I rely on that treatment.**

### ٱلْعِزَّة — the analysis of the word

**The root ع-ز-ز: that a thing be hard, firm, out of reach.** *Arḍun ʿazāz* — hard ground. From there comes the sense of **superiority, invincibility**.

**And the form of the sentence must be recorded: the verse asks a question (*a-yabtaghūna ʿindahumu'l-ʿizza*) and gives the answer in a sentence of *restriction*: *fa-inna'l-ʿizzata li'llāhi jamīʿā* — "all honour belongs to God."**

**The word *jamīʿā* is set there as an emphasis.** And at `063-munafikun.md` 63/8 the same word occurs as a claim from the other side: *la-yukhrijanna'l-aʿazzu mina'l-adhall*. **Two sūras use the same root in two different mouths.**

---

## 4/140-141 — وقد نزل عليكم في الكتاب أن إذا سمعتم آيات الله يكفر بها ويستهزأ بها فلا تقعدوا معهم حتى يخوضوا في حديث غيره إنكم إذا مثلهم … · الذين يتربصون بكم فإن كان لكم فتح من الله قالوا ألم نكن معكم

*Wa qad nazzala ʿalaykum fi'l-kitābi an idhā samiʿtum āyāti'llāhi yukfaru bihā wa yustahzaʾu bihā fa-lā taqʿudū maʿahum ḥattā yakhūḍū fī ḥadīthin ghayrih, innakum idhan mithluhum … · Alladhīna yatarabbaṣūna bikum fa-in kāna lakum fatḥun mina'llāhi qālū a-lam nakun maʿakum*

"It has already been sent down to you in the Book: when you hear God's verses being denied and mocked, do not sit with them until they pass to some other talk; otherwise you are like them… Those who wait to see what happens to you: if a victory comes to you from God they say, 'were we not with you?'"

### `006-enam.md` 6/68 — the same ruling in two sūras

**The most striking side of this verse is that the ruling is found in another sūra as well, and that the verse says so itself: *wa qad nazzala ʿalaykum fi'l-kitāb* — "it has already been sent down to you in the Book."**

**And the verse at `006-enam.md` 6/68 is this:**

> **وَإِذَا رَأَيْتَ ٱلَّذِينَ يَخُوضُونَ فِىٓ ءَايَٰتِنَا فَأَعْرِضْ عَنْهُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَخُوضُوا۟ فِى حَدِيثٍ غَيْرِهِۦ**
> *"When you see those who talk idly about Our verses, turn away from them until they pass to some other talk."* (al-Anʿām 6/68)

**I set the two verses side by side:**

| | **`006-enam.md` 6/68** | **al-Nisāʾ 4/140** |
|---|---|---|
| **What triggers it** | *Raʾayta* — **when you see** | *Samiʿtum* — **when you hear** |
| **Who is addressed** | Singular — *fa-aʿriḍ* | **Plural** — *fa-lā taqʿudū* |
| **The command** | *Aʿriḍ ʿanhum* — turn away | *Lā taqʿudū maʿahum* — **do not sit with them** |
| **The limit** | ***Ḥattā yakhūḍū fī ḥadīthin ghayrih*** | ***Ḥattā yakhūḍū fī ḥadīthin ghayrih*** |
| **Added qualification** | — | *Innakum idhan **mithluhum*** |

**And the fourth row must be recorded separately: the clause of limit is word for word identical in the two sūras.** This is a countable fact.

**What was recorded for this limit at `006-enam.md` 6/68 must be carried over here:**

> *"And the limit of the command is worth recording: *ḥattā yakhūḍū fī ḥadīthin ghayrih* — until they pass to some other talk. **I record this as my own reading:** the turning away is not **without term**; it is **tied to the subject.** And it is tied not to persons but **to what is being said.** **That is, the separation is not set up as a permanent break.**"*

**I rely on that treatment and keep that record here too.** **This is a textual ground for the method's rule against sweeping rulings: the command is tied not to a person but to a subject.**

**And the qualification al-Nisāʾ adds must be recorded: *innakum idhan mithluhum* — "then you are like them".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two verses:** the command in al-Anʿām states a turning away; the command in al-Nisāʾ counts **the sitting together itself** as a taking part. **Al-Nisāʾ adds the ground of the ruling.**

### يَتَرَبَّصُونَ — the analysis of the word

**The root ر-ب-ص: to watch and wait for something to happen.** *Tarabbuṣ* (Form V) — **to wait and watch for an opportunity.**

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: both possibilities are counted, and in each the words spoken are given.**

| Case | What is said |
|---|---|
| *In kāna lakum **fatḥun** mina'llāh* | *A-lam nakun maʿakum* — were we not with you |
| *Wa in kāna li'l-kāfirīna **naṣīb*** | *A-lam nastaḥwidh ʿalaykum wa namnaʿkum mina'l-muʾminīn* |

**And the word *naṣīb* occurs here for the seventh time in the sūra.** The word's distribution in the sūra will be set out in a table in the concluding section.

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the verse describes a disposition by **two separate sayings in the face of two opposite outcomes**. **The description is tied not to a person but to the changing of the words.**

---

## 4/142-147 — إن المنافقين يخادعون الله وهو خادعهم وإذا قاموا إلى الصلاة قاموا كسالى … · مذبذبين بين ذلك لا إلى هؤلاء ولا إلى هؤلاء · … إن المنافقين في الدرك الأسفل من النار … · إلا الذين تابوا وأصلحوا واعتصموا بالله وأخلصوا دينهم لله فأولئك مع المؤمنين · ما يفعل الله بعذابكم إن شكرتم وآمنتم

*Inna'l-munāfiqīna yukhādiʿūna'llāha wa huwa khādiʿuhum wa idhā qāmū ila'ṣ-ṣalāti qāmū kusālā … · Mudhabdhabīna bayna dhālika lā ilā hāʾulāʾi wa lā ilā hāʾulāʾ · … Inna'l-munāfiqīna fi'l-darki'l-asfali mina'l-nār … · Illa'lladhīna tābū wa aṣlaḥū wa'ʿtaṣamū bi'llāhi wa akhlaṣū dīnahum li'llāhi fa-ulāʾika maʿa'l-muʾminīn · Mā yafʿalu'llāhu bi-ʿadhābikum in shakartum wa āmantum*

"They seek to deceive God, but it is He who turns their deceit back upon them. When they rise to prayer they rise sluggishly… Wavering between the two, neither to these nor to those… The hypocrites are in the lowest depth of the Fire… Except those who repent, set things right, hold fast to God and make their religion purely for God: these are with the believers… Why should God punish you, if you are grateful and believe?"

### مُذَبْذَبِينَ — the picture in the word

**ذ-ب-ذ-ب — a four-letter root with reduplication.** The lexicographers explain the word as **a thing swinging to both sides, hanging and being tossed about**.

**And the pattern of the word must be recorded: *mudhabdhab* is a passive participle — that is, not one who swings but one who is swung.**

**And the continuation of the sentence completes the picture: *lā ilā hāʾulāʾi wa lā ilā hāʾulāʾ* — "neither to these nor to those".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the pattern of the word together with the structure of the sentence:** the verse describes standing between two sides not as a position but as a **state of irresolution**.

**And this picture has a counterpart within the sūra: *ka'l-muʿallaqa* (left suspended) in the hundred and twenty-ninth verse.**

| Verse | Word | Who is left hanging |
|---|---|---|
| **129** | *Ka'l-muʿallaqa* | **The wife** — the party left hanging |
| **143** | *Mudhabdhabīn* | **The person himself** — between two directions |

**Both words build the picture of "being caught in between".** I record this as an observation about word order; **I set up no link of ruling between the two verses.**

### ٱلدَّرْكِ ٱلْأَسْفَلِ — the analysis of the word

**The root د-ر-ك: to overtake, to reach — and from there *darak*, a step descending downwards.**

**And it must be recorded that this is the root's third occurrence in the sūra:** at the seventy-eighth verse there was *yudrikkumu'l-mawt*, at the hundredth *yudrikhu'l-mawt*. **Here the same root returns as a noun.**

### إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ تَابُوا۟ وَأَصْلَحُوا۟ — the four items of the exception

**The hundred and forty-sixth verse describes the way out of the description with four verbs:**

| Order | Verb | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Tābū* | To turn back |
| 2 | ***Aṣlaḥū*** | **To set right** |
| 3 | *Iʿtaṣamū bi'llāh* | To hold fast |
| 4 | *Akhlaṣū dīnahum li'llāh* | To make purely for God |

**And the second item must be recorded: the root ص-ل-ح occurs in this sūra for the fifth time.** The root's distribution in the sūra was set out in a table in the discussion of the hundred and fourteenth verse; **this row is added to it.**

***Iʿtaṣamū* — root ع-ص-م: to hold a thing, to protect, to hold back.** *ʿIṣma* — being protected. **Form VIII: to hold fast.**

**And the form of the return must be recorded: *fa-ulāʾika maʿa'l-muʾminīn* — "these are with the believers".**

**This is the same structure as in the sixty-ninth verse** (*fa-ulāʾika **maʿa**'lladhīna anʿama'llāhu ʿalayhim*). **In both places the sūra gives the return as a *companionship*.**

### مَّا يَفْعَلُ ٱللَّهُ بِعَذَابِكُمْ — the form of the question

**The hundred and forty-seventh verse is a question, and it closes the block.**

**And the structure of the question must be recorded: after a heavy description comes a sentence asking why punishment should be needed.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the place of the sentence:** the block closes not with a threat but **with the possibility of the threat being lifted**. **And the condition has two items: *in shakartum wa āmantum*.**

**And the verse-ending must be recorded: *wa kāna'llāhu Shākiran ʿAlīmā* — the root ش-ك-ر is used in the same verse both of the servant and of God.** This is a countable fact.

---

## 4/148-152 — لا يحب الله الجهر بالسوء من القول إلا من ظلم … · إن تبدوا خيرا أو تخفوه أو تعفوا عن سوء فإن الله كان عفوا قديرا · … الذين يفرقون بين الله ورسله … · والذين آمنوا بالله ورسله ولم يفرقوا بين أحد منهم أولئك سوف يؤتيهم أجورهم

*Lā yuḥibbu'llāhu'l-jahra bi's-sūʾi mina'l-qawli illā man ẓulim … · In tubdū khayran aw tukhfūhu aw taʿfū ʿan sūʾin fa-inna'llāha kāna ʿAfuwwan Qadīrā · … Alladhīna yufarriqūna bayna'llāhi wa rusulihī … · Wa'lladhīna āmanū bi'llāhi wa rusulihī wa lam yufarriqū bayna aḥadin minhum ulāʾika sawfa yuʾtīhim ujūrahum*

"God does not like the open utterance of evil speech — except by one who has been wronged… Whether you make a good deed known, or hide it, or pardon an evil — God is pardoning, powerful… Those who make a separation between God and His messengers… And those who believe in God and His messengers and make no distinction between any of them — to these He will give their rewards."

### إِلَّا مَن ظُلِمَ — the place of the exception

**The verse lays down a prohibition and immediately opens an exception: *illā man ẓulim* — "except one who has been wronged".**

**And there is disagreement in the classical commentaries over what the exception covers:**

| Reading | What the exception covers | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | That the one wronged should **state his situation** | The sense of the word *jahr* as "to say openly" |
| **B** | That he should **pray against** the one who wronged him | That the sentence concerns *sūʾ* (evil speech) |
| **C** | That he should **make the wrong done to him known** to another, that he should complain | The general use of the word |

**All three readings are transmitted; I impose no preference and draw no legal ruling from this.**

**What can be said from the text is this, and it is an observation about word order:** while forbidding the open utterance of evil speech, the verse **leaves a door open for the one who has been harmed.** The exception is in the same sentence as the prohibition.

### إِن تُبْدُوا۟ خَيْرًا أَوْ تُخْفُوهُ أَوْ تَعْفُوا۟ عَن سُوٓءٍ — three possibilities

**The verse counts three things, and the return for all three is given with the same verse-ending: *inna'llāha kāna ʿAfuwwan Qadīrā*.**

| Order | Verb | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Tubdū khayrā* | To **make known** a good deed |
| 2 | *Aw tukhfūh* | To **hide** a good deed |
| 3 | *Aw taʿfū ʿan sūʾ* | To **pardon an evil** |

**And the choice of the verse-ending must be recorded: the verse sets the attributes *ʿAfuww* (much-pardoning) and *Qadīr* (able) side by side.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the coming together of the two attributes:** pardoning means something when it is done **while one has the power**, not because one lacks it. **The verse-ending is a measure set beside the third item.**

**And this verse is the last row of the trio set out in a table in the discussion of the twenty-fifth verse** (*wa an taṣbirū khayrun lakum* — *wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr* — *in tubdū khayran aw tukhfūhu aw taʿfū ʿan sūʾin*). **I rely on that.**

### يُفَرِّقُونَ بَيْنَ ٱللَّهِ وَرُسُلِهِۦ — and the sentence set over against it

**The root ف-ر-ق: to separate, to divide.**

**And the verses give a disposition and its exact opposite one after the other:**

| Verse | Wording | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **150** | *Yufarriqūna bayna'llāhi wa rusulih* … *wa yurīdūna an yattakhidhū bayna dhālika sabīlā* | Those who separate |
| **152** | *Wa **lam yufarriqū** bayna aḥadin minhum* | **Those who do not separate** |

**The same verb, once affirmative and once negative, two verses apart.** This is a countable fact.

**And the hundred and fifty-second verse coincides with the hundred and thirty-sixth of the same sūra:** there too the things to be believed in were counted out one by one (God, the messenger, the Book, the earlier Book). **The block opens with an enumeration and closes with one.**

**And the same expression occurs at `002-bakara.md` 2/285** (*lā nufarriqu bayna aḥadin min rusulih*), **and there it is a sentence of prayer.** **The same construction in two sūras: one a statement, the other an utterance.**

---

## 4/153-159 — يسألك أهل الكتاب أن تنزل عليهم كتابا من السماء … · فبما نقضهم ميثاقهم وكفرهم بآيات الله وقتلهم الأنبياء بغير حق وقولهم قلوبنا غلف … · وبكفرهم وقولهم على مريم بهتانا عظيما · وقولهم إنا قتلنا المسيح عيسى ابن مريم رسول الله وما قتلوه وما صلبوه ولكن شبه لهم … وما قتلوه يقينا · بل رفعه الله إليه

*Yasʾaluka ahlu'l-kitābi an tunazzila ʿalayhim kitāban mina's-samāʾ … · Fa-bimā naqḍihim mīthāqahum wa kufrihim bi-āyāti'llāhi wa qatlihimu'l-anbiyāʾa bi-ghayri ḥaqqin wa qawlihim qulūbunā ghulf … · Wa bi-kufrihim wa qawlihim ʿalā Maryama buhtānan ʿaẓīmā · Wa qawlihim innā qatalna'l-Masīḥa ʿĪsa'bna Maryama rasūla'llāh, wa mā qatalūhu wa mā ṣalabūhu wa lākin shubbiha lahum … wa mā qatalūhu yaqīnā · Bal rafaʿahu'llāhu ilayh*

"The People of the Book ask you to bring down upon them a book from heaven… For their breaking of their pledge, their denial of God's signs, their killing of the prophets without right, and their saying 'our hearts are wrapped up'… And for their denial and their speaking a great slander against Mary (Maryam)… And for their saying, 'we killed the Messiah, Jesus (ʿĪsā) son of Mary, the messenger of God'. But they did not kill him and did not crucify him; it was made to appear so to them… They did not kill him with certainty. Rather, God raised him to Himself."

### First, a note on method — that the rulings are tied to acts

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) this block is read thus: what is at issue are acts, not a community itself. And this is not an interpretation but the verse's own word order.**

**The hundred and fifty-fifth verse is a *list of causes*, and every item comes with the particle *bi-*. The table gives the wording of the verse:**

| Order | Wording | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Bimā **naqḍihim** mīthāqahum* | Breaking a pledge |
| **2** | *Wa **kufrihim** bi-āyāti'llāh* | Denying the signs |
| **3** | *Wa **qatlihimu**'l-anbiyāʾa bi-ghayri ḥaqq* | Killing the prophets |
| **4** | *Wa **qawlihim** qulūbunā ghulf* | Saying a thing |
| **5** | *Wa **qawlihim** ʿalā Maryama buhtānan ʿaẓīmā* (156) | Uttering a slander |
| **6** | *Wa **qawlihim** innā qatalna'l-Masīḥ* (157) | Uttering a claim |

**All six of the six items are tied to an act or to an utterance.** This is a countable fact.

**And the verses themselves narrow in two places:**

| Wording | What it does |
|---|---|
| *Fa-lā yuʾminūna **illā qalīlā*** (155) | An exception |
| *Lākini'l-rāsikhūna fi'l-ʿilmi **minhum*** (162) | A section is set apart **from within the same community** |

**No general ruling is made in this commentary about any religious, ethnic or national group. What is at issue are attributes; whoever bears the attribute is included in it.** **And the two rows above are the textual ground of that rule.**

### قُلُوبُنَا غُلْفٌ — the analysis of the word

**The root غ-ل-ف: to put a thing into a covering.** *Ghilāf* — a sheath, a covering.

**The word occurs at `002-bakara.md` 2/88 as well** (*wa qālū qulūbunā ghulf*), **and there too the same utterance is related.** **Two sūras relate the same utterance.**

**And the verse's answer must be recorded: *bal ṭabaʿa'llāhu ʿalayhā bi-kufrihim* — "no, God has set a seal upon them for their denial".**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** in the sentence *bi-kufrihim* stands as a **ground**. **The seal, that is, is named not as something that comes first but as following upon an act.**

**And the same pattern was treated at `063-munafikun.md` 63/3** (*āmanū thumma kafarū fa-ṭubiʿa ʿalā qulūbihim*), **where the importance of the sequence was recorded under the heading "the seal". I rely on that treatment.**

### بُهْتَٰنًا عَظِيمًا — for the third time in the sūra

**The word occurs in this sūra for the third time.**

| Verse | Construction | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **20** | *Buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā* | The taking back of a bridal gift |
| **112** | *Buhtānan wa ithman mubīnā* | Slandering an innocent person |
| **156** | ***Buhtānan ʿaẓīmā*** | What was said about Mary |

**Three occurrences: a countable fact.** And the analysis of the word was given in the discussion of the hundred and twelfth verse (resting on `024-nur.md` 24/11-20). **I rely on that.**

**And one link must be recorded: Sūrat `024-nur.md` is likewise a discussion of slander, and there too what was said about a woman is the subject.** **Al-Nisāʾ 4/156 names the same act in another context.**

### وَمَا قَتَلُوهُ وَمَا صَلَبُوهُ وَلَٰكِن شُبِّهَ لَهُمْ — a table of the disagreement

**First a note on method: no speculation is entered into about this verse in this section, and no preference is imposed.**

**And the reason is the verse's own sentence. The verse does not recount how the event happened; it says that there is no knowledge about the event:**

| Wording | What it says |
|---|---|
| *Wa inna'lladhīna'khtalafū fīhi **la-fī shakkin minh*** | Those who differ about it are **in doubt** |
| *Mā lahum bihī **min ʿilmin*** | **They have no knowledge** |
| *Illa'ttibāʿa'l-**ẓann*** | They follow only **conjecture** |
| *Wa mā qatalūhu **yaqīnā*** | They did not kill him with certainty |

**All four of the four items concern *knowledge*.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the verse's own stress is not to supply an alternative account but to state that **the claim does not rest on knowledge**. **To go into detail would run against the verse's own qualification.**

**Nevertheless there is disagreement in the classical commentaries over the expression *shubbiha lahum*, and it must not be concealed. I put it in a table; I impose no preference, I give no details and no names, and none of it is binding.**

| Reading | What *shubbiha lahum* states | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **It was made to appear so to them**; what they saw was not what they supposed | That the verb is passive (*shubbiha*) and that its object is *lahum* (to them) |
| **B** | **The matter was confused for them**; no certainty arose about the event itself | The qualifications *la-fī shakkin minh* and *mā lahum bihī min ʿilm* in the continuation of the verse |
| **C** | The verse merely **rejects the claim**, giving no detail; the manner is not explained | That the verse gives no detail; that the word *yaqīnā* belongs to the field of knowledge |

**All three readings are transmitted. I prefer none of them, and nothing definite is said in this commentary about the manner.**

**بَل رَّفَعَهُ ٱللَّهُ إِلَيْهِ** — **the root ر-ف-ع: to raise.** **There is disagreement in the classical literature over the manner of this expression as well; its details are not entered into in this commentary and no preference is imposed.**

### وَإِن مِّنْ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ إِلَّا لَيُؤْمِنَنَّ بِهِۦ قَبْلَ مَوْتِهِۦ — a disagreement over pronouns

**In the hundred and fifty-ninth verse there are two pronouns, and disagreement is transmitted in the classical commentaries over what each of them refers to.**

| Reading | To whom *bihī* refers | Whose death *mawtihī* is |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | To Jesus | **Each individual** of the People of the Book — his own death |
| **B** | To Jesus | The death of Jesus |
| **C** | To the Prophet or to the Book | The person's own death |

**The readings are transmitted; I impose no preference and draw no conclusion from them.**

---

## 4/160-162 — فبظلم من الذين هادوا حرمنا عليهم طيبات أحلت لهم وبصدهم عن سبيل الله كثيرا · وأخذهم الربا وقد نهوا عنه وأكلهم أموال الناس بالباطل … · لكن الراسخون في العلم منهم والمؤمنون يؤمنون بما أنزل إليك وما أنزل من قبلك والمقيمين الصلاة والمؤتون الزكاة والمؤمنون بالله واليوم الآخر أولئك سنؤتيهم أجرا عظيما

*Fa-bi-ẓulmin mina'lladhīna hādū ḥarramnā ʿalayhim ṭayyibātin uḥillat lahum wa bi-ṣaddihim ʿan sabīli'llāhi kathīrā · Wa akhdhihimu'l-ribā wa qad nuhū ʿanhu wa aklihim amwāla'l-nāsi bi'l-bāṭil … · Lākini'l-rāsikhūna fi'l-ʿilmi minhum wa'l-muʾminūna yuʾminūna bimā unzila ilayka wa mā unzila min qablik, wa'l-muqīmīna'ṣ-ṣalāta wa'l-muʾtūna'l-zakāta wa'l-muʾminūna bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhir, ulāʾika sa-nuʾtīhim ajran ʿaẓīmā*

"For the wrongdoing of those who became Jews, and for their barring many from the way of God, We forbade them good things that had been lawful for them. And for their taking usury, though they had been forbidden it, and for their consuming people's property by false means… But those among them who are deeply grounded in knowledge, and the believers, believe in what has been sent down to you and what was sent down before you. Those who keep up the prayer, who give the alms, who believe in God and the Last Day — to these We shall give a great reward."

### The causes tied again to acts

**The hundred and sixtieth and hundred and sixty-first verses also build a list of causes, and each item comes again with the particle *bi-*:**

| Order | Wording | Act |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Bi-**ẓulmin*** | Wrongdoing |
| 2 | *Wa bi-**ṣaddihim** ʿan sabīli'llāhi kathīrā* | Barring |
| 3 | *Wa **akhdhihimu**'l-ribā wa qad nuhū ʿanh* | Taking what had been forbidden |
| 4 | *Wa **aklihim** amwāla'l-nāsi bi'l-bāṭil* | **Consuming property by false means** |

**And the fourth item must be recorded: the construction is in exactly the same field as the sūra's twenty-ninth verse.**

| Verse | Wording | To whom it is said |
|---|---|---|
| **29** | *Lā taʾkulū **amwālakum baynakum bi'l-bāṭil*** | **To those addressed — a prohibition** |
| **161** | *Wa **aklihim amwāla'l-nāsi bi'l-bāṭil*** | Of the group described — a cause |

**The same verb and the same qualification *bi'l-bāṭil*, at the two ends of the sūra.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording the two verses share:** the act the sūra counts as a cause in the case of one group **it had also forbidden to those it addresses.** **The measure, that is, is the same for both sides — and this is a textual ground for the method's rule against sweeping rulings.**

### لَٰكِنِ ٱلرَّٰسِخُونَ فِى ٱلْعِلْمِ مِنْهُمْ — the place where the distinction is made

***Rāsikh* — root ر-س-خ: to be firmly settled, to take root.** **The picture in the word is a thing settled so firmly that it will not shift from its place.**

**And the qualification *minhum* must be recorded separately: "of them".** **The distinction, that is, is made from *within* the community.**

**I bring this forward separately, because here the method's rule about attributes is being established by the text itself.**

### وَٱلْمُقِيمِينَ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ — a point of case-inflection

**There is a grammatical detail in the verse, and it is dwelt on in the classical literature.**

**The items listed in the sentence are in the nominative** (*al-rāsikhūn*, *al-muʾminūn*, *al-muʾtūn*, *al-muʾminūn*), **but one item comes in the accusative: *al-muqīmīna'ṣ-ṣalāta*.**

**Different tendencies are transmitted in the classical literature of grammar on the explanation of this difference:**

| Explanation | How |
|---|---|
| **A** | It is the style of **specification (*ikhtiṣāṣ*) / praise (*madḥ*)**: an item is drawn out of the flow of the sentence and put in the accusative for praise. It is a known usage in Arabic |
| **B** | The word is coordinated with an earlier item and takes its case from there |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I impose no preference.**

**And what must be recorded as a datum of the text is this:** of the five items counted in the sentence only one carries this difference, and that item is **prayer.** This is a countable fact.

---

## 4/163-166 — إنا أوحينا إليك كما أوحينا إلى نوح والنبيين من بعده … · ورسلا قد قصصناهم عليك من قبل ورسلا لم نقصصهم عليك وكلم الله موسى تكليما · رسلا مبشرين ومنذرين لئلا يكون للناس على الله حجة بعد الرسل

*Innā awḥaynā ilayka kamā awḥaynā ilā Nūḥin wa'l-nabiyyīna min baʿdih … · Wa rusulan qad qaṣaṣnāhum ʿalayka min qablu wa rusulan lam naqṣuṣhum ʿalayk, wa kallama'llāhu Mūsā taklīmā · Rusulan mubashshirīna wa mundhirīna li-allā yakūna li'l-nāsi ʿala'llāhi ḥujjatun baʿda'l-rusul*

"We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah (Nūḥ) and to the prophets after him… We sent messengers whose story We have recounted to you before, and messengers whose story We have not recounted to you. And God spoke to Moses directly. Messengers bearing good news and warning, so that after the messengers people should have no argument against God."

### وَرُسُلًا لَّمْ نَقْصُصْهُمْ عَلَيْكَ — a qualification left open

**The verse counts separately the messengers whose story is recounted and those whose story is not.**

**And it must be recorded that this produces a note on method: the text says plainly that what it recounts is not the whole.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the sentence itself:** the wording of the verse also closes off talking about those not recounted — **to give details about something announced as not recounted would be to add to the text.**

### لِئَلَّا يَكُونَ لِلنَّاسِ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ حُجَّةٌۢ — the form of the ground

***Ḥujja* — root ح-ج-ج: to head in a direction, to make for; and from there, a proof.** **The root was treated at `006-enam.md` 6/80, `045-casiye.md` 45/25 and `040-gafir.md` 40/47. I rely on those treatments.**

**And the direction of the ground must be recorded: messengers are sent so that people should be left with no argument.**

**I record this as an observation about word order:** the sentence ties the reason for the sending to the **position of those addressed**. **And the qualification *baʿda'l-rusul* says that the argument can fall away only *after* a messenger.**

---

## 4/167-170 — إن الذين كفروا وصدوا عن سبيل الله قد ضلوا ضلالا بعيدا … · يا أيها الناس قد جاءكم الرسول بالحق من ربكم فآمنوا خيرا لكم وإن تكفروا فإن لله ما في السماوات والأرض

*Inna'lladhīna kafarū wa ṣaddū ʿan sabīli'llāhi qad ḍallū ḍalālan baʿīdā … · Yā ayyuha'l-nāsu qad jāʾakumu'l-rasūlu bi'l-ḥaqqi min rabbikum fa-āminū khayran lakum, wa in takfurū fa-inna li'llāhi mā fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ*

"Those who deny and bar others from the way of God have strayed far into error… O mankind! The Messenger has brought you the truth from your Lord. Believe; it is better for you. And if you deny — to God belongs whatever is in the heavens and the earth."

### يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ — the return of the address

**This address returns here for the first time since the first verse of the sūra, and it will come once more in the hundred and seventy-fourth.**

| Verse | Address | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yā ayyuha'l-nās* | Creation from a single soul, mindfulness of God, kinship |
| **170** | *Yā ayyuha'l-nās* | The Messenger's bringing the truth |
| **174** | *Yā ayyuha'l-nās* | *Burhān* and *nūr mubīn* |

**Three occurrences: a countable fact.** And the sūra's widest address stands **at its beginning and at its end**.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the distribution of the address:** the sūra has thus placed all the rulings in between two wide addresses.

### فَـَٔامِنُوا۟ خَيْرًا لَّكُمْ — word order

**Different explanations of the case of the construction *khayran lakum* are transmitted in the literature of grammar;** it is widely said to be attached to an elided verb. **I do not go into the details.**

**What belongs here is this: the return is set beside the command.** And this is a pattern repeated in the sūra: in the twenty-fifth verse *wa an taṣbirū **khayrun** lakum*, in the hundred and twenty-eighth *wa'l-**ṣulḥu khayr***, in the forty-sixth *la-kāna **khayran** lahum wa aqwam*.

**In four places the same word stands beside a command or an alternative.** This is a weave that can be verified within the sūra.

---

## 4/171-173 — يا أهل الكتاب لا تغلوا في دينكم ولا تقولوا على الله إلا الحق إنما المسيح عيسى ابن مريم رسول الله وكلمته ألقاها إلى مريم وروح منه … ولا تقولوا ثلاثة انتهوا خيرا لكم … · لن يستنكف المسيح أن يكون عبدا لله ولا الملائكة المقربون · فأما الذين آمنوا وعملوا الصالحات فيوفيهم أجورهم ويزيدهم من فضله

*Yā ahla'l-kitābi lā taghlū fī dīnikum wa lā taqūlū ʿala'llāhi illa'l-ḥaqq, innama'l-Masīḥu ʿĪsa'bnu Maryama rasūlu'llāhi wa kalimatuhū alqāhā ilā Maryama wa rūḥun minhu … wa lā taqūlū thalātha, intahū khayran lakum … · Lan yastankifa'l-Masīḥu an yakūna ʿabdan li'llāhi wa la'l-malāʾikatu'l-muqarrabūn · Fa-amma'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti fa-yuwaffīhim ujūrahum wa yazīduhum min faḍlih*

"O People of the Book! Do not go beyond the bound in your religion, and say nothing about God but the truth. The Messiah, Jesus son of Mary, is only the messenger of God, and His word which He conveyed to Mary, and a spirit from Him… Do not say 'three'; stop, it is better for you… The Messiah does not disdain to be a servant of God, nor do the angels brought near. As for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will pay them their rewards in full and give them more of His bounty."

### First, a note on method

**No ruling about a community is established in this block. What is at issue is a claim, and the verse itself frames it so:**

| Wording | What it does |
|---|---|
| *Lā **taqūlū** ʿala'llāhi illa'l-ḥaqq* | A **saying** is forbidden |
| *Wa lā **taqūlū** thalātha* | An **utterance** is made the subject |
| ***Intahū** khayran lakum* | A **call** — and its return set beside it |

**All three of the three items are tied to an utterance and to an act.** And the command *intahū* (stop) shows that the door is left open.

### غُلُوّ — the root غ-ل-و

**The concrete sense of the root must be recorded, because it carries the whole weight of the word: *ghalā* — for a thing to exceed its measure and its worth.**

**The family of senses that follows from this:**

| Word | Sense | The link |
|---|---|---|
| **غَلَا** (*ghalā*) | Its price rose, it became dear | The concrete sense |
| **غَلَاء** (*ghalāʾ*) | Dearness | — |
| **غَلَا بِٱلسَّهْم** | **To shoot an arrow beyond the target** | A usage the lexicographers record |
| **غُلُوّ** (*ghuluww*) | **To exceed the bound, to cross the limit** | — |
| **غَالِي** (*ghālī*) | One who goes to excess | — |

**And the example of the arrow is worth recording: the word states not a *failure to reach* the target but an overshooting of it.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concrete sense of the root:** *ghuluww* is the worth given to a thing exceeding that thing's own measure. **The word, that is, points *upward* in direction — not a diminishing but an over-raising.**

**And the same address occurs once more at `005-maide.md` 5/77:** *yā ahla'l-kitābi lā taghlū fī dīnikum ghayra'l-ḥaqq*. **The same command in two sūras, and almost the same wording.** This is a countable fact.

| Place | Wording | Added qualification |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/171** | *Lā taghlū fī dīnikum* | *Wa lā taqūlū ʿala'llāhi illa'l-ḥaqq* |
| `005-maide.md` **5/77** | *Lā taghlū fī dīnikum* | *Ghayra'l-ḥaqq* |

**In both verses the word *ḥaqq* is set beside the prohibition.** **The measure, that is, is given by the verse itself: the opposite of exceeding the bound is speaking the truth.**

### عَبْدًا لِّلَّهِ — and `019-meryem.md` 19/30 with 19/88-93

**And now comes the strongest datum of the text in this discussion.**

**The hundred and seventy-second verse is this: *lan yastankifa'l-Masīḥu an yakūna ʿabdan li'llāh*.**

***Yastankif* — root ن-ك-ف: to shrink from a thing, to hold back from it as beneath oneself.**

**And the same word was treated in two separate places in `019-meryem.md`. I carry those records over here.**

**The first, from the discussion of 19/30:**

> *"The first word of the sentence must be recorded: *innī ʿabdu'llāh* — 'I am the servant of God.' **I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the structure of the sūra:** in the eighty-eighth verse the sūra will take up the claim *wa qālu'ttakhadha'l-Raḥmānu waladā*. **And the first word out of the mouth of the person who is the subject of that claim is *ʿabd*.**"*

**The second, from the discussion of 19/88-95:**

> *"**In the thirtieth verse of the sūra Jesus's first words were *innī ʿabdu'llāh*.** **The ninety-third verse applies the same word to everyone without exception.**… **the answer to the claim is given in the words of the very person the claim is about, and is then generalised.**"*

**I rely on both records. And setting the three verses side by side now gives a weave between two sūras that can be verified:**

| Place | Wording | Who says it / whom it covers |
|---|---|---|
| `019-meryem.md` **19/30** | *Innī **ʿabdu'llāh*** | **Jesus — he says it himself** |
| `019-meryem.md` **19/93** | *In kullu man fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi illā āti'l-Raḥmāni **ʿabdā*** | **Everyone — without exception** |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/172** | *Lan yastankifa'l-Masīḥu an yakūna **ʿabdan li'llāhi** wa la'l-malāʾikatu'l-muqarrabūn* | **The Messiah and the angels brought near** |

**In all three verses the same word: *ʿabd*.** This is a countable fact.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** Sūrat Maryam gives the answer **in the person's own words** and then generalises it; al-Nisāʾ gives the same answer as **the rejection of a shrinking back**, and sets the angels beside him. **The three verses look in the same direction, and in all three the word does not change.**

### كَلِمَتُهُۥٓ … وَرُوحٌ مِّنْهُ — a record

**The verse uses two expressions: *kalimatuhū alqāhā ilā Maryam* and *rūḥun minh*.**

**Explanations of the manner of these two expressions are transmitted in the classical commentaries; their details are not entered into in this commentary and no preference is imposed.**

**What can be said from the text is this: the preposition *min* here states in Arabic not a *part* but a source** — and this is widely recorded in the classical commentaries. **I relate this record as it is transmitted.**

**And the verse's own closing lays down a measure: *subḥānahū an yakūna lahū walad*.**

---

## 4/174-175 — يا أيها الناس قد جاءكم برهان من ربكم وأنزلنا إليكم نورا مبينا · فأما الذين آمنوا بالله واعتصموا به فسيدخلهم في رحمة منه وفضل ويهديهم إليه صراطا مستقيما

*Yā ayyuha'l-nāsu qad jāʾakum burhānun min rabbikum wa anzalnā ilaykum nūran mubīnā · Fa-amma'lladhīna āmanū bi'llāhi wa'ʿtaṣamū bihī fa-sa-yudkhiluhum fī raḥmatin minhu wa faḍlin wa yahdīhim ilayhi ṣirāṭan mustaqīmā*

"O mankind! A *burhān* has come to you from your Lord, and We have sent down to you a clear light. As for those who believe in God and hold fast to Him, He will admit them into a mercy from Himself and a bounty, and will guide them to Himself by a straight path."

### بُرْهَٰن — the analysis of the word

**ب-ر-ه-ن — it is counted a four-letter root; the lexicographers also debate its relation to *ب-ر-ه* and do not agree on any settled derivation. Nothing definite is said in this commentary about the origin of the word.**

**On its meaning, however, they agree: a decisive and binding proof.**

**The word occurs at `028-kasas.md` 28/75 as well** (*hātū burhānakum*), **and there it is used as a demand.**

| Place | Wording | Who to whom |
|---|---|---|
| `028-kasas.md` **28/75** | *Hātū **burhānakum*** | **It is demanded** |
| **al-Nisāʾ 4/174** | *Qad jāʾakum **burhānun** min rabbikum* | **It is given** |

**The same word, once a demand and once a response.** I record this as an observation about word order.

### وَٱعْتَصَمُوا۟ بِهِۦ — for the second time in the sūra

**The root ع-ص-م had occurred in this sūra at the hundred and forty-sixth verse too** (*wa'ʿtaṣamū bi'llāh*) **and was treated there. I rely on that treatment.**

| Verse | Who | Context |
|---|---|---|
| **146** | Those who come out of the description | **An exception** |
| **175** | Those who believe | **The closing** |

**The same verb, in two separate places in the sūra, and in both a *holding fast*.**

---

## 4/176 — يستفتونك قل الله يفتيكم في الكلالة إن امرؤ هلك ليس له ولد وله أخت فلها نصف ما ترك وهو يرثها إن لم يكن لها ولد فإن كانتا اثنتين فلهما الثلثان مما ترك وإن كانوا إخوة رجالا ونساء فللذكر مثل حظ الأنثيين يبين الله لكم أن تضلوا والله بكل شيء عليم

*Yastaftūnak, quli'llāhu yuftīkum fi'l-kalālah, ini'mruʾun halaka laysa lahū waladun wa lahū ukhtun fa-lahā niṣfu mā tarak, wa huwa yarithuhā in lam yakun lahā walad, fa-in kānata'thnatayni fa-lahuma'l-thuluthāni mimmā tarak, wa in kānū ikhwatan rijālan wa nisāʾan fa-li'l-dhakari mithlu ḥaẓẓi'l-unthayayn, yubayyinu'llāhu lakum an taḍillū, wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin ʿAlīm*

"They ask you for a ruling. Say: God gives you a ruling concerning *kalāla*. If a man dies leaving no child but having a sister, hers is half of what he leaves; and he inherits from her if she has no child. If there are two sisters, theirs is two-thirds of what he leaves. If they are brothers and sisters, men and women, then to the male the like of the share of two females. God makes it clear to you, lest you go astray. God knows all things."

### First, a note on method — and this note is not binding

**From this verse arose one of the most technical discussions in classical jurisprudence. That discussion is not entered into in this section and no legal ruling is issued.**

**This record was entered in the discussion of the sūra's eleventh verse as well** ("First, a note on method — and this note holds for the whole of the eleventh, twelfth and hundred and seventy-sixth verses") **and I rely on that. A summary of what was written there:**

- **The shares are put in a table only from the verse's own wording.**
- **The debates over reckoning — what is to be done when the shares exceed or fall short of the whole — are not entered into.**
- **The differences among the schools of law are not the subject of this text.**
- **The table below is not a fatwā and cannot be read as a guide to practice. It is not binding.**

### The shares that follow from the verse's wording — a table

**The table gives the verse's own clauses in order. Nothing outside the verse's wording has been added to the table.**

| Case (the verse's wording) | The share the verse gives |
|---|---|
| *Ini'mruʾun halaka laysa lahū waladun wa lahū ukhtun* | *Fa-lahā **niṣfu** mā tarak* — half of what he leaves |
| *Wa huwa yarithuhā in lam yakun lahā walad* | The brother inherits from her — no amount stated |
| *Fa-in kānata'thnatayni* | *Fa-lahuma'**l-thuluthāni** mimmā tarak* — two-thirds |
| *Wa in kānū ikhwatan rijālan wa nisāʾan* | *Fa-li'l-dhakari **mithlu ḥaẓẓi'l-unthayayn*** |

**And one thing visible in the table must be recorded: the verse counts four cases and gives an explicit fraction in three of them, and not in one.** This is a countable fact.

### كَلَٰلَة — the word's second occurrence

**The word occurred at the twelfth verse of this sūra as well, and was treated there in detail under the heading "كَلَٰلَة — the analysis of the word". I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it.**

**What belongs here is this: the word occurs twice in the sūra, and both times in the context of inheritance.**

| Verse | Wording | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **12** | *Wa in kāna rajulun yūrathu **kalālatan** awi'mraʾatun* | A brother or a sister — a sixth |
| **176** | *Yuftīkum fi'l-**kalāla*** | A sister (or sisters) and siblings |

**The difference between the two verses must be recorded, and it is a datum of the text:** in the twelfth verse a share is given for a single sibling; in the hundred and seventy-sixth several cases are counted. **How this difference is to be reconciled is a matter of classical jurisprudence and is not the subject of this text.**

### حَظّ — the word's second occurrence

**The root ح-ظ-ظ was treated at the eleventh verse. I rely on that treatment.**

**And the construction *mithlu ḥaẓẓi'l-unthayayn* occurs for the second time in the sūra: at the eleventh and hundred and seventy-sixth verses.** This is a countable fact.

**The records entered in the discussion of the eleventh verse hold here too, and I do not repeat them.**

### يُبَيِّنُ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ أَن تَضِلُّوا۟ — the closing sentence

**The last sentence of the sūra is a sentence of ground, and its word order must be recorded.**

**Two explanations of the case of the construction *an taḍillū* are transmitted in the literature of grammar:**

| Explanation | How |
|---|---|
| **A** | A negation is understood: *an lā taḍillū* — "so that you may not go astray" |
| **B** | *Karāhata an taḍillū* is understood — "because your going astray is not wanted" |

**Both explanations are transmitted and both arrive at the same place. I impose no preference.**
