**And the choice of the verb must be recorded: *yubayyinu* — from the root ب-ي-ن, "to make clear, to separate one thing from another".**

**And this coincides with the sūra's twenty-sixth verse:** *yurīdu'llāhu **li-yubayyina** lakum*. **The same verb occurs in the middle of the sūra as a statement of will and at its end as a ground.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb the two verses share:** the sūra says twice why its rulings are laid down — and on both occasions the answer is **to make clear**.

### On the sūra's last sentence

**The sūra closes with a *fatwā*: *yastaftūnak*.**

**And the observation recorded in the introductory section is completed here:**

> *"The most striking side of the structure is that the sūra's closing section opens with a *fatwā* and closes with a *fatwā*. The hundred and twenty-seventh verse begins *wa yastaftūnaka fi'l-nisāʾ* and names **the orphan girls**; the hundred and seventy-sixth begins *yastaftūnaka* and gives the ruling on **kalāla**."*

**I rely on that.**

**And the second observation is completed here too:**

> *"The sūra opens with the word *al-arḥām* (the ties of kinship) (1) and closes with the discussion of *kalāla* — the estate of a person who leaves neither child nor father (176)."*

**The sūra's last ruling ties the share of the person who leaves behind neither child nor father to his siblings.** **The tie, that is, is not cut even at its thinnest point; it is carried one ring further out.**

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the wording of the verse.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

**This section looks back over a hundred and seventy-six verses and gathers what can be counted in the sūra. All the tables below are data of the text: the words and their places can be counted. Sentences that are interpretation have been marked as such.**

### One. Repetitions within the sūra — words and constructions

**The table below gathers the meaningful repetitions that occur in two or more places in the sūra.**

| Word / construction | The verses where it occurs | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **نَصِيب** (*naṣīb*) | 7 (twice), 32 (twice), 33, 85, 141 | A share; the sūra's most frequently returning word for a right |
| **نَصِيبًا مَّفْرُوضًا** | 7, 118 | The same construction: once a **right**, once a **claim to seize** |
| **بُهْتَان** | 20, 112, 156 | The slander that freezes; in 20 and 112 the construction is word for word identical |
| **ص-ل-ح** (*iṣlāḥ / ṣulḥ*) | 16, 35, 114, 128, 146 | In all five places a **setting right of a breach** |
| **ق-و-م** | 5, 34, 46, 103, 127, 135 | *Qiyām*, *qawwām*, *aqwam*, *qiyāman*, *taqūmū*, *qawwāmīn* |
| **ض-ع-ف** | 9, 28, 75, 98, 127 | Weakness as a state, as a nature and as a wrong done |
| **و-س-ع** | 97, 100, 130 | The wideness of the earth, of migration and of separation |
| **نُشُوز** | **34, 128** | **One the woman's, one the man's** |
| **ش-ق-ق** | 35, 115 | *Shiqāq* — between spouses; *yushāqiq* — breaking with the Messenger |
| **م-ي-ل** | 27, 129 | *Maylan ʿaẓīmā* / *kulla'l-mayl* |
| **ر-ض-و** (consent) | 4, 24, 29 | In all three, mutual consent |
| **م-ن-ي** (*tamannī / amāniyy*) | 32, 119, 120, 123 | Prohibition, prompting, rejection |
| **د-ر-ك** | 78, 100, 145 | Death overtaking (twice), *darak* |
| **يُبَيِّتُونَ** | 81, 108 | Words contrived by night |
| **يَكْتُمُونَ** | 37, 42 | Concealing / being unable to conceal |
| **إِلَّا مَا قَدْ سَلَفَ** | 22, 23 | The leaving of the past outside |
| **لَا يَغْفِرُ أَن يُشْرَكَ بِهِۦ** | 48, 116 | The very same sentence, two different parties addressed |
| The return given with **مَعَ** | 69, 146 | *Maʿa'lladhīna anʿama'llāh* / *maʿa'l-muʾminīn* |
| **وَلِيًّا … نَصِيرًا** | 45, 75 | One a statement, one a prayer |
| The threefold enumeration **ٱلْمُسْتَضْعَفِين** | 75, 98, 127 | *Al-rijāl*, *al-nisāʾ*, *al-wildān* |
| **خَيْر** (beside a command) | 25, 46, 128, 149, 170 | The "better" set beside a ruling |
| **ج-ن-ب** | 31, 36, 103 | Keeping away, nearness, the body |
| **أَمْوَال … بِٱلْبَٰطِلِ** | 29, 161 | The same verb: once a prohibition to those addressed, once in a list of causes |
| **يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ** | 1, 170, 174 | The sūra's widest address: at the beginning and at the end |
| **يَسْتَفْتُونَكَ** | 127, 176 | Two questions asking for a ruling |
| **كَلَٰلَة** | 12, 176 | Two discussions of inheritance |
| **مِثْلُ حَظِّ ٱلْأُنثَيَيْنِ** | 11, 176 | Word for word the same construction |
| **يُبَيِّنُ / لِيُبَيِّنَ** | 26, 176 | The ground of the rulings: to make clear |
| **The words for the smallest measure** | 40 (*dharra*), 77 (*fatīl*), 124 (*naqīr*) | All three for the absence of injustice |
| **أُو۟لِى ٱلْأَمْرِ** | 59, 83 | Obedience / where news is to be taken |
| **ٱعْتَصَمُوا۟** | 146, 175 | Two holdings fast |
| **ح-ك-م** (*ḥakam / ḥukm*) | 35, 58, 59, 60, 65, 105 | The sūra's weave of adjudication |

### Two. The places where the two parties are counted separately

**There are three places in the sūra where, in giving a right or a return, the two parties are named separately, and all three build the same structure.**

| Verse | Subject | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | **Inheritance** | *Li'l-rijāli **naṣībun** mimmā tarak … wa li'l-nisāʾi **naṣībun** mimmā tarak* |
| **32** | **Earnings** | *Li'l-rijāli naṣībun mimma'**ktasabū** wa li'l-nisāʾi naṣībun mimma'**ktasabna*** |
| **124** | **The return for one's deeds** | *Wa man yaʿmal mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti **min dhakarin aw unthā*** |

**And the arrangement of arbitrators in the thirty-fifth verse carries the same structure:** *ḥakaman **min ahlihī** wa ḥakaman **min ahlihā***.

**This is a countable fact.**

### Three. The places where the sūra leaves a door open

**At every heavy place where it lays down a ruling, the sūra leaves a way out or a qualification of relief. The table below gathers these and rests on the wording.**

| Verse | Ruling | What is set beside it |
|---|---|---|
| **16** | A penalty | *Fa-in tābā wa **aṣlaḥā** fa-aʿriḍū ʿanhumā* |
| **25** | A permission | *Wa an taṣbirū **khayrun** lakum* |
| **26-28** | A list of prohibitions | *Yurīdu'llāhu an **yukhaffifa** ʿankum* |
| **34-35** | Three steps | *Fa-lā tabghū ʿalayhinna sabīlā* → **arbitration** → ***iṣlāḥ*** |
| **43** | A prohibition | *Fa-**tayammamū*** and *inna'llāha kāna ʿAfuwwan Ghafūrā* |
| **90** | A ruling | *Fa-mā jaʿala'llāhu lakum ʿalayhim **sabīlā*** |
| **92** | An obligation | *Fa-man **lam yajid** fa-ṣiyāmu shahrayni mutatābiʿayn* |
| **98-99** | An interrogation | ***Illa'l-mustaḍʿafīn*** … *ʿasa'llāhu an yaʿfuwa ʿanhum* |
| **128** | A dispute | ***Wa'l-ṣulḥu khayr*** |
| **129** | An impossibility | *Fa-lā tamīlū **kulla'l-mayl*** — only the whole of it is forbidden |
| **146** | A description | ***Illa'lladhīna tābū wa aṣlaḥū*** |
| **147** | A threat | *Mā yafʿalu'llāhu bi-ʿadhābikum in shakartum wa āmantum* |
| **149** | A permission | *Aw **taʿfū** ʿan sūʾ* |
| **171** | A prohibition | ***Intahū** khayran lakum* |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** in fourteen separate places the sūra does the same thing — it lays down the ruling and leaves a door beside it. **This is the same line recorded in the section on divorce in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment.**

### Four. The places where the sūra blocks a sweeping generalisation

**In this sūra the method's rule that "no sweeping ruling is made about a group" is established by the text itself. The table below gathers the verses' own narrowings.**

| Verse | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **44, 51** | *Alladhīna ūtū **naṣīban** mina'l-kitāb* | "Those given a share" — an indefinite section |
| **46** | ***Mina**'lladhīna hādū* | *Min* — a particle stating a part |
| **46, 155** | *Fa-lā yuʾminūna **illā qalīlā*** | An exception |
| **81, 113** | ***Ṭāʾifatun minhum*** | "A group of them" |
| **90** | Three separate exceptions | Those who do not fight, who keep away, who offer peace |
| **123** | *Laysa **bi-amāniyyikum** wa lā amāniyyi ahli'l-kitāb* | **The same measure to both parties** |
| **131** | *Alladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba min qablikum **wa iyyākum*** | **The same charge to both parties** |
| **140** | *Ḥattā yakhūḍū fī ḥadīthin **ghayrih*** | The command is tied to **the subject**, not to a person |
| **146** | ***Illa'lladhīna tābū wa aṣlaḥū*** | A way out |
| **155-157, 160-161** | Every item tied by the particle *bi-* to an **act** | The ruling is tied to an act |
| **161** | *Wa aklihim amwāla'l-nāsi **bi'l-bāṭil*** | An act forbidden **to those addressed too** at 4/29 |
| **162** | *Lākini'l-rāsikhūna fi'l-ʿilmi **minhum*** | A distinction **from within the community** |

**I record this separately:** not one of the twelve rows above is a qualification added by this commentary; **all of them are the verses' own wording.**

### Seven. The line the sūra follows from beginning to end

**A reading was recorded in the introductory section, and it is tested here after a hundred and seventy-six verses. That reading was:** *"Sūrat al-Nisāʾ is built from beginning to end on **the right of the one who has no protection**."*

**The table below gives the counterparts of that reading in the second half of the sūra.**

| Who | Verse | What is regulated |
|---|---|---|
| **The woman** | 32, 34-35, 128-130, 176 | Earnings, dispute, settlement, inheritance |
| **The orphan** | 36, 127 | The list of kindness; a ruling |
| **The neighbour, the companion on the road, the traveller** | 36 | **Ties outside belonging** |
| **The one under one's hand** | 36, 92 | The end of the list; setting free |
| **The mustaḍʿafūn** | 75, 98, 127 | The ground for war; the acceptance of an excuse |
| **One who migrates and does not arrive** | 100 | That the reward does not lapse |
| **The innocent** (*barīʾ*) | 112 | The burden of slander |
| **The one testified against** | 135 | Oneself, parents, near kin |
| **Two parties who separate** | 130 | *Yughni'llāhu kullan min saʿatih* |
| **Kalāla** | 176 | The thinnest point of the tie |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the table above:** the sūra does not let go of the person whose right is in someone else's hands in its second half either. **And what all ten rows have in common is the same: the person who cannot take his own share for himself.**

**And the sūra's order of verse-endings carries this line, as was recorded in the introductory section.** Three instances from the second half:

| Verse | Command | Verse-ending |
|---|---|---|
| **58** | Deliver trusts to those entitled to them, judge with justice | *Inna'llāha kāna **Samīʿan Baṣīrā*** |
| **86** | Return a greeting with a finer one | *Inna'llāha kāna ʿalā kulli shayʾin **Ḥasībā*** |
| **135** | Uphold justice even against yourselves | *Fa-inna'llāha kāna bimā taʿmalūna **Khabīrā*** |

**In all three verse-endings there is an attribute of *hearing*, of *reckoning*, or of *knowing the inside*.** **This is the place where the reading established in the introductory section is confirmed at the sūra's close.**

## Where this section does not speak with certainty

**In keeping with this commentary's rule of accuracy, the places in this sūra where nothing definite is said are counted out plainly. What follows is not a deficiency but a record.**

### One. The places where no legal ruling is issued

**From this sūra arose the widest discussions of classical jurisprudence. On none of the following does this text issue a ruling, prefer a school of law, or propose a practice.**

| Verse | The juristic discussion |
|---|---|
| **3, 129** | The number of marriages and the legal character of the condition of justice |
| **11-12, 176** | The reckoning of inheritance; the sum of the shares; the exclusion of heirs |
| **15-16, 25** | The rulings on penalty and on marriage |
| **23-24** | The scope of the prohibitions of marriage |
| **34-35** | The application of the three steps; the binding force of the arrangement of arbitration |
| **43** | Ablution, the full washing, dry ablution; the scope of the expression *lāmastum* |
| **92-93** | Retaliation, blood-money, expiation, the kinds of intent |
| **101-104** | Travel, the shortening of prayer, the performance of the prayer of fear |
| **128-130** | The rulings on settlement, separation and maintenance |

**In all these discussions the disagreements have been transmitted and none has been preferred.**

### Two. Expressions whose scope is not filled in

| Verse | Expression | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *Kabāʾir* — the great sins | The verse gives neither a list nor a definition |
| **34** | *Baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍ* | The subject and the object of the sentence are indefinite pronouns |
| **119** | *Fa-la-yughayyirunna khalqa'llāh* | Its scope is not written in the verse; to fill in the content would be to add to the verse |
| **157** | *Shubbiha lahum* | **The verse itself says there is no knowledge** |
| **158** | *Bal rafaʿahu'llāhu ilayh* | Its manner is not explained |
| **171** | *Kalimatuhū* and *rūḥun minhu* | No preference is imposed on the manner |
| **163-164** | *Wa rusulan lam naqṣuṣhum ʿalayk* | **The text itself says what it has not recounted** |

### Three. The places where nothing definite is said about a word's origin

| Word | Verse | Record |
|---|---|---|
| *Al-nisāʾ* | The name of the sūra | The lexicographers agree on no settled derivation |
| *Jibt* | 51 | There is no agreement on its origin or its meaning |
| *Burhān* | 174 | Its derivation is disputed; its meaning is agreed |
| *Kalāla* | 12, 176 | The analysis of the word was given at 4/12; the disagreement was tabled there |

### Four. Dating and the narratives of the occasions of revelation

**As was recorded in the sūra's introductory section:** as to which event and which time each verse belongs to, **datings I cannot give with certainty have not been given.**

**And the narratives of the occasions have been related in the language of "it is transmitted", without naming persons.** This holds especially in the following places:

| Verse | What is transmitted in the sources |
|---|---|
| **77** | The difference of period between the command to hold back the hands and the command to fight |
| **88-91** | A dispute after a battle |
| **94** | An incident on a journey |
| **105-113** | **A judicial episode** — no name was given |
| **127** | A ruling given in answer to a question |

**In the discussion of the hundred and fifth to hundred and thirteenth verses the ground for this was written out separately:** the block takes as its subject the casting of an offence upon an innocent person; **to go into the details of the report would be to do the very thing the verse under treatment forbids.**

### Five. The debates not entered into

**One. Theological polemic.** The seventy-eighth and seventy-ninth verses and the ninety-third have been used in classical theological debates. **This commentary takes no side in those debates.**

**Two. Current politics.** Today's debates around the fifty-ninth verse (*ulu'l-amr*), the seventy-fifth (*the mustaḍʿafūn*) and the discussions of war (71-104) **are not the subject of this text.**

**Three. Current debates around the thirty-fourth verse.** How the verse is understood today, how it is answered in this or that legal order, and the social debates around it **are not the subject of this text.** The disagreement was given with its classical readings and their grounds; no preference was imposed.

**Four. General rulings about groups.** The verses from the forty-fourth to the fifty-seventh, from the hundred and thirty-sixth to the hundred and fifty-second, and from the hundred and fifty-third to the hundred and seventy-third touch on the history, the acts and a claim of a community. **What is at issue are attributes and claims; whoever bears the attribute is included in it.** And as was set out in a table under the heading "Four" above, **it is the verses themselves that make this narrowing.**

### Six. The places marked as this section's own reading

**In this commentary the sentences that are my own inference were marked "I record this as my own reading" or "I record this as an observation about word order", and the ground of each was given beside it.**

**None of them is a transmitted report and none of them is binding.** Their grounds are the wording of the verses, the roots of the words, and the repetitions that can be counted within the sūra. **What is countable and what is interpretation were kept apart throughout the text.**
